# Tasks and claims

## Claim protocol

1. Pick a task below whose status is `open`.
2. Edit its row: `status` -> `claimed`, add your agent name and the date.
3. Add your files to "Files" so others know what is read-only.
4. Work, verify per `TESTING.md`, write a handoff if you stop.
5. On completion: `status` -> `done`, add the commit hash, and move the row to
   the Completed section at the bottom (keep history).

Do not claim more than one task. If a task is too big, split it into subtasks
and claim one.

Status values: `open`, `claimed`, `blocked`, `review`, `done`, `parked`
(`parked` = superseded by ADR-0010; do not resume, kept for history).

## Workstreams

- **P-1xx** core plumbing (build, platform, assets)
- **P-2xx** rendering and animation
- **P-3xx** engine integration (use decompiled code)
- **P-4xx** tooling, testing, docs
- **P-5xx** future targets (audio, WASM, netplay)
- **P-6xx** decompiled-port bring-up (ADR-0010; milestones S0..S7)

| P-824 | Browser: stop issuing ~9,000 redundant GL calls a frame binding texture units | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-17 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.{c,h}`, `native/decomp/render/viewer_main.c` | `4421311b9` + follow-up. The draw loop bound **all eight units on every draw** (`glActiveTexture`+`glBindTexture` x8 plus a reset = 17 calls) when a typical draw samples one, and three 8-element uniform arrays were uploaded beside them outside the P-801 cache. Native does not notice; a browser pays ~0.5 us per call (G-198). **Binds issued per frame: 8/draw -> 2.6/draw.** **Correction to the commit's own framing:** it landed as two changes and the browser logs show only the *second* one matters -- skipping units the draw does not sample. The redundant-bind shadow skips **60 of 71,715** in a real browser match (0.08%), because consecutive draws almost never reuse a unit. The shadow is kept only for the `unit_active` tracking and the `binds=` counter, **not** because it saves binds; do not cite it as a win. `MELEE_GX_TEX_BINDS=0` restores the old path and captures are byte-identical on stages 2/8/9/24. |
| P-825 | Browser: JSPI replaces Asyncify for the synchronous disc read | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-17 | `native/tools/wasm_census.sh` | `83134c14f`. **The largest browser win of the session, and it was a build flag nobody had revisited.** `-sASYNCIFY_ADVISE` reports **36,334 instrumented functions** -- the whole program rewritten into state machines to support one DVD read. It is that broad because `EMULATE_FUNCTION_POINTER_CASTS` routes every indirect call through a JS `invoke_*` import and Asyncify must assume any import can suspend, so **an `ASYNCIFY_ONLY` list cannot help until the trampolines go**. JSPI instruments nothing: **wasm 14.5 MB -> 4.5 MB**, owner-verified booting/streaming/playing in Chrome *and* Firefox. `MELEE_WASM_SUSPEND=asyncify` is the fallback. Native is unaffected. |
| P-827 | **Owner-reported crash on the Special/Info menu: `SIGSEGV at 0x7c`** -- archive symbol names stored as `float` literals come out byte-reversed | done | claude (opus-5 1M), 2026-09-17 | `decomp/src/melee/mn/mninfo.c` + `patches/src/melee/mn/mninfo.c.patch` | **Fixed, and it is a class rather than a one-off.** `0x7c` is exactly `offsetof(HSD_JObj, aobj)`, so the jobj in `mn_8022ED6C` was NULL: the screen's GObj had no `hsd_obj` because its model never loaded. `mnInfo_803EFC08` is declared `AnimLoopSettings[0x12]` -- true of its first twelve bytes only. The rest is `MnInfoDataLayout`: SIS ids, two `printf` formats, three assert strings, and the **four archive symbol names** `mnInfo_80252758` passes to `lbArchive_LoadSections`. The decompiler rendered those bytes as float literals (`2.405757e8f` is `0x4D656E4D`, i.e. `"MenM"`). **A float literal is a value, so a big-endian machine stores its bytes in the order that spells the string and a little-endian one stores them backwards** -- `MenMainConCo_Top_joint` compiled as `MneMniaoCnopoT_ioj`, the lookup found nothing, and the joint stayed NULL. **Values recovered from the compiled binary, not guessed:** dumped `.data` at the symbol, reversed each 4-byte group and read the ASCII, giving `"%s.%s.%s"`, `"%s:%s:%s"`, `"Can't get user_data.\n"`, `"mninfo.c"`, `"user_data"`, sis_ids `{0x505,0x506,0x507,0x508}` and the four `MenMainConCo_Top_*` names. Now declared as a real `MnInfoDataLayout` with string literals under `PORT_PC`; `strings` confirms the four names appear intact. The layout is also 0xE0 while the array is 0xD8, the last name spilling into `gap_07_803EFCE0_data`, which the single object also settles. GameCube re-verified **100.00% matched (1130/1130)**; ctest 33/33. G-212 -- **sweep for other numeric-typed string data is worth doing; absurd float literals in a decompiled initializer are the tell.** |
| P-826 | Browser: the frame vertex upload is ~17 MB and the browser pays for it in a way native does not | open (deferred by the owner) | — | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c`, `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.h` | **Measured, then deliberately not done.** After P-824 and P-825, GL call count is no longer the browser's bottleneck (~7 calls/draw, ~1.2 ms) but `render` is still 7-15 ms. At 118k verts `glBufferData` uploads **17 MB every frame** across the JS/wasm boundary. A throwaway probe clamping `uv[8]` to `uv[4]` (144 -> 96 bytes) moved browser render from **0.114 to 0.089 us per vertex, ~-22%**, implying ~0.44 ms per MB uploaded. Packing to the 1-3 texcoords actually used would save ~9-10 MB/frame, ~4 ms, which is the 50->60 fps gap. **This directly contradicts P-817**, which measured the same idea at 0.17% *on native* -- native memcpy at that rate is bandwidth the GPU has spare, the browser's copy is 10-50x dearer. **Not implemented because the repack costs CPU native does not want, so it would mean a per-target code path, and the owner's instruction is to keep browser and native identical.** Revisit only with a design that pays on both, or if that constraint changes. |
| P-821 | ~~Web only: the opponent renders in the wrong costume colours~~ -- **NOT A BUG, closed** | not-a-bug | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | — | **CLOSED 2026-09-17: this is correct retail behaviour.** The owner checked it against Dolphin -- **the opponent's costume is supposed to be random.** The decompilation says so outright: `mncharsel.c:2607` sets `doors[door].costume = HSD_Randi(gm_GetNumCostumesForCKind(...))` when the CSS fills an unattended slot. So a CP Yoshi in blue and a CP Kirby in orange are the game working. **Why it looked like a regression, and the bit worth keeping:** this port only started *showing* it at P-751, which seeded the RNG from the host clock. Before that `OSGetTick` was virtual and every playthrough was identical, so the "random" costume was the same one every time and read as fixed. **Unfreezing an RNG makes correct-but-varying behaviour look like a new bug** -- P-752 and P-753 were real bugs P-751 uncovered the same way, so the prior was wrong here, and this is the first case where the answer was that nothing was broken. **Everything below is the investigation, retained because it rules things out for whoever meets the next costume-shaped report.** Original report follows. **Owner-reported 2026-09-16, browser build only; native is correct.** Player 1 is the right colour, the opponent is wrong: a CP Yoshi came out blue instead of green, a CP Kirby orange instead of pink. Wrong in the **character-select preview and the actual game model**, so it is one value chosen once, not a render-path artifact. Costume 0 looks correct and non-zero costumes look wrong, but **that is an inference from two screenshots, not a measurement** -- the discriminating test nobody has run yet is: **on the CSS, cycle your own costume with X/Y and see whether your own alternates are also wrong.** If they are, it is per-costume data; if not, it is the per-port costume assignment. **Ruled out:** (1) *the asset converter* -- I diagnosed `conv_ft_parts_desc`'s vis_table walk terminating on a legal NULL hole, and that **defect is real and now fixed** (P-820, `80effa94f`, 13 of 17 tables measured stopping on a hole). It is **still not this bug**: the converter is shared by both targets and native renders costumes correctly, so a converter defect cannot produce a browser-only symptom. **Do not close P-821 against P-820.** (Moot now -- P-821 is not a bug at all -- but the reasoning stands for any future report.) (2) *gross struct layout drift between i386 and wasm32* -- `wasm_census.sh` does pass `-DLINT`, so all 189 `ASSERT_SIZE`/`ASSERT_OFFSET` declarations compile on the wasm build and it links clean. (3) *the CSS logic itself* -- `doors[].costume`, `isDuplicateCostume` and `gm_80169264`'s table were read and are sound, and `PlayerInitData.color` is a plain `u8` at offset 3 with no endianness hazard. **Where to look next:** something target-specific between Clang/wasm32 and GCC/i386 that `-DLINT` does not cover -- bitfield groups are the obvious family, since P-806 already found Clang silently ignoring `scalar_storage_order`. `ctest bit_order` covers the `CmdUnion` and `ColorOverlay_x8_t` structs under Emscripten, but **`PORT_BF_BE` groups outside those structs are not covered by it**, and `PlayerInitData` itself carries two 8x1-bit `u8` groups at xC/xD. Start by printing `ckind` and `color` per player at `fn_8016D8AC` (`gmvs.c:1591`) in both builds and diffing -- that says in one run whether the value is wrong when assigned or later. |
| P-822 | Two walks of the same per-costume `vis_table` disagree about NULL holes | done (duplicate of **P-820**) | melee-a4, 2026-09-16 |  `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` | Found while chasing P-821 and **not** its cause. `conv_ft_data` (~line 4186) reads the slot, `continue`s on NULL, and only then requires a relocation target. `conv_ft_parts_desc` (~line 4015) runs the same two tests in the **opposite order**, so a legitimately-NULL slot fails the reloc test and sets `ended`, terminating the whole walk -- everything behind the hole is then reachable by nothing. Its own comment claims it is "bounded the same way" as `conv_ft_data`. This is the G-199 shape a third time. **Verified and fixed by melee-a4 as P-820** (`80effa94f`, converter v130): it probed every vis_table walk across all 861 archives and **13 of 17 tables stop on a NULL hole**, most at costume 0 column 3, with **2 to 14 live slots still behind**. So `conv_ft_parts_desc` was converting costume 0's first three lookups and nothing else, and every later costume's `FtPartsVisLookup` stayed big-endian -- the same unconverted-lookup shape as P-765. **This is a real defect and it is not P-821's cause**: the converter is shared by both targets, and P-821 is browser-only. Kept as a cross-reference so the two are not conflated again. |

| P-820 | `conv_ft_parts_desc`'s `vis_table` walk ends at the first NULL slot, so most costumes are never converted | done | claude (opus-5 1M, crash burn-down), 2026-09-16 (reported by the frame-time session) | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v130**) | Two walks cover the same per-costume `void* (*)[4]` table and disagree about holes. `conv_ft_data` reads the slot, treats NULL as legal and keeps going, and only ends on a **non-NULL** word that is not a relocation target. `conv_ft_parts_desc` tested `c->reloc[p]` **first**, and a legitimately-NULL slot is not a relocation target -- so it ended the walk at the first hole, while its own comment claimed to be "bounded the same way". **Measured before fixing, because the report was a code reading:** across the disc, **13 of the 17 tables stop on a NULL slot**, most of them at costume 0 column 3, with between **2 and 14** relocation-backed slots still behind them. | P-824 | **Owner-reported: DK and Ness have a "force field" that shoves the sandbag and other fighters away; every other character walks straight through them** | done | claude (opus-5 1M, crash burn-down), 2026-09-17 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v131**), `native/CMakeLists.txt` (`decomp_match` pin) | **Fixed.** `ftData->x50` is the player-nudge box, `Vec2 { x_offset, radius }`; `ftChangeParam` copies it to `fp->x2C4` and `ftCommon_8007DD7C` shoves two fighters apart when `ABS(dx) < a->x2C4.y + b->x2C4.y`. The converter took the array length from `off + 0x54`, **which is not a count** -- the decomp types `ftData::x54` as `int`, but it is a relocation field in **all 30** fighter archives, and `conv_ft_data` already walks it correctly as P-685's per-costume part-table pointer. Read as a count it gave 28132-ish, the `count <= 256` guard rejected it, and the nudge box was **never converted for any fighter**. Left big-endian the radius means whatever its bytes mean reversed: DK `5.2 = 40a66666` and Ness `3.1 = 40466666` both end `66 66` and come back as **2.72e+23** -- an infinite-radius force field -- while Mario `3.3` -> 4.2e-8, Bowser `8.0` -> 9.1e-44 and Peach `2.9` -> **negative**, i.e. no push at all. **The two characters the owner named are exactly the two whose bytes end `66 66`**, which is what turned a guess into a diagnosis. **How it was found:** the owner's own `[pos]` capture, after the probe was extended to print the ECB -- it showed DK and the sandbag separating at exactly **0.3/frame each** with `vel=0` and `kb=0`, 30 units apart and still pushing, and moving in **z** as well as x. A direct position write in x and z is `fp->xF8_playerNudgeVel` (fighter.c:2328-2330) and nothing else. **Verified:** `fp->x2C4` now reads the disc values at runtime (DK 5.2, Ness 3.1, Mario 3.3, Bowser 8.0); ctest **33/33**; five matrix cells re-run clean. **`decomp_match`'s pinned end position moved** from `(14.20,41.04)` to `(15.95,44.89)` -- a real behaviour change, because the mechanic has been dead since the port began. Proven to be this change and not drift by reverting only the converter hunk and watching the old value return exactly. G-206. |
| P-823 | **`-O3` is not worth taking: +2.5% for a rendered-output change nobody can explain** | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | — | The build is `-O2 -g -DNDEBUG -fno-strict-aliasing`, 32-bit, no LTO/PGO, so `-O3` is the obvious free lever. Measured: **67.65e9 -> 65.96e9 instructions, -2.5%** on a 900-frame heavy match; cycles roughly flat. **But captures differ on every stage tried** -- 0.04% to 0.27% of bytes, max delta 65, which is triangle edges moving by a subpixel, i.e. float evaluation changing. **Not FP contraction**: `-ffp-contract=off` on top still differs. Unexplained, and possibly `-O3` exploiting UB that `-O2` does not, in a tree that compiles `src/` with `-w` and already needs `-fno-strict-aliasing` for exactly that reason. **2.5% is a bad trade for the byte-identical capture, which is this project's main verification tool.** Revisit only with the cause identified. See G-203 for the methodological consequence. **Numbering:** filed as P-820 in `5cd465a74` and renumbered to P-823 the same day -- melee-a4's `80effa94f` took P-820 concurrently and is cited by its commit message and converter v130, so that one keeps the number. |
| P-826 | **Owner-reported crash in the Data / VS Records menu, and the crash reporter could not report it** | **both fixed** | claude (opus-5 1M), 2026-09-17 | `native/decomp/boot/boot_triage.c`, `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c` | **Two bugs in one report.** The crash: `SIGSEGV at 0xc024c400`, `mode 1 scene 0` (`GM_MENU`), reached from the Data/VS-Records screens; the in-proc gobj is `class=0 user_data=(nil)` and the item list is garbage (`kind=-2132339776`, an unaligned `entity`). **Cause not yet known, because the reporter produced no usable stack.** The reporter: `crash_reporter` was installed with **`SA_NODEFER`**, so the signal stayed unblocked inside its own handler -- `backtrace()` is not async-signal-safe and unwinds the very stack that just failed, and when it faulted the handler re-entered itself, giving thirty frames of `_Unwind_Backtrace` and nothing else. There was also **no alternate signal stack**, so a stack-exhausting crash could not be reported at all. Both fixed: `SA_NODEFER` dropped (the closing `raise()` still kills the process, because a blocked signal is delivered on return), a 64 KB `sigaltstack` with `SA_ONSTACK`, and a re-entry guard that writes one line with `write(2,...)` and lets the default action end it. `dump_items` now range-checks each `Item*` against MEM1 the way `dump_fighter` has since G-204. **Proven with a standalone overflow test:** without the alt stack a stack overflow prints *nothing*; with it the handler reports the fault and 64 frames. G-210. **Next:** one re-run of the same repro now produces a real backtrace, and the shape of the original -- a fault inside `backtrace()` -- pointed at stack exhaustion. **That hypothesis was wrong and the re-run said so**: with a working reporter the same screen gave a four-frame answer -- `assertion "jobj"` at `jobj.h:513` from `mnDiagram_DrawCellValue` <- `mnDiagram_DrawGridValues` <- `mnDiagram_RefreshGrid` <- `mnDiagram_InputProc`. **Cause: half a cross-symbol overlay.** `mnDiagram_Init` fills its models through a `mnDiagram_Assets` cast whose eight pointer arrays run 0x94..0x118, and on the console those bytes *are* the eight `MenMain*_Top` `StaticModelDesc` globals. An earlier fix backed that run with `mnDiagram_asset_block` and aliased `mnDiagram_FighterDisplayOrder` and `mnDiagram_NameDisplayOrder` into it but left the descriptors as separate objects, so the archive filled the block while every `MenMain*_Top` stayed zero and `HSD_JObjLoadJoint(NULL)` returned NULL. The mapping is spelled out by the loader itself -- `"MenMainNmB_Top_joint"` writes `&assets->NmB[0]` and `StaticModelDesc` is exactly `{joint, animjoint, matanim_joint, shapeanim_joint}` -- so all eight are now `.set` aliases at the console's offsets, with a `STATIC_ASSERT` per offset. Symbols verified in `nm`: block `0xfbd6a0`, `MenMainNmB_Top` `0xfbd744` = block+0xA4. GameCube re-verified **100.00% matched (1130/1130)**; ctest 33/33. G-211. |
| P-816 | Resolve texgen matrices once per primitive instead of once per vertex | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c` | `5065f21a7`. `texgen_coord` re-derived two `tex_mtx_slot` range-classifications and the `GX_PNMTX` special case per vertex per coordinate; none of it can change inside a primitive. Hoisted into a plan built beside the vertex decoder, caching **pointers** (matrix contents are reloaded between primitives and must be read live; the address is what is stable). | P-817 | Four of the five `fighter stuck` soak rows were the detector, not the game | done | claude (opus-5 1M, crash burn-down), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c` | **Not a game bug -- the rule was wrong.** The stuck test is `motion_id` frozen **and** `cur_anim_frame` frozen, and the states where the engine parks a fighter have no animation to advance, so they trip it by construction. Traced the Kongo Jungle case rather than guessing: the barrel captures the fighter at frame 297 and sets its own release countdown from `yakumono_param->unk20/unk24` (`grkongo.c:442`) to **479 frames**, which it then counts down one per frame, correctly -- and the detector's threshold is **420**. The barrel could never win. `ftCo_MS_BarrelWait` (293), `ftCo_MS_Barrel` (340) and `ftCo_MS_LiftWait` (174) are now exempt, and `ftCo_MS_DeadDown` (0) is exempt **only with no stocks left**, because the same state with stocks remaining is a stalled respawn and is worth reporting -- which is exactly what it then caught (P-818). With items on the Barrel Cannon is also an item, which is why Corneria was in this group. **Result: p19-20/g7, p1-2/g30, p16-17/g27 and p7-8/g5 all clean; p4-5/g11 survives and is real.** |

| P-813 | **The frame texture index was never cleared with the table it indexes** -- `gx_hle_begin_frame` reset `frame_tcount` but left `frame_tex_hash` holding every key its predecessors wrote | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c` | `4a9d64be6`. Owner reported ~38 fps with a full house (`game=13.8ms render=12.2ms draws=780 verts=145k`); two players was fine, so it scaled with fighters, not time. Never *unsafe* -- `frame_tex_find` rejects `idx >= frame_tcount`, and `gx_hle_discard_geometry` has always cleared both -- but it made the hash degenerate into a scan within seconds of play, two ways at once: a lookup for a new key stopped hitting an empty slot early and probed the whole stale cluster `memcmp`-ing 48-byte keys, and because `GXLoadTexObj` only records a key when `frame_tex_find` hands back an **empty** slot, insertions quietly stopped happening. The table filled, stayed full, and indexed less and less of the frame. **Also degraded the dedup itself**, which is pressure on exactly the `GX_HLE_MAX_TEXTURES` cap P-740 raised to 2048 and then overran again on Corneria. Measured: 900-frame headless match, pinned seed, pinned core, **66.04e9 -> 53.03e9 instructions, -19.7%**; run-to-run spread 0.35% -> 0.02%, which is the same bug seen from the other side. Geometry identical over 900 frames, frame-420 capture byte-identical, ctest 33/33. G-200. |
| P-814 | **`GXInitTexObj` threw away the decoded GL texture on every call**, and HSD re-inits a texobj every time it binds a material | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.{c,h}`, `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.{c,h}`, `native/decomp/render/viewer_main.c` | `661e14e0a`. **The cache was not too small, it was being emptied.** Over 300 frames of a two-player match: 26,187 decodes, 18,198 of them **CMPR art straight off the disc that cannot have changed** -- against **166** LRU evictions. I had filed this as a `MAX_GL_TEXTURES=256` vs 2048-binds thrash and that was **wrong**; the counters said invalidations ~= misses and evictions ~= 0. P-685 added the invalidation for the opening movie's THP planes, which are `HSD_MemAlloc`'d and CPU-updated in place; it was only ever needed for images the game **writes to**. Now gated on `gx_hle_image_is_asset()` -- skip it when the image lies in a registered archive range. EFB copy destinations are deliberately not covered: `efb_copy_tex` invalidates its destination explicitly at all three sites. The range table has 125 entries and this runs per `GXInitTexObj`, so the answer is memoised direct-mapped and stamped with a generation the table bumps, making a stale answer impossible rather than merely unlikely. Decodes **26,187 -> 1,305**, CMPR **18,198 -> 270** (load time); what remains is format 0, the per-frame I4 shadow copies, which are genuinely dynamic. **53.04e9 -> 33.34e9 instructions, -37.1%**, one binary against itself. Two-player `render=` **4.0ms -> 1.0ms**. `MELEE_GX_TEX_INVALIDATE=all` restores the old behaviour and captures at frames 200/420/700 are byte-identical between the modes -- that equivalence is the licence for the whole change. `MELEE_GX_TEX_STATS=1` reports hits/misses/evictions/decodes/invalidations. ctest 33/33 including `decomp_opening`, which is P-685's own regression. G-201. |
| P-812 | `texobj_find`'s 128-slot linear scan is **not** worth replacing -- measured, reverted, recorded so it is not retried | done | claude (opus-5, frame time), 2026-09-16 | — | **A dead end, documented on purpose.** I read `GXLoadTexObj`'s 7.8% self time as this scan and said so before measuring. Replacing it with a pointer-keyed open-addressed hash measured **66.24e9 against a 66.04e9 baseline** -- no gain, marginally worse, and it buys 2 KB of state and a rebuild-on-evict path. Re-profiling after the change showed `GXLoadTexObj` still at 7.81%: the cost was the frame texture dedup **inlined into it** (P-813), not the scan. 128 pointers is 8 cache lines walked linearly with a perfectly predictable stride, which the hardware prefetcher covers; the table is fixed-size, so the scan does not get longer with more fighters either. **Read the srcline profile before attributing a symbol's self time to the code you happen to be looking at.** |

| P-806 | **Gate zero: the browser build decoded every subaction command wrongly** -- `CMD_BE` was `scalar_storage_order`, which Clang ignores in silence, so `0x44000000` ("play sound", opcode 17) decoded as opcode 4 | done | claude (opus-5, 1M), 2026-09-16, worktree `../melee-wasm` branch `wasm` | `native/decomp/shim/decomp_cmd_bits.h` (new, generated), `native/tools/gen_cmd_bits.py` (new), `native/tests/test_bit_order.c`, `native/tests/cmd_bits_checks.inc` (new, generated), patches for `lb/types.h`, `ft/types.h`, `ftaction.c`, `itanimlist.c`, `lbcommand.c`, `lb_013B.c`, `Runtime/platform.h`, `gm/types.h`, `gr/types.h` | **Fixed by stating the layout instead of asking a compiler for it.** All 76 `CmdUnion` member structs (plus `gmScriptEventDefault` and the three `ColorOverlay_x8_t` groups) are now declared over one 32-bit **host-order** word -- the console's fields reversed and padded to the top -- and read through `CMD_U(cmd)` / `CO_X8(p)`, which byte-swap that one word. `PORT_BF_BE`'s two groups sit in a single byte, where there is no byte order, so they are simply padded and reversed. No attribute is left in the tree. **The stream is never converted and must not be:** `Command_05`/`Command_07` carry relocated host pointers inline, so the W0 note's "byte-swap the script at load" corrupts every jump; the converter cannot find scripts either (G-178). **Proof:** `ctest bit_order` now walks every field of every struct -- 122,884 reads -- comparing against the console's arithmetic, and passes identically under GCC and Emscripten; a separate 924,000-read differential run confirmed the new declarations decode exactly as GCC's `scalar_storage_order` ones did, so **desktop behaviour is bit-identical**. G-193, ADR-0022 amendment. **GameCube build verified for real:** `decomp/orig/GALE01/sys/main.dol` was never populated, so `ninja` had never run here; it does now, and `main.dol` comes out byte-identical to retail (`08e0bf20...`, 1130/1130 files). It caught one regression the token-stream argument could not -- `CMD_U(c)` expands to `((c)->u)`, which is token-equivalent but makes MWCC reload a pointer the original had already loaded (`it_802790C0`, 99.71%). Gated. |

| P-808 | **`dest % 32 == 0` at `devcom.c:0x1F0`, browser only** -- plus the `indirect call signature mismatch` behind it | done | claude (opus-5, 1M), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/sysdolphin/baselib/synth.{c,static.h}.patch`, `native/tools/wasm_census.sh` | **Two defects, both latent on every target.** (1) `hsd_SynthSFXLoadBuf` is a DMA destination declared `static u32[8]`, alignment 4; the console put it in MEM1 where section alignment made `dest % 32 == 0` true, and **x86 has been passing on luck** -- wasm-ld gave it a 16-byte boundary (`dest=0x00118c50`). `ATTRIBUTE_ALIGN(32)` here and on `lbl_804C4540`. (2) Three functions are cast into the `HSD_DevComCallback` slot without having its signature; PPC and x86 ignore the surplus, wasm's `call_indirect` traps. | P-810 | `itAnimlistCmdUnk` is read little-endian -- the item animlist opcode comes out byte-reversed **and** bit-reversed | fixed; **ruled out as the cause of P-780** | claude (opus-5 1M), 2026-09-17 | `decomp/src/melee/it/itanimlist.c` + `patches/src/melee/it/itanimlist.c.patch` | `typedef struct itAnimlistCmdUnk { u16 x0_b0:6; u16 opcode:8; u16 x0_b14:2; u16 x2; }` carries no endian marker, so `it_8027978C` got it wrong twice: the `u16` loads byte-reversed on a little-endian host, **and** GCC allocates bit-fields from the LSB where MWCC allocates from the MSB. On the console the group is packed MSB-first into a big-endian `u16`, so the opcode is bits 9..2 -- `(V >> 2) & 0xFF`. Same family as the `CMD_U16`/`CMD_S16` accessors already in this file (P-788) and `ftaction.c`'s `gfx_id`; those two were fixed and this one was left, deliberately, because it changes desktop gameplay and wanted a repro. **The owner has now supplied one: picking up the bat in Home-Run Contest freezes the fighter** (screenshot, 2026-09-17), which is the open P-780 symptom. **Why the shapes match:** read wrongly the opcode is almost always outside the handled 0..11 range, and `it_8027978C` falls through to `done` having consumed the command and performed **nothing** -- so the animlist keeps running while the action it should have taken never happens, which is a fighter frozen mid-pickup rather than a crash. **Not yet confirmed to be the cause**, and deliberately not claimed: `it_8027978C` is reached by no soak cell tried (0 hits across two 5400-frame item-heavy matches), so it could not be verified headlessly. GameCube build re-verified **100.00% matched (1130/1130)**; ctest 33/33. **Owner's experiment, two runs:** `MELEE_ANIMLIST_TRACE=1` and pick up the bat -- each command prints `word=`, `opcode_be=`, `opcode_le=` and marks the old decode when it was out of range; then `MELEE_ANIMLIST_LE=1` restores the old decode so the freeze can be turned back on and off without rebuilding. **Owner ran it 2026-09-17 and the answer was no: zero `[animlist]` lines during the bat pickup and the freeze, so `it_8027978C` is never reached on that path and this is not P-780's cause.** The fix stands on its own merits -- the bit layout was provably wrong and it is the third of the `CMD_U16`/`gfx_id` family -- but it must not be credited with the freeze. He also reported the freeze happens on **swinging** the bat, not only on picking it up, which moves the search to the fighter's item-attack state machine. |

The fighter proc is running on a gobj that is **not a Fighter**: the dump shows `kind=?(-1) player=255 motion=-1 scale=(0.6,0.6,1) self_vel=(-nan,-nan,-nan) held=0xe97de97d`. | P-815 | **Owner-reported crash: `dobj.c:312` `mobj has unexpected blending flags (0x31001060)` on Samus's throw** | done | claude (opus-5 1M, crash burn-down), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v128**) | **Fixed.** Stack: `ftCo_CatchWait_IASA` -> `ftCo_800DD1E4` -> `ftCo_800DD4B0` -> `ftCo_800DD398` -> `ftSs_Init_CreateThrowGrappleBeam` -> `ftCommon_SetAccessory` -> `HSD_JObjLoadJoint` -> `HSD_DObjLoadDesc`. **The panic prints its own diagnosis:** `DObjLoad` switches on `mobj->rendermode & 0x60000000` and panics on the fourth combination, and `0x31001060` byte-reversed is `0x60100031`, which lands on `case 0x60000000`. So the descriptor was never converted rather than corrupt. **Cause:** `conv_ft_data`'s `x48_items` walk treats the array as `Article*` and stops at the first slot that fails the Article test. Samus's `items[4]` is a `UNK_SAMUS_S1` (`{HSD_Joint*; HSD_AnimJoint**; HSD_AnimJoint*; HSD_MatAnimJoint*}`, ftSamus/types.h:80), so the walk stopped there and the grapple beam's whole joint tree stayed big-endian -- measured directly: the joint at `PlSs.dat` +0x1a4a8 had `seen=0` and its three scale words read `0x0000803f`, which is `1.0f` from the wrong end. **Three more fighters had the identical hole and all three reach the same `ftCommon_SetAccessory` panic:** Kirby `items[4]` (`ftKb_SpecialN_800F5898` -> `ftCo_ThrownKirby.c:126`), Yoshi `items[3]` (`ftYs_SpecialN_8012CDD4` -> `ftCo_YoshiEgg.c:108`) and Sheik `items[4]`/`items[5]` (`ftSk_SpecialS_80110610`). On disc all three have the same shape -- `class_name` NULL, a reversed flags word, a child pointer, `next` NULL -- an ordinary `HSD_JObjDesc` root that no shape heuristic can tell from an Article, which is why this is keyed on the symbol name exactly as G&W's `items[10]` is (P-765). **Also landed: the run is now clamped by `next_public_after`.** Measured on all 21 fighters that reach the clamp, it fires at exactly the slot where the array ends, and that slot is `ftData` itself -- so the old loop's last act was to offer `ftData->x0` to the Article test and trust a heuristic to reject it. Proven lossless: no fighter's accepted-slot count dropped, coverage 82.83% -> 82.84%, ctest **33/33**. **Two traps cost time here and are now G-199:** `MELEE_UNWALKED` reports only targets whose own first word is a relocation, and a joint root's `class_name` is NULL, so `PlSs.dat` reported **zero** unwalked descriptors while carrying this; and `hsd_asset_convert` reads the asset cache before converting, so a probe inside a walker prints nothing until `MELEE_NO_ASSET_CACHE=1`. **Owner retest wanted:** grab and throw with Samus (all four directions), Kirby's swallow, Yoshi's egg, Sheik's side-B. No automated input could produce a connecting grab, so the proof here is byte-level, not gameplay. |
| P-801 | **The browser renderer issued ~24,000 GL calls a frame** -- `upload_draw_uniforms` does 52 `glUniform*` per draw, 461 draws | done | claude (opus-5, 1M), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c`, `native/decomp/render/viewer_main.c` | Native GL does not care (desktop rendered the same scene in 8.6 ms); in a browser each call crosses the JS/wasm boundary at ~0.5 us, ~12 ms of a 15 ms render. Desktop render 8.56 -> 6.15 ms. **`MELEE_GX_UNI_CACHE=0` forces every upload and the seeded match is pixel-identical either way** -- keep that switch, it is the only thing that makes a renderer cache reviewable. `MELEE_GX_UNI_STATS=1` prints the hit rate. | P-802 | The frame vertex buffer is a **21 MB `glBufferData` respec every frame**, two thirds of it texcoords nothing reads | open | — | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c:2774` | **Re-measured 2026-09-16; the row's premise was stale.** It is no longer once per draw -- the call is hoisted out of the draw loop and happens once a frame. What is left is size. `sizeof(GxHleVertex)` is 144 bytes of which `uv[8][3]` is 96, and a probe over a 900-frame match says the in-match texgen count is **1 for 70% of vertices, 3 at most**; counts 5-7 occur only at load. At the owner's 145,662 verts that is 21 MB and 1.26 GB/s, and `memcpy` is now the top line in the profile (~8%). Packing to a per-frame stride would cut it roughly in half, but the vertex is written before the frame's max texgen count is known, so it needs a repack at upload -- read 144B/write 48B per vertex against one flat driver `memcpy`, which may not win. **Measure, do not assume.** Orphan-then-`glBufferSubData` was tried and is **not** the answer on its own: instruction count identical (33.334e9 both ways, it changes driver sync not CPU work) and `render=` indistinguishable at two players (1.93/1.97 vs 2.05/1.81 ms). It may still help at four players, where render is 12 ms; that case cannot be reproduced headlessly, since the match harness is `MELEE_MATCH_P0`/`P1` only. |

| P-764 | `ftparts.c:793 assertion "0"` on **Roy, Pichu and Ganondorf**, every stage | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v101) | Found by the P-759 fighter x stage matrix, 54 of 780 runs. The three affected fighters are **all clones** (Roy<-Marth, Pichu<-Pikachu, Ganondorf<-Captain Falcon), and no non-clone hits it, so look at the clone data path rather than at `ftparts.c`. Repro: `MELEE_NO_CARD=1 MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x838169d0 MELEE_MATCH_P0=23 MELEE_MATCH_P1=24 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=10 ./build/native/melee_decomp_boot --boot-frames 900 --boot-timeout 90 --boot-match 20`. Note P-755 is `ftparts.c:518` on the Kirby copy path -- same file, probably the same family of per-character part tables. **Fixed (converter v101): the `vis_table` walk in `conv_ft_data` broke only its inner loop when it ran off the end of the table.** The walk is `for costume in 0..7 { for col in 0..3 { p = vis_table + (costume*4+col)*4; ... if (!c->reloc[p]) break; } }`, and the comment already said the first non-pointer word is past the table. Breaking only the inner loop left `costume` free to advance *past* the end and find a later word that happened to be a relocation target -- for Pichu, the `ftData_x8_x8.xC` costume table three words on. `conv_ft_vis_lookup` then read the TObj-index arrays as `FtPartsVisLookup` entries and `conv_u32` byte-swapped words that hold **two u16 indices each**, so index 2 came back as `0x0200` = 512 and `ftParts_80075240` asserted "can't find tobj!". Fighters with fewer costumes have shorter `vis_table`s, which is exactly why only the three clones hit it. An `ended` flag now stops both loops. **Verified:** Roy/Pichu/Ganondorf/Dr.Mario across all 29 stages, 116 runs, zero `ftparts.c:793`. **Note the descriptor coverage went *down* 0.01 pt (76.88 -> 76.87, 29 descriptors)** -- those 29 were being "walked" wrongly, so the drop is the fix working; the floor's 0.05 tolerance absorbs it and **the floor was not lowered**. |
| P-765 | `SIGSEGV in HSD_DObjSetFlags` on **Fox, Mr. Game & Watch and Kirby**, every stage | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v102) | P-759 matrix, 52 of 780 runs. Fighter-bound, not stage-bound. `HSD_DObjSetFlags` walks the DObj list of a model, so this is a model/DObj tree that is malformed for exactly these three -- and G&W and Kirby are the two most unusual model set-ups on the disc. Repro: seed `0x838169d0`, `MELEE_MATCH_P0=2 MELEE_MATCH_P1=3 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=11`. **Partly diagnosed.** Not the same bug as P-764 -- it survives that fix. The crash is `HSD_DObjSetFlags(dobj=0x40)` <- `ftParts_80074D7C` (ftparts.c:665) <- `ftParts_800750C8` <- `ftDrawCommon_80081200` <- `lbShadow_8000F38C`, i.e. the shadow pass walking the part-visibility lookups. At the crash `k` (the `TempS.x0` loop bound) reads **184549376 = 0x0B000000, which is 11 byte-swapped**, and `r29[k]` then indexes `dobj_list->data` (count 116) with 202. So a **count inside `FtPartsVisLookup`/`TempS` is still big-endian**. `conv_ft_vis_lookup` is the walker; its two suspect guards are `count > 0 && count <= 64` (a legitimately larger count skips the whole `TempS` loop, leaving every `TempS.x0` unconverted) and `if (!in_data(c, t, 8) || !mark(c, t)) break;` -- **`mark` returning false means "already visited", and `break` then abandons the rest of the array** rather than skipping one entry. G&W's `model_num` is 11, which matches the observed value, and G&W/Fox/Kirby are exactly the three with unusual model counts. Possibly the same root as (see P-758's head item): these files are 12-13% walked. **Wind-up measurement (opencode glm-5.3-flash, 2026-09-15; handoff `native/AI/handoffs/2026-09-15-P-765-windup.md`).** Crash reproduced and traced in gdb: the bad `TempS` is at runtime `0x80adcafc`, `x0 = 0x0B000000` (big-endian 11), `x4 = 0x80adcaf0` -> a u8 index array whose bytes are `01 04 05 06 07 08 0a 0b 0c 0d 0e` -- exactly 11 indices, matching the cold count. For `PlGw.dat` (`model_num` 11 confirmed at data 0, `vis_table` at 0x7c08): only costume row 0 holds real lookup pointers (0x76e0/0x7878/0x7b58/0x7bb0), rows 1-4 are zero, and **neither suspect guard fires** -- all 11 entries of all four arrays convert (counts <= 8), every observed `mark`-false `break` hits an already-fully-converted region, and after the walk **zero** reachable `TempS.x0` reads big-endian. So the cold count is NOT in the arrays `vis_table` reaches. Two leads: (1) the repro's `MELEE_MATCH_P0=2 P1=3` is Ft_Kind_Captain/Donkey per `decomp/src/melee/ft/forward.h`, i.e. this seed's crash may be Falcon's or DK's file, not G&W -- pattern-match the crash region (needle: `01 04 05 06 07 08 0a 0b 0c 0d 0e` followed by word `00 00 00 0b`) against the other `Pl*.dat` and check that offset's coverage (several `Pl*` files are 12-13% walked); (2) a separate converter bug found on the way: in an `Nr`-file conversion `conv_ft_vis_lookup` walked `off=0x18`, the public-symbol string region, and byte-swapped ASCII symbol-name words -- a `vis_table` slot can relocate to a non-lookup value (`0x18`); needs a pointee-shape check like `looks_like_unconverted_joint` (P-748/G-183). The gdb scripts and the probe replaying the walk on the raw file are in `/tmp/opencode/` (`gw-trace5.gdb`, `probe_vis3.py`, raw `PlGw.dat`). | **FIXED (converter v102).** Two corrections to the row above first: it is **G&W alone**, not three fighters -- the matrix pairs fighter *i* with *i+1*, so the failing pairs p2-3 and p3-4 share only CKind 3, and listing both members of each pair overcounted. And it is **not** the `Pl*` descriptor gap. **Cause:** `ftData->x48_items` is *almost* an `Article*` array, and the walk proves each entry looks like one before following it -- but `ftGw_Init_OnLoad` (ftgamewatch.c:540) does `fp->x5AC.xC[4] = items[10]`, where `items[10]` is an `FtPartsVisLookup[]`, the fifth part-visibility group. Nothing else in the archive points at it. Left unconverted its `TempS.x0` read `0x0B000000` (big-endian 11), so `ftParts_80074D7C` indexed a 116-entry DObj list with 202 and handed the garbage to `HSD_DObjSetFlags`, in the **shadow pass**. **A shape heuristic does not work here and I tried it first:** G&W's lookup array passes the Article test, because its first word is a plausible `ItemAttr*`, so the generic walk was converting it as an Article. The fix keys the special slot on the **symbol name** (`ft_x48_vis_lookup_slot`), which is exact because the decompilation names it. **Verified:** `HSD_DObjSetFlags` is gone from a 145-run sweep of CKind 1..5 x 29 stages. **Kirby has a separate instance** -- `ftKb_SpecialN_800F14B4` sets `xC[4]` from `hat->hat_dynamics[3]`, i.e. the copy-ability hat archives, not `x48_items`; it now surfaces as `SIGSEGV in HSD_JObjGetFlags` on G&W/Kirby pairs and is filed as **P-776**.

| P-769 | **Mute City** asserts `mplib.c:4804 "0"` | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v103) | P-759 matrix: 25 of 26 fighters, `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=10`, 24 runs. `mplib` is map collision, so this is the stage's collision data. Note Mute City's `yakumono_param` was a converter fix (v94) -- check whether the collision data has the same shape of problem. **Diagnosed:** `mpJointUpdateDynamics(joint_id=4)` <- `mpLib_80055E24` <- `grMuteCity_801F0D20` (grmutecity.c:1073) <- `grMuteCity_801F0120`. The assert is the final `else` of a slope classification: it fires only when a collision line's two vertices have **`dx == 0` and `dy == 0`**, i.e. a degenerate line whose endpoints coincide. So this is bad vertex data or a bad vertex index on dynamic collision joint 4 (Mute City's moving platforms), not a logic bug in `mplib`. Next step: print `v0_idx`/`v1_idx` and the two `groundCollVtx` positions at the assert and see whether the indices are wrong or the positions are zero/unconverted. | **FIXED (converter v103) -- and the earlier reading of the assert was wrong.** It does not only fire on a zero-length line: **NaN fails every comparison**, so NaN coordinates fall through `dx > 0`, `dx < 0`, `dy > 0`, `dy < 0` to the same final `else`. The vertices were NaN, not coincident. Chain: `conv_joint` had `if (!(flags & (HSD_JOBJ_PTCL | HSD_JOBJ_SPLINE)) && u != 0) conv_dobj(c, u);` -- so a joint whose flags say **SPLINE** had its +0x10 union **skipped entirely** and the `HSD_Spline` behind it stayed big-endian: `numcv`, `tension`, the `Vec3` control points and both precomputed arc-length tables. `grMuteCity_801F0D20` evaluates that spline (`grMuteCity_801F094C` -> `grMc_SplineEvalWrapped`) and feeds the result to `mpLineSetPos`, which writes it straight into `groundCollVtx[].pos` -- so the collision mesh became NaN and `mpJointUpdateDynamics(4)` asserted. **New walker `conv_spline`.** Its three arrays have type-dependent lengths (`cv` is indexed `[idx]`, `[idx*3]` or `[idx]-1` by `type`, spline.c:91-131), so each is bounded by `next_pointed_at_after` rather than a guessed count -- guessing an array length is what P-739 was. **This also fixed P-772**: Zebes and Onett now run clean. **A different bug is behind it on Mute City** -- `Ground_801C2FE0` (ground.c:1694) now segfaults on a NULL `Ground_804D6950`, filed as **P-777**. Two dead ends worth recording: the vertex data is *correct* at `mpLibLoad` (real floats, scale 1.0), and joint 4 owns vertices 24..29 but has a **NULL** JObj so its own update returns before writing `pos` -- I spent several runs looking for another joint that owned them, and there isn't one.
| P-770 | **Dream Land (OldPupupu)** asserts `memory.c:55 "adr"` | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (`conv_oldpupupu_param`, v110) | P-759 matrix: 24 of 26 fighters, `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=28`, 22 runs. An `HSD_ObjAlloc` free-list assert -- the same failure *class* as **P-725** (Ness' yo-yo, `memory.c:23`) and **P-749** (the 32 KB shadow buffer), so all three may share a heap-accounting cause. **Diagnosed:** `HSD_MemAlloc(size=1024)` fails inside `hsdAllocMemPiece(136)` <- `HSD_JObjAlloc` <- `JObjLoadJointSub` <- `HSD_JObjLoadJoint` <- `Ground_GetStageGObj(map_id=2)` <- `grOldPupupu_802108B4(2)`. So the HSD heap is **exhausted while loading map 2's joint tree**, not corrupted by a bad pointer. Two candidates worth separating before fixing: the tree is genuinely huge because a `child`/`next` chain is unconverted and effectively unbounded (count the `HSD_JObjAlloc` calls -- if it is thousands, that is the answer), or the heap is legitimately too small on this stage. GrOp.dat is 90.5% walked, so an unconverted joint field is plausible. **Measured 2026-09-16, and the measurement is weaker than it looks -- do not lean on it.** `HSD_JObjAlloc` is hit **11,354 times** on Dream Land against **1,633** on the default stage, but those are **whole-boot counts, not scoped to the failing `HSD_JObjLoadJoint`**, and the two runs load different stages, fighters and attract demos, so a 7x difference is not by itself evidence of an unbounded tree. **Against the unbounded theory:** the backtrace at the failure is only **three** `JObjLoadJointSub` levels deep, and both the `child` and the `next` descent recurse, so a cyclic or runaway chain would show as deep recursion -- and it does not. That points at a **wide but finite** tree, i.e. possibly a genuinely undersized heap rather than a converter bug. **To settle it:** count allocations *between* entry to `Ground_GetStageGObj(map_id=2)` and the failure, and compare against the joint count the converter walks in `GrOp.dat`. If they agree, the tree is real and this is heap sizing -- which is **not** a converter fix and does not belong to the P-758 session. **SOLVED 2026-09-16 -- cause found, fix specified, handed to the P-758 session because it lives in `hsd_convert.c`. It is not a heap-sizing bug and not an unbounded joint tree.** The arena is a correct 24 MB and the heap really is exhausted (`HSD_MemAlloc: 1024 bytes FAILED, heap free = 512`), but the cause is upstream. Scoped counts: `HSD_JObjAlloc` **10,340**, `HSD_JObjLoadJoint` **950**, and `grOldPupupu_802108B4` **448** -- against only nine call sites, eight of them one-shot at stage init. The 448th comes from `groldpupupu.c:408`, `for (i = 0; i < count; i++) grOldPupupu_802108B4(2)`, where `count` is a literal 1 or 3; so the *outer* spawn routine is running ~150 times. It is gated by a respawn timer read straight out of `yakumono_param` (`respawn = yakumono_param->x2; min_respawn = yakumono_param->x0;`). **Measured at runtime on GrOp.dat: `x0 = -18421`, `x2 = -24561`, and `x14`..`x30` all denormals (4.9e-41, 5.5e-41, ...) -- the whole struct is still big-endian.** Negative timers never gate, so it spawns cloud objects every frame until the heap dies. **This is exactly the open audit in P-708** ("the remaining VS-legal stages whose `yakumono_param` public is still converted raw"), now with a crash attached. **Fix:** give `GrOp.dat` (Dream Land) a `yakumono_param` layout in `conv_yakumono_param`, the way GrMc/GrBb/GrZe already have one; the struct here starts with at least two `s16` timers at +0x00/+0x02 and a run of `f32` from +0x14. **Do the rest of P-708's list at the same time** -- any stage still raw has the same failure waiting, and this one only surfaced because the spawn loop allocates.  **FIXED, converter v110.** `conv_oldpupupu_param`, selected by the marker **`GrdOldpupupu`** -- it appears in 62 of `GrOp.dat`'s 72 publics and in **no other archive on the disc**, and GrOp matched none of the existing markers, which is exactly why it fell through to the raw fallback. **It is not a flat `conv_u32_range`** like Mute City or Big Blue, and that is the one thing to get right: `struct grOldpupupu_YakumonoParam` (groldpupupu.c:25) starts with **four `s16`**, then two `int`, then nine `f32` -- 0x34 bytes, which is the symbol's extent in the archive to the byte. Swapping the first two words as `u32` would exchange the pairs. **Confirmed against the raw archive before writing it:** the fields read 3000, 4000, 30, 0, 600, 1200, 0.2, -17, 76, -18, -74, 40, -10, 180, 360 -- every one plausible -- and `x0`/`x2` read little-endian are **-18421 / -24561**, exactly the runtime values in the diagnosis. **13 words change, in `GrOp.dat` alone**, of 861 archives. **Dream Land goes 26 of 26 failing to 0 of 26** (items on, stage 28, all fighters; with items on it was worse than the 24/26 this row recorded). ctest 32/32, coverage unchanged at 79.50%. No `DWARF:` annotation is possible -- the struct lives in `groldpupupu.c`, not a header. **P-708's remaining stages are still open**; this one only surfaced because its spawn loop allocates. |
| P-771 | **Corneria** SIGSEGVs in `HSD_JObjAddAnim` for 10 of 26 fighters | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (`convert_extern_chains`, v109), `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.h` | P-759 matrix, `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=7`, 5 runs. Fighter-dependent on one stage, so it is an interaction: Falcon, DK, Mario, Marth, Zelda, Sheik, Falco, Y.Link, Dr.Mario, Roy. `HSD_JObjAddAnim` binding an animation to a joint tree points back at the same `Pl*` animation-descriptor gap as P-758's head item. **Diagnosed 2026-09-16, not fixed.** `HSD_JObjAddAnim(jobj, an_joint=0x80786884, mat_joint=0x18070600, sh_joint=0x1c070600)` <- `Item_80268BE0` (item.c:1056) <- `Item_80268D34` <- `Item_80268E5C` <- `it_802E7A4C` (itarwinglaser.c:340) <- `grCorneria_801DED50`. The first pointer is valid; the other two are **unrelocated big-endian values** -- `0x18070600` is the little-endian reading of the bytes `00 06 07 18`, i.e. raw data offset `0x60718`. So `ItemStateDesc.x4_matanim_joint` and `.x8_parameters` were never relocated while `.x0_anim_joint` in the same struct was. **That is the interesting part:** the relocation pass converts every field the table lists, independent of any walker, so a struct with one relocated and two unrelocated pointers means the runtime is reading an `ItemStateDesc` the table never described -- most likely `Item_80268E5C`'s `msid` indexing past the end of `item_data->xBC_itemStateContainer`, not a converter gap. Next: print `msid`, the container base and the state count at `item.c:1107`, and check whether the desc address is inside the array. Same arwing-laser path shows up on Venom (`g22`) and Corneria (`g7`). **Narrowed hard 2026-09-16, not fixed. Three of my earlier guesses are now ruled out.** Established: (1) `msid` is a **literal `type = 2`** passed from `grcorneria.c:1336` -> `it_802E72E0` -> `it_802E7A4C`, and `anim_id` comes from `it_803F8DE8`, a **static table in the executable** -- both legitimate, so this is not a bad index. (2) The article for kind 234 is at `GrCn.dat +0x60744`, reached correctly through `itemdata` (+0x5f5e4), so **P-762's path works and the article is the right one** -- `It_Kind_Old_Kuri` is 208, so 234 maps to `it_804A0F60[26]`, in range. (3) `xC_itemStates` is +0x606e4 with 0x10 entries, and index 2 is inside the six the converter walks, so **the state-count heuristic is not at fault either.** **The actual finding:** across the whole array, `desc[N]+0x00` **is** a relocation entry and `desc[N]+0x04` / `+0x08` **never are** -- and their values are always `own_offset + 0x10`, with one entry holding `{-1, -1}`. So on the console those fields read `0x00060718` and friends, which are **not valid addresses**; they cannot be ordinary pointers, and the port is not misconverting them. The crash is `HSD_DObjAddAnimAll(jobj->u.dobj, mat_joint->matanim, ...)` at jobj.c:316 with `jobj->u.dobj == NULL` but `mat_joint != NULL`, so the guard that saves the console is not `union_type_dobj`. **Leading hypothesis, unverified: these are archive *extern* references**, resolved at load against another archive's symbols (`HSD_ArchiveGetExternAddress`, archive.c:89-108), and the port never resolves them, so they keep raw table offsets. **Next step:** check `GrCn.dat`'s `nb_extern` and whether its extern table lists offsets `0x60708`/`0x6070c`; if it does, this is a **new bug class** -- unresolved externs -- and likely explains other item-animation crashes too. **SOLVED 2026-09-16 -- cause found, fix specified, handed to the P-758 session because it lives in `hsd_convert.c`. This is a bug *class*, not one crash.** The extern hypothesis was right. `HSD_ArchiveLocateExtern` (archive.c:96) patches a **linked list of sites**: each field holds the *data offset of the next field to patch*, terminated by `-1`, and the loop is `while (offset != -1U && offset < data_size) { next = *(u32*)(data + offset); *(u32*)(data + offset) = addr; offset = next; }`. `lbArchive_InitializeDAT` (lbarchive.c:27) runs it for every extern symbol with `addr = NULL`, so on console all these fields end up **NULL** and the `if (matanim_joint != NULL)` guards do their job. Measured in `GrCn.dat`, the chain is exactly that: `0x606e8 -> 0x606f8 -> 0x60708 -> 0x60718 -> 0x60728 -> 0xffffffff`, with a second parallel chain for `x8` at `0x606ec -> ... -> 0x6073c`. **Why the port breaks:** those chain links are plain numeric data -- not relocation entries, not reachable by any walker -- so the converter never byte-swaps them. `next` then reads `0x18070600` instead of `0x60718`, `offset < data_size` fails, and the walk **stops after the very first site**. Every later site keeps its raw link, which the game later dereferences as a pointer. **Fix (converter):** for each of the `nb_extern` entries, take `extern_info[i].offset` as the chain head and walk it in the data section byte-swapping each 4-byte link in place -- reading each link big-endian to find the next, stopping at `0xffffffff` or an out-of-range offset, with a visit cap so a corrupt chain cannot loop forever. The extern *table* is already swapped by `conv_header`; it is only the in-data chain that is missed. **Scope:** every archive with a non-zero `nb_extern` is affected, so this very likely also explains **P-775** and other item-animation crashes. Two dead ends already ruled out and recorded above: it is not a bad `msid`/`anim_id`, not the wrong article, and not the state-count heuristic.  **FIXED, converter v109 — `convert_extern_chains`.** The spec was right. Implemented as a pass straight after `convert_relocs`: for each of the `nb_extern` entries take `extern_info[i].offset` as the chain head, read each link **big-endian** to find the next, `conv_u32` it in place, stop at `-1`, out of range, a link that is somehow a relocation field, or the visit cap. Placed after `convert_relocs` on purpose so `c->reloc[]` is populated -- a link that is also a relocation field would already be host-order and reading it big-endian would give the wrong `next`. `c->num[]` doubles as the cycle guard, since a revisited site is already converted. **Scope, measured:** 25 of 852 archives carry externs, 556 symbols, **630 chain sites**, of which **74 were sites the port never reached**. 11 archives and 273 words change. **Verified, items on, 7 stages x 26 fighters = 182 runs: 10 failures -> 5, no stage worse.** `HSD_JObjAddAnim` is **gone from Corneria** (5 -> 3; p0-1 and p23-24 now pass, and the v109 failing set is a strict subset of v107's, deterministic over 3 repeats each). **Venom (stage 22) 3 -> 0, which closes P-775 as well** -- agent-a's handoff had already called it "the unwalked-article/animation-descriptor family, belongs to whoever holds `hsd_convert.c`", and this was it. The two Corneria runs still failing (p24-25, p9-10) now die *later*, in `GXSetVtxDesc` rather than `HSD_JObjAddAnim`, so they are a separate bug that this one was masking; p3-4 is unchanged and is the `HSD_JObjGetFlags` item (P-776). ctest 32/32, coverage unchanged at 79.50%. |
| P-772 | `lbvector.c:383` position sanity assert on **Zebes and Onett** | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/gr/grzebes.c.patch` | P-759 matrix, 3 runs: 6 fighters on `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=6` and `=9`. `pos3d->x>-50000.0F&&pos3d->x<50000.0F` -- something puts a fighter or article at an absurd coordinate, which is the classic unconverted-float signature. | **NOT fixed by the P-769 spline walker -- I claimed it was and I was wrong.** I verified with the default fighters, which pass on Zebes, and concluded the whole row was closed; the matrix count did not move (8 before, 8 after). It is **fighter-dependent**: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=0 P1=1` (Falcon/DK) passes on `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=6`, while `P0=12 P1=13` (Peach/Pikachu) and `P0=17 P1=18` (Yoshi/Zelda) both still assert. So the NaN here comes from the *fighter* side, not the stage spline. **Verify a stage fix across fighters, not just the default pair** -- that is the second time this session that reading a single pair gave the wrong answer. `pos3d->x` is still NaN rather than merely large, so it is the same NaN class, just a different source. **FIXED -- G-176, ninth instance, and the first a pointer bias could not fix.** Chased with two hardware watchpoints rather than guesses. The assert is on `fp->x890_cameraBox->pos.x`, but the camera is innocent: `prev_pos.x = -129.3` and `self_vel.x = -0.825` while `cur_pos.x = 1.02e14`, so the position was **written**, not integrated. Watchpoint on `cur_pos.x` -> `PSVECAdd` from `Fighter_procUpdate` (fighter.c:2389), which adds `mpGetSpeed`'s moving-platform delta. Watchpoint on the collision vertex -> `mpVtxSetPos(vtx_id=4, x=7.86e13)` from `grZebes_801D881C` (grzebes.c:630), and the vertex's own source `x0 = 2.60` with previous `x10 = 2`. **Cause:** `grZe_BubbleState { Vec3 positions[4]; grZe_BubbleEntry bubbles[20]; }` is cast from `&grZe_8049F140`, but that is `static Vec3 grZe_8049F140[2]` -- the struct reaches `grZe_8049F158` at +0x18 and `grZe_8049F170` at +0x30 purely by console linker adjacency. Under `-fdata-sections` `grZebes_UpdateCollisionColumns` read bubble entries out of unrelated data and fed the result to `mpVtxSetPos`. **Fix:** the struct spans *three* symbols, so the P-777 base-pointer bias does not work; instead the three statics become members of one object under `PORT_PC`, with `#define`s keeping their names, so every reference and every overlay cast still resolves and adjacency is guaranteed by the language. Console path untouched. **Verified: Zebes 26/26 fighters, GameCube 100.00% matched (1130/1130), ctest 32/32.** Note the same file has two more overlay casts (`grZe_BubbleSpawnPos` at lines ~527/539) that this same change also repairs.
| P-773 | **Icicle Mountain** hangs inside `HSD_Randi` for 4 fighters | open | — | — | P-759 matrix, 2 runs, `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=25`. A hang whose SIGALRM lands in `HSD_Randi` means a `while` loop rerolling forever -- a rejection-sampling loop whose acceptance condition can never be met, which usually means the data it tests against is wrong. |
| P-775 | Venom still SIGSEGVs in `grAnime_801C8138` for 7 of 26 fighters | open | — | — | Uncovered by fixing P-767: Venom went from 26 of 26 runs failing to 7, and the survivor is a different crash. `grAnime_801C8138` is the shared stage-animation helper (`gr/granime.c`, which already carries a `PORT_PC` patch), and the seven affected fighters are contiguous in the matrix (p14..p20 pairings), which suggests it depends on something per-fighter rather than on the stage alone. Check **P-774** first -- four unfixed cross-symbol overlays through `grVe_803E5348` are live in the same file. **Checked after P-774 (agent-a, 2026-09-15): the named crash is gone but the run still SIGSEGVs, further down.** `grAnime_801C8138` came from `grVenom_802053B0`'s `base[type + 0x7A]` garbage read -- P-774 fixed exactly that. The same repro now dies in the arwing-fire path: `HSD_JObjAddAnim+0xe3` <- `Item_80268D34` <- `Item_80268E5C` <- `it_802E7654` (the arwing-laser item spawn) <- `grVenom_80205F30` <- `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`. The PC is in real code (the in-process backtrace is trustworthy here, unlike P-767), and no `(Type*) &file-scope-symbol` arithmetic exists in the chain. **Verdict: not the symbol-adjacency class -- it is the unwalked-article/animation-descriptor family, i.e. an `hsd_convert.c` walker gap (agent-b's file), same shape as P-771's diagnosis. Do not fix in `patches/src/**`.** Stage-22 sweep on this build: 20/26 clean; 4 runs `HSD_JObjAddAnim` (this bug: p0-1, p11-12, p15-16, +1); 2 runs `HSD_DObjSetFlags` = **P-765**, not Venom-specific. | P-776 | Triage the G-176 sweep; fix remaining instances of the class | open | — | `patches/src/**` (candidates), `native/AI/**` | Brief B work, step 2 of the P-774 brief, stopped at the owner's request after P-774. The class grep ran but only the `sysdolphin/` half was captured; the `melee/` half must be re-run (expect `grcorneria.c` to mirror Venom -- `grVenom_80204F20`'s comments cite `grCn_803E214C`). First-pass candidates, in priority order: `hsd_3B5C.c:297..310/555/660` (`base = (u8*) &hsd_804D2E70`, then `((s32*) &base[0x818])[component] += dc` -- check `hsd_804D2E70`'s size vs 0x81C+); `synth.c:1379` (`(AXPBADPCMLOOP*)((u32*)&lbl_804C4540 ...`, read the whole expression); `hsd_393C.c:291..307` (`vi_base = (u8*)&HSD_VIData + i*0x60` -- fine iff HSD_VIData is a 0x60-record array); `hsd_3B34.c:11` (`HSD_804D2648_BUF` macro, audit every use); `debugconsole_main.c:2498` (debug tool, low priority). Ruled out: `hsd_3A76.c:507/509` (negative offsets from a *stack* local -- one function's locals stay adjacent on the host too), the `*(s32*)&scalar` bit-twiddling sites, `synth.c`'s `ratioHi` (G-098, already handled), `hsd_3A94.c`'s `(s32*) &cmd` (locals). Method: size each symbol from the release DWARF (`gdb -ex 'print sizeof(...)'`, P-745's recipe), compare against the largest offset used; also grep `decomp/src` for doc comments claiming adjacency (`back-to-back` etc. -- G-176's rule that such a comment is a defect report). Fix any instance the P-767/P-774 way: name the symbol under `PORT_PC`, arithmetic in the comment. Full state: `native/AI/handoffs/2026-09-15-P-774-venom-tables-and-G176-sweep.md`. |
| P-776 | `SIGSEGV in HSD_JObjGetFlags` on **Mr. Game & Watch**, several stages | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (theirs) | **SOLVED 2026-09-16, not fixed here: the fix is one walker in `hsd_convert.c`, which the P-758 session holds.** The row's original guess (Kirby's copy-ability hats) was wrong, and so was the title -- it is **G&W's own Turtle item**. Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=3 MELEE_MATCH_P1=4 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=7 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0`. Crash: `HSD_JObjGetFlags(jobj=0xb)` <- `it_8026EC54(gobj, arg1=1280, arg2="\4\7\13\16\21")` (itdraw.c:66) <- `it_8026EECC_inline_1` <- `it_8026EECC`. **`arg1 = 1280` is `0x0500`, i.e. the `u16` 5 never byte-swapped**, and `arg2` really does hold exactly five bone indices (04 07 0b 0e 11), so the loop runs 1280 times over a 5-entry list and indexes `ip->xBBC_dynamicBoneTable->bones[]` with garbage. **The unconverted object:** `itgamewatchturtle.c:36` takes `attr = item->xC4_article_data->x4_specialAttributes` and `Item_AttachGameWatchArticle` (it/kinds/inlines.h:71) passes **`attr[0]`** to `it_8027CE64` (itzako.c:732), which stores it in `item->xDD4_itemVar.gamewatch.attr`. `it_8026EECC_VARS` (itdraw.c:146) then reads it as `{ u16 x0; u8* x4; u16 x8; u8* xC; }` -- **two `{count, bone-index list}` pairs, 0x10 bytes.** Measured live (PlGw.dat, runtime 0x80921018): `00 05 | ptr | 00 05 | ptr`, both pointers correctly relocated, **both counts raw big-endian**. `conv_article_special_attrs` converts the attribute *block* as dense u32 but never follows `special[0]`, so this descriptor has no walker. **Fix:** follow `special[0]` for the G&W articles and `conv_u16` at +0x00 and +0x08 (the two pointers are relocation fields and need nothing). **Key it on the symbol name, not on shape**, the way `ft_x48_vis_lookup_slot` does for P-765: these articles come through `ftData->x48_items` with the item kind unknown (`-1`), and G&W's slots 0..9 are named in `ftGw_Init_OnLoad` (ftgamewatch.c:542-551). **`PlKb*` needs the same** -- `itkirbygamewatchchefpan.c:27` reaches the identical struct through Kirby's copy of the G&W article, which is why this first surfaced as "Kirby". If a shape check is wanted as a belt-and-braces guard, the signature is exact: `!reloc[s+0x00] && reloc[s+0x04] && !reloc[s+0x08] && reloc[s+0x0C]`, both big-endian `u16` in 1..64, and both byte arrays in range. Original (wrong) diagnosis kept for history: Uncovered by the P-765 fix. Kirby reaches the same fifth part-visibility group by a different route: `ftKb_SpecialN_800F14B4` (ftkirby.c:3752) does `lookup = (FtPartsVisLookup*) hat->hat_dynamics[3]; fp->x5AC.xC[4] = lookup;` -- so the array lives in a **copy-ability hat archive** (`PlKbCp*.dat`), not in `ftData->x48_items`, and the name-keyed slot fix for G&W does not reach it. `ftkirbyspecials.c:210` also reads `x48_items[4]` as `HSD_Joint**`, which is a second non-Article slot worth checking. Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=3 MELEE_MATCH_P1=4 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=4`. Related: **P-755** is also on the Kirby copy path.  **FIXED 2026-09-16, converter v124 (claude, opus-5).** `conv_gamewatch_vis_pair` follows `specialAttributes[0]` and converts the two `u16` counts. Keyed on shape, not on the fighter name: two counts that are not relocation fields alternating with two that are, both counts in 1..64, both byte lists in range -- which covers `PlGw.dat` and Kirby's copy in `PlKb.dat` without having to guess which archives carry it. Stages 4, 5, 7 and 11 with `p3-4` all run a clean 900; `HSD_JObjGetFlags` and `it_8026EC54` are both gone from the matrix. |
| P-778 | `itcoll.c:1050 assertion "0"` -- "item dynamics hit num over!", every Party Ball spawn | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (`conv_item_dynamics` + `conv_itcoll_dynamics`, v107) | **Owner-reported from live play, then reproduced: 81 of 754 matrix runs once items were enabled** (the full sweep; an earlier partial sample read 77). **Fully diagnosed; the fix is written out below.** `Article.x14_dynamics` is read through **two different structs**, and the on-disc object is the union of both -- 0x10 bytes, two `{count, descs}` pairs: `ItemDynamics { int count; BoneDynamicsDesc* dyn_descs; }` (it/types.h:145) and `ItCollDynamics { u8 _pad[8]; s32 count; ItCollDynamicsDesc* descs; }` (itcoll.c:72). **`ItCollDynamics::_pad[8]` *is* the `ItemDynamics` pair**, so its own count lives at **+0x08** and its descs at **+0x0C**. `conv_item_dynamics` walks only the first pair, so the second count stays big-endian -- measured at runtime on `It_Kind_Kusudama`: the four words read `0x1`, `0x80337c10`, **`0x01000000`**, `0x80337c28`, i.e. a 1 that never got swapped. `it_8027163C` compares it against 2 and asserts. **Fix:** bound with `in_data(c, off, 0x10)`, then after the existing first-pair walk add `conv_u32(c, off + 0x08)` and walk `rd32(c, off + 0x0C)` as `count` entries of **0x14** bytes (`ItCollDynamicsDesc { s32 bone_id; Vec3 offset; f32 size }` -- five 4-byte fields, no byte members), keeping the same `count > 0 && count <= 64` guard. The two desc arrays are different types *and* different sizes (0x18 vs 0x14), so they must be walked separately, not as one array. **Why the soak never found it:** `onEnterDebugVs` sets `item_freq = -1`, so every headless run spawned no items at all. `MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS` / `MELEE_SOAK_ITEMS` now exist for exactly this; with `MELEE_SOAK_ITEMS=4` it is the single largest failure on the board. **Items-on baseline, 2026-09-16: 137 of 754 fail (18.2%), eleven distinct**, against 59 of 754 with items off -- so the items-off figure was never the real denominator, it was the number with a third of the game switched off. Re-baseline with items on after this lands; the item article/collision/dynamics path has never been exercised headlessly and there is likely more behind this one.  **FIXED, converter v107.** The spec above was right field for field; two notes on landing it. **(1) It had to be a second walker, and `decomp_layout` is why.** `conv_item_dynamics` is annotated `/* DWARF: ItemDynamics */`, which is 8 bytes, so reading +0x08/+0x0C inside it failed the cross-check three times with "past the end of ItemDynamics (0x8)". The gate was right -- the object is not an `ItemDynamics`, it is two structs overlaid -- so the `ItCollDynamics` half is now `conv_itcoll_dynamics`, keeping the first half honestly checked and confining the unverifiable half. **It gets no annotation**: `ItCollDynamics` and `ItCollDynamicsDesc` are declared inside `itcoll.c`, not a header, so `dwarf_types.c` cannot include them; re-declaring them there would check my transcription against itself. `decomp_layout` floor unchanged at 51. The split is byte-identical to the inline version across 797 archives. **(2) Bounded by evidence, not by the 0x10 assumption.** `it_8027163C` casts unconditionally so the object is always 0x10 to the engine, but the second pair still requires the extent to reach 0x10 with nothing else starting inside (`next_pointed_at_after` **and** `next_public_after` -- see v106) and requires +0x0C to be a relocation field. The first pair keeps its own `in_data(c, off, 8)` so a short object at the end of the data section does not lose both halves. **Blast radius, measured:** exactly **2 of 861 archives change, 6 words** -- `ItCo.dat` and `ItCo.usd`, the same content twice. Per archive: `data+0x108` BE 5 -> 5 (`bone_id`), `data+0x118` BE `41000000` -> 8.0f (`size`), `data+0x124` BE 1 -> 1 (`ItCollDynamics.count`, the word that asserted). The three `offset` words are zero and so unchanged. **One item on the disc has ItCollDynamics data, and it is the Party Ball** -- which matches the report exactly. **Verified with items on**, 4 stages x 26 fighters = 104 runs: **17 failures -> 6**, and **zero `itcoll.c:1050` in any v107 run**. Stage 31 3/26 -> 0/26, stage 2 6/26 -> 0/26, stage 18 3/26 -> 0/26, stage 11 3/26 -> 1/26, stage 7 unchanged at 5/26 (the `HSD_JObjAddAnim` family, not this). ctest 32/32; coverage unchanged at 79.50% -- the second pair lives inside an already-counted descriptor, so this fixes data without moving the metric, which is the normal shape for a chain fix. **Re-baseline the items-on matrix (was 137/754).** |
| P-781 | `lbvector.c:383` position sanity survives on **Onett and Garden** -- a different cause from P-772 | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | — | **Diagnosed 2026-09-16, not fixed.** P-772's fix (Zebes' three overlaid statics) took this from 8 matrix runs to 5; the survivors are `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=9` (Onett) and `=12` (Garden) and are **not** the same bug. In P-772 the *fighter's* `cur_pos` was corrupted; here `cur_pos` is sane (21.88, 2.96) and the **camera box's `bone_pos`** is garbage in all three components. Chain: `ftCamera_UpdateCameraBox` (ftcamera.c:86) -> `ftLib_800866DC` -> `lb_8000B1CC(joint_for(camera_zoom_target_bone), &co_attrs.x170, v)`, which computes a joint's world position. **Both inputs are fine** -- bone index 5, offset `{0,0,0}` -- and the joint pointer is valid, so it is the joint's **matrix**: translation column `(-3.16e14, -1.55e14, -2.48e13)` and a rotation part out of unit range (`-4.19`). Walking the parent chain, the bad level is **one joint up: `translate.z = 1.02117042e+14`**, with *its* parent sane. The joint's own `translate`/`scale` at the failing level are fine, so the corruption is inherited from that animated parent transform. **So this is animation data, not stage data**, which puts it with the `Pl*` FObj work rather than with the G-176 overlay family. Observed on Jigglypuff. **Not a regression from the P-758 conversion work** -- it was 8 runs before that landed and 5 after, so it predates it. **Next step:** find what animates that parent joint's `translate.z` and whether its `HSD_FObjDesc` chain is still big-endian; note 1.02e14 is suspiciously close to the 1.02082682e+14 seen in P-772, so the two garbage values may share a source even though the delivery path differs.  **Traced to the bottom 2026-09-16 (claude, opus-5); not fixed, but the mechanism is now exact and the earlier guesses are ruled out.** Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=14 MELEE_MATCH_P1=15 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=9 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0`. The fighter is **Jigglypuff**; `camera_zoom_target_bone` is 5 and the bad transform is **one joint above it**. Chain, caught with `watch *(float*)<joint translate.z>`: `HSD_JObjSetTranslateZ(z=1.02117042e+14)` <- `JObjUpdateFunc(type=7)` <- `FObjUpdateAnim` <- `HSD_FObjInterpretAnim` <- `HSD_JObjAnim` <- `ftAnim_8006E054`. **The value is not data at all -- it is uninitialised stack.** `FObjUpdateAnim` (fobj.c:340) switches on `fobj->op_intrp` and its `default:` branch leaves `fobjdata` untouched, then calls `obj_update(obj, obj_type, &fobjdata)` regardless; at the crash `op_intrp == 8`, which is not a valid opcode (1..6), so the joint gets whatever was on the stack -- here a value whose bits are a host address. **Why `op_intrp` is 8:** the FObj's key stream is being misparsed. State at the crash: `length = 11`, `frac_value = frac_slope = 0x87` (= `HSD_A_FRAC_U8 | 7`, which is legal), `p0 = 1.5859375 = 0xcb/128`, `p1 = 1.1484375 = 0x93/128`, `fterm = 381`, `ad - ad_head = 12`. The 11 stream bytes at `ad_head` are `64 00 0a 00 00 cb 00 08 03 93 fd`. **Parsing them as stored does not reproduce that state** -- `0x64` decodes as `op = 4` (SPL, two bytes per key) with `nb_pack = 7`, which gives `p0 = p1 = 0`. **Parsing them with the first byte as `0x26` reproduces every observed field exactly**: `op = 6` (KEY), `nb_pack = 3`, three one-byte keys at idx1/3/5 with waits at idx2/4/6 (so `p1` ends as `0xcb`), then idx7 = `0x08` read as an opcode -> invalid -> `op_intrp = 8` on the next round, then idx8 = `0x03` (SPL0), `p1 = 0x93`, wait `fd 02` = 381, `ad = 12`. And `0x26050000` is **exactly** what `conv_waitanim_flags` (hsd_convert.c:3355) turns into the stored `0x000a0064` -- that transform is a bijection, so the pre-image is unique. So the first word of this FObj key stream has been through the `Fighter_WaitAnimData.x10_animCurrFlags` bit-order repacker. **What is still open, and it is the interesting part:** the stream lives in the fighter's shared animation buffer (`fp->x59C`, here `0x80e4aac0`), which `ftData_80085CD8` (ftdata.c:1785) re-fills by ARAM DMA on **every** motion-state change, and a watchpoint on the word shows the DMA as the writer -- so the mangling happened before the bytes reached ARAM, not in the converted copy the watchpoint sees. Two candidates: the AJ data was converted once with a `conv_ft_data` WaitAnimData walk overrunning its array (the array length is a heuristic -- `count = (end - start) / 0x18` bounded by the nearest later `ftData` pointer field -- and the in-place comment already records P-652, where exactly this overran into the part-animation `x8` arrays), or the same buffer is converted twice. **Suggested fix regardless of which:** clamp the WaitAnimData count with `next_pointed_at_after(c, start)` (and `next_public_after`) as well, the way the part-animation table immediately above it already is -- an FObj `ad` stream is a relocation target, so that bound lands exactly at its first byte and the overrunning entry falls outside. `length = 11` also looks one key too long for three `KEY` packs (7 bytes), which is consistent with the same overrun having touched the `HSD_FObjDesc` as well. Lives in `hsd_convert.c`, which the P-758 session holds. **Two dead ends closed 2026-09-16 (claude, opus-5); claim released without a fix, and the row's own suggested fix is disproved.** (1) **The `next_pointed_at_after` clamp on the WaitAnimData walk would be a no-op.** The diagnosis above says `conv_ft_data`'s xC/x14 walk overruns into an FObj key stream and puts its first word through `conv_waitanim_flags`. Instrumented `conv_waitanim_flags` to report every call landing on a relocation target -- an FObj `ad` stream is a relocation target, so an overrun onto one is exactly detectable -- and swept all 861 archives: **zero hits**. The predicted pre-image word `0x26050000` never occurs in any archive, and never occurs at runtime on the repro either. The arithmetic in the diagnosis is right (`conv_waitanim_flags(0x26050000) == 0x000A0064`, and stored little-endian that is the observed `64 00 0a 00`), so the *transform* is identified correctly; what is wrong is the claim that `conv_ft_data` is what applied it. Whatever applies it, it is not that walk. (2) **The repro is ASLR-flaky and every measurement in this row needs re-reading in that light.** Same pinned seed, same `MELEE_MATCH_*`: it crashed on the first 3 runs and then ran clean 25 consecutive times. Fresh conversion (`MELEE_NO_ASSET_CACHE=1`) is deterministic, so the variance is not in the asset bytes -- it is the runtime. That fits the one mechanism the row does establish: `FObjUpdateAnim`'s `default:` branch (fobj.c:383) leaves `HSD_ObjData fobjdata` uninitialised and calls `obj_update` with it anyway. On PowerPC a leftover stack word is a `0x8xxxxxxx` address, which reinterpreted as a float is a harmless denormal; on 32-bit x86 it is a PIE address around `0x56dxxxxx`, which as a float is ~1e14 -- and `0x56D...` is exactly the reported `1.02117042e+14`. ASLR is what makes the bug come and go. **Do not reproduce this under gdb**: gdb sets `disable-randomization on` by default, which pins the stack layout and hides it -- that is why it never fired in ~10 gdb reproductions here. Use `setarch -R` deliberately when you want a pinned repro, and leave ASLR on when you want to *find* this class. **Caveat on the clean runs:** the shared checkout had another agent's uncommitted edits to `ft/ftaction.c` and `it/itanimlist.c` land mid-measurement, so the 25 clean runs are not cleanly attributable to HEAD. Re-measure on a quiet tree before concluding P-781 is gone. **Next step for whoever takes it:** instrument the `default:` branch itself (env-gated `OSReport` of `op_intrp`, `op`, `nb_pack` and the raw stack word) and loop the repro with ASLR on until it fires; a clean run never enters that branch at all, so a single hit names both the bad opcode and the animation that produced it.  **FIXED and MEASURED 2026-09-16.** The mechanism is the other session's, not mine: my filed theory (a `conv_ft_data` WaitAnimData overrun) was **disproved** by instrumenting `conv_waitanim_flags` to flag any call landing on a relocation target across all 861 archives -- zero hits, and the predicted pre-image word occurs nowhere. The real cause is in the original code. `FObjUpdateAnim`'s `default:` arm (fobj.c:384) hands `obj_update` an **uninitialised local**; `op_intrp` is legitimately outside the handled set because `HSD_A_OP_NONE` is 0, so the arm is reached in ordinary play. On PowerPC the leftover stack word is `0x8xxxxxxx`, a denormal near zero that moves a joint by nothing; on 32-bit x86 it is a PIE address near `0x56xxxxxx`, **which as a float is ~1e14** -- the `1.02117042e+14` this row recorded from the start.
