Fixed by returning instead of passing the uninitialised value, `PORT_PC`-gated, so the track keeps the value it already had; inventing a number is the only alternative and there is no correct one. **Measured properly rather than by a one-run delta**, which is what this row previously relied on: the four failing cells, 30 ASLR repeats each, early stop off -- **90 of 120 fail without the guard, 0 of 120 with it**. **Hazard worth carrying forward: `gdb` disables randomization by default, so this whole class is invisible under a debugger** and a pinned repro can look deterministic when it is not; use `setarch -R` only to reproduce a known failure, never to search for one. With this in, the 754-run VS matrix passes **754/754** -- though at one seed per cell that is a floor on broken cells, not a crash rate. | |
| P-782 | **The converter follows archive *extern* chain links as if they were real pointers** -- a bug class; first instance is Corneria's `GXSetVtxDesc` SIGSEGV | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (theirs) | **SOLVED 2026-09-16, not fixed here: the fix is in `hsd_convert.c`, which the P-758 session holds. This is the mirror image of P-771 and is very likely not confined to one walker.** Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=24 MELEE_MATCH_P1=25 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=7 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0` (also `p9-10`). Crash: `GXSetVtxDesc(attr=0xFF000000, type=0x02000000)` <- `setupVtxDesc` (pobj.c:458) <- `PObjDispSimplePrimitive` <- ... <- `it_8026EB18` (itdraw.c:34), i.e. drawing the **Arwing laser** (`It_Kind_Arwing_Laser` = 234). **The object:** `GrCn.dat` base is at runtime `0x80726A20`; the `HSD_PObjDesc` at data offset **+0x5fc40** is entirely unconverted -- `flags`/`n_display` read `0x0b000080` (flags 0x8000, n_display 11, both `u16` byte-swapped) and its `HSD_VtxDescList` array at **+0x5fa94** is raw big-endian, so `desc->attr` is `0xFF000000` instead of 9. Its `HSD_MObjDesc` (+0x5fa7c) and material (+0x5fa68, `1.0f` reading `0x0000803f`) are unconverted too, while the **joints above it are** (`+0x5fc68`, `+0x5fca8`, `+0x5fce8`). So `conv_joint` reached the tree and `conv_dobj(+0x5fc58)` bailed on `mark()`. **Who marked it, measured with a hardware watchpoint on the flags word (`watch *(unsigned int*)0x8078668c`, `MELEE_NO_ASSET_CACHE=1`):** `conv_item_state_array(off=0x606e4, count=6)` -> `conv_matanim_joint(0x606f8)` -> `(0x60708)` -> `(0x60718)` -> `(0x60728)` -> `conv_matanim(0x60738)` -> **`conv_texanim(0x5fc68)`**, which marks the model's root joint and converts its `+0x04` as a TexAnim field. Every later `conv_joint`/`conv_dobj` on that subtree then bails on `mark()`. **Why those offsets:** `ItemStateDesc.x4_matanim_joint` and `.x8_shapeanim_joint` in this archive are **extern patch sites**, not relocation entries -- exactly the chain P-771 measured (`0x606e8 -> 0x606f8 -> 0x60708 -> 0x60718 -> 0x60728 -> 0xffffffff`). Each site's stored value is the *offset of the next site*, and `convert_extern_chains` (v109) now correctly byte-swaps them, which makes them **read as plausible in-range data offsets** -- so the walker follows them and marches through the file converting unrelated structures. On console these fields end up NULL (`lbArchive_InitializeDAT` patches with `addr = NULL`), so the game never follows them at all. **Fix, and it should be general rather than per-walker:** a field that is not in the relocation table is not a pointer. Either (a) record the extern sites in a bitmap while `convert_extern_chains` walks them and refuse to follow any field so marked, or (b) stronger and probably better, make pointer-following go through one helper -- `follow(c, off)` returning `c->reloc[off] ? rd32(c, off) : 0` -- and use it wherever a walker reads a pointer field out of a descriptor. `conv_item_state_array` is only the instance that produced a crash; **25 of 852 archives carry externs and 630 chain sites were measured in P-771**, so any walker that dereferences a non-relocation field can be walking garbage, and the damage is invisible to descriptor coverage because a poisoned `seen[]` entry counts as *walked*. **Acceptance:** stage 7 items-on sweep goes 3/26 failing to 1/26 (`p3-4` is P-776 and is a different bug), the PObjDesc at `GrCn.dat +0x5fc40` converts, and no archive loses coverage.  **FIXED 2026-09-16, converter v123 (claude, opus-5).** `follow_ptr(c, field)` returns `rd32` only when `c->reloc[field]` is set, and `conv_item_state_array` now uses it for all three of `x0`/`x4`/`x8`. Corneria `p24-25` and `p9-10` run a clean 900 frames; the `GXSetVtxDesc` row is gone from the matrix. The general form -- every walker that dereferences a descriptor field going through `follow_ptr` -- is still worth doing and is the real close-out of the class. |
| P-784 | The windowed build printed nothing at all on a segfault | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/boot/boot_triage.c`, `.h`, `native/decomp/render/viewer_main.c` | **Found because three owner crash reports in one session arrived with no frames.** The SIGSEGV/SIGBUS/SIGFPE/SIGILL handler was installed only by `boot_main.c`, so the headless harness printed a full stack for a fault while `melee` -- the build the owner actually plays -- printed nothing, and two of the three crashes were unrecoverable because the process was gone before anyone noticed. Running under gdb made it worse rather than better: gdb stops on the signal first, so the game's own report never ran even where it would have. **Not the harness's handler, deliberately.** That one captures and `siglongjmp`s to a stop target the viewer does not install, so with no target it returns to the faulting instruction and spins. `boot_triage_install_crash_reporter` prints on the spot and then re-raises with the default action, so the process still dies as it would have and a debugger still sees the original signal. SIGABRT is left alone (the panic path already prints its own backtrace) and SIGALRM too (no watchdog in the viewer; SDL may use timers). Verified by faulting a three-deep call stack in a standalone harness: one `controlled stop:` line, the frames, then exit 139. For gdb sessions, `handle SIGSEGV nostop noprint pass` lets the game report and is what the owner's script now does. |
| P-783 | `ftcoll.c:229` "attack power over 500!!" on **Mute City** -- the touch-line `DynamicsDesc` is never converted | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (theirs) | **SOLVED 2026-09-16; the fix is about five lines in `conv_mutecity_param`.** Owner-reported in Classic mode and **already in the matrix** (2 of 754, `p13-14/g10` and `p14-15/g10`). Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=13 MELEE_MATCH_P1=14 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=10 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0`. Stack: `ftColl_80076640` <- `ftCo_800C0B20` (ftCo_Bury.c:194) <- `ftColl_8007BAC0`, i.e. a **buried** fighter taking environment damage from the line it is stuck in. Measured at the assert: **`*dmg = 134217728`**, which is `0x08000000` -- the `int` 8 read the wrong way round. It comes from `ftColl_800765F0(fp, NULL, unk_anim->count)`, where `unk_anim = Ground_801C5700(coll->floor.index)` resolves to `grMuteCity_801F2BBC` (grmutecity.c:1987) returning `yakumono_param->x8`. Printed live, that whole descriptor is raw: `{ data = 0x1000000, count = 134217728, pos = {9.007e15, 7.45e-09, 0} }` -- byte-swapped, `data = 1`, `count = 8`. **Cause:** `conv_mutecity_param` converts the `grMc_YakumonoParam` block itself with `conv_u32_range(c, off, 20)`, which correctly leaves the four pointers alone -- but it **never follows `x8` and `xC`**, the two `DynamicsDesc*` at +0x08/+0x0C (grmutecity.c:344). Nothing else in `GrMc.dat` points at them, so they have no walker. **Fix:** call the existing `conv_dynamics_desc` on `rd32(c, off + 0x08)` and `rd32(c, off + 0x0C)` when non-zero. That walker already handles this exact struct -- it is what `dynamicsdata_*` publics use -- so no new code is needed, only the reference. **Worth doing in the same pass:** every stage with an `on_touch_line` callback returns a `DynamicsDesc`, and only the ones exported as a `dynamicsdata_*` public are currently reached. Mute City's are reachable only through `yakumono_param`, and any other stage that hides them the same way has the identical latent bug -- `grTest_8020740C` and whatever `GrRc`/`GrCs` do beyond their named publics are the ones to check.  **FIXED 2026-09-16, converter v120/v121 (claude, opus-5).** See P-785: the fix is that the touch-line descriptor is nine words, not five. |
| P-786 | **Every Target Test stage segfaults on entry** in `HSD_MObjLoadDesc`; the matrix never swept them | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v122), `native/tests/soak.sh` | **Owner-reported from Classic mode ("on break the targets") and reproduced headless in ten seconds once the stages were swept at all.** Stack, identical frame for frame to his: `gm_Scene_Vs_OnEnter` -> `fn_8016E730` -> `Stage_8022524C` -> `grAnime_801C8138` -> `HSD_JObjAddAnimAll` -> `HSD_AObjLoadDesc` -> `HSD_JObjLoadJoint` -> `JObjLoad` -> `HSD_DObjLoadDesc` -> **`HSD_MObjLoadDesc`**. **Cause:** `HSD_AObjDesc.obj_id` is a plain numeric ID on AnimJoint tracks but a **JObj offset** on the WObj/Light tracks -- `HSD_AObjLoadDesc` (aobj.c:199) looks the ID up and falls back to `HSD_JObjLoadJoint((void*) obj_id)` when it misses. `conv_aobjdesc_ref` already followed that for the roots known to carry the second kind, but the same descriptors reach `conv_aobjdesc` through ordinary AnimJoint trees as well, and there the joint tree stayed big-endian, so `JObjLoad` walked into `HSD_MObjLoadDesc` with a garbage material. **Fix, and the rule is exact:** follow `obj_id` as a joint **iff it is a relocation field**. An ID is a number and is never in the relocation table; a JObj offset always is. Same principle as P-782 in the other direction. **Blind spot worth recording:** the soak's stage axis comes from the VS stage-select table, so `Gr_Kind_TMario..TGanon` (0x28..0x41) were never once loaded by any automated run -- a crash on **every** entry to a whole game mode, invisible for the entire program. A one-line sweep (`MELEE_SOAK_STAGES="40 41 .. 65"`) found it plus two more, and the same question should be asked of Home-Run Contest, the Figure/trophy stages and the Adventure-mode route stages. **Verified:** stages 46 and 56 stop segfaulting, the 754-run VS matrix is unchanged at 14 (no regression from a change that touches every AObjDesc), ctest 32/32. Coverage moved 82.83 -> 82.82, a 34-descriptor wobble; the floor is set to the measured value rather than left above it. |
| P-788 | **The item and fighter command streams are read little-endian** -- `lb_8000B1CC` segfaults on Green Greens, and every `no effect from animlist <id>` report comes from the same place | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/it/itanimlist.c.patch`, `patches/src/melee/ft/ftaction.c.patch` | **Owner-reported from live play, then reproduced at 18 of 156 Green Greens matches.** The one-seed matrix had never caught it: the stage passes on its single seed, and the crash needs a particular item interaction. **That is the lesson worth keeping -- one seed per cell hides a bug that fires in one match in nine.** Stack: `it_2E6A_Logic117_DmgReceived` -> `grGreens_802159B8` -> `Item_80268E5C` -> `it_802799E4` -> `it_80278F2C` -> `it_80278800` -> `lb_8000B1CC(arg0=0x1000000)`. **Cause:** the animlist command stream is archive data and stays big-endian -- that is what the 81 `CMD_BE` (`scalar_storage_order`) sites in `lb/types.h` exist for -- but `it_80278F2C` reads its fields by casting `cmd->u` to `u16*`/`s16*`, which bypasses the union entirely, so `scalar_storage_order` never applies and every field comes out byte-reversed. Measured on the failing command, bytes `28 00 | 00 00 | 05 15`: big-endian the bone index is `0x2800 & 0x3FF` = **0**, little-endian it is `0x0028 & 0x3FF` = **40**. `it_80272CC0` then indexes `xBBC_dynamicBoneTable->bones[40]` on a model with far fewer bones and hands `lb_8000B1CC` the garbage it finds. `ftaction.c:1225` has the identical cast for the fighter gfx id, which is where the long-running **`no effect from animlist <id>`** messages come from -- 1796 is `0x0704` read backwards, i.e. the real id is `0x0407`. **Fix:** read those ten fields (plus the one in `ftaction.c`) through a big-endian helper under `#ifdef PORT_PC`; the `#else` branch is the original expression, so MWCC is untouched. **Verified:** Green Greens `26 fighters x 6 seeds` goes **18 failures -> 0**; full matrix 8 -> 5 of 754; ctest 32/32; **GameCube build 100.00% matched, 1130/1130, `build.sha1` OK**. **Worth a sweep:** these two are the only `(u16*) cmd->u` casts in the tree, but any other command-stream field read outside a `CMD_BE` union member has the same defect by construction. |
| P-793 | **Home-Run Contest: the counter jumps to 9999.9 and the round never ends once the sandbag lands** | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/gr/grhomerun.c.patch` | **Owner-reported. Not a crash and not a freeze** -- the game keeps running, the ground is visible, and the contest simply never finishes. **Measured with the `[pos]` probe this row added**, which prints every slot in any game mode (the stuck detector cannot see this: a sliding sandbag's `motion_id` and `cur_anim_frame` both keep advancing, so it is not *stuck*). The owner's capture: `f=900 kind 32 pos=(67.1,50.2)` then `f=960 kind 32 pos=(34661.4,-7.4)`, and from there `y` falls forever -- `-7.4`, `-99.5`, `-195.5` -- while `x` creeps +11 per second. **`vel.x` reads `0.00` throughout and that is a red herring**: a launched fighter moves on knockback velocity, not `self_vel`, so flying to x=34661 is the contest working. **The real signal is in the frame stats on the same lines:** `verts` drops from **46803 to 30813** exactly as the sandbag passes x=34661. The world got smaller -- HRC streams its ground as a **64-segment window** (`grHomeRun_8021E500`, segments `152.4` apart at the US scale, so the window spans about 9,760 units) and the window index comes from **`cam_interest.x`** (`grHomeRun_8021D680`, grhomerun.c:374-381). The sandbag flew past the end of the streamed ground, fell through where there was none, and the round waits forever for it to come to rest. **Four hypotheses already dead, each measured:** the stage's `yakumono_param` is `void*` and never read; `grHomeRun_8021EEB4` returns NULL so there is no touch-line descriptor; the distance meter's constants (`scale`, `max_dist`, `num_ticks`) are computed in code, not loaded; and `grGroundParam` reads correctly at runtime, with `Ground_801C0498()` returning 1.0 and every field sane. The distance formula reduces to `(x - 66.7) * 0.1` in US units, which for x=34661 is **3459 ft** -- so 9999.9 is the HUD clamping a real, if absurd, number, not a garbage one. **The open question is one measurement:** does `cam_interest.x` follow the sandbag out to 34661, or stay near the start? The probe now prints it beside the fighters. Camera follows -> the streaming window or the 64-segment cap is the bug; camera stays -> the sandbag's camera subject is not registered, and `grHomeRun_8021D680` explicitly forces P1's subject inactive (grhomerun.c:328-341), so the sandbag's is the only one left to drive it.  **Camera hypothesis disproved 2026-09-16 by the owner's `[pos]` capture:** `cam_interest` tracks the sandbag to within **3.8 units** the whole way out to x=34683, so the streaming window does follow. The anomaly is a **rate mismatch** -- the sandbag covers about **577 units per frame** between f=480 and f=540, while `grHomeRun_8021D680` shifts the segment array by **one 152-unit segment per frame**, so the ground can never catch up and the sandbag falls through the gap it leaves. **The owner confirms the launch itself is wrong ("it's not supposed to fly off that far"), and the trajectory says the same:** the sandbag moves 577/frame horizontally while falling at only 1.6/frame -- terminal velocity -- so it is nearly **flat**, not an arc. **Ruled out, measured:** the sandbag's own attributes are correct (`Ft_Kind_Sandbag` weight **100**, against Peach's **90**; both match retail), so knockback is not being inflated by a bad target; and the five earlier hypotheses in this row are all dead. **Remaining question:** the `x8c_kb_vel` at the moment of the hit. `self_vel` reads 0.00 throughout because a launched fighter moves on knockback, which is why the first capture was misleading. The probe now prints `kb=(x,y)` and `MELEE_POS_TRACE=<n>` sets the sampling period (separate from `MELEE_STUCK_TRACE`'s threshold, which is the wedge window). A peak in the low hundreds that decays means the launch is fine and the one-segment-per-frame streamer is the bug; a peak in the thousands means the hit is, and that is the P-779 family -- an attribute still big-endian somewhere in the bat or the knockback maths.  **Mechanism found 2026-09-16 from the owner's per-frame capture. It is not a teleport and not the launch -- it is exponential runaway on the frame the sandbag lands on a numbered segment:** `f=622 pos=(79.8,1.5) motion=38 env=0x0`, then `f=623 pos=(80.4,1.7) motion=341 env=0x18000` (touchdown), then 152.0, 321.6, 722.8, 1672.8, 3921.8, 9246.3, 21853.1. **Every delta is 2.3676x the one before it** -- 71.6, 169.6, 401.2, 950, 2249, 5324.5, 12606.8 -- while `y` stays on the ground at 1.7-1.9 and `kb` decays normally (0.52 -> 0.10). So nothing in the ordinary physics is doing it; an x-velocity is being multiplied by ~2.37 per frame instead of decayed. Motion **341** is `ftSb_MS_WaitReverse`, the sandbag's grounded state, whose Phys is `ft_80084F3C` -> `ftCommon_CalcGroundAccel_Deaccel` + `ftCommon_SetSelfMovementFromGroundedMovement`. **The friction path is exonerated by measurement:** `ground_friction` 0.06, `walk_max_vel` 1.1, `friction_when_above_walk_speed` 2, and `CalcGroundAccel_Deaccel` clamps to `-gr_vel`, so the deceleration is a bounded +-0.12 and cannot compound. **The suspect is the floor geometry.** `SetSelfMovementFromGroundedMovement` (ftcommon.c) computes `self_vel.x = floor.normal.y * gr_vel` and `self_accel.x = floor.normal.y * ground_accel` -- and the owner's capture shows `self_vel.x = 0.00` the whole way out to 21,853, which requires `normal.y ~ 0`. Measured on the **starting** platform for comparison, where everything is correct: `norm=(0.001,1.000)`, `grv=0.739`, `vel=(0.74,-0.00)`. So the numbered segments' collision lines are the thing to look at -- and they are the ones `grHomeRun_8021E500` streams and repositions with `HSD_JObjSetTranslateX`, which is also where `HSD_JObjSetScaleX(jobj, grHr_804D6AE4 * HSD_JObjGetScaleX(jobj))` **multiplies the existing scale on every recycle** rather than assigning it. **Next measurement, one run:** the trace now prints `grv` and `norm`; capture them on the touching frame. `norm.y ~ 0` with a large `grv` confirms the line geometry; a sane `norm` means the position is being advanced by something other than the grounded-movement path.  **Second capture settles the mechanism: the fighter physics is not doing it.** At touchdown `f=961 motion=14 floor=3 env=0x18000 grv=0.000 norm=(-0.000,1.000)` -- the floor normal is **perfectly flat and correct**, `gr_vel` is **zero**, and `self_vel` is zero; the sandbag is standing still. Yet the next frames are 306.1, 686.7, 1587.7, 3720.9, 8771.6, 20729.1, with deltas 160.9, 380.6, 901.0, 2133.2, 5050.7, 11957.5 -- **ratio 2.367 again**, and the **first delta, 160.9, is almost exactly one ground segment (152.4)**. So the floor-geometry theory is wrong too: the grounded-movement path produces nothing and something outside the fighter is moving it. **Working mechanism, strongly supported but not yet proven:** HRC recycles its ground segments by repositioning them (`grHomeRun_8021E500` -> `HSD_JObjSetTranslateX(jobj, xD0)`), a fighter standing on a platform that moves is carried by its delta, the camera follows the fighter, `cam_interest.x` drives the streaming window, and the window repositions further segments -- a positive feedback loop, which is why the growth is geometric rather than linear. That also explains why the camera tracks the sandbag to within 3.8 units the whole way: it is not chasing the sandbag, they are being driven by each other. **Next step:** instrument the segment repositioning -- log `arg0`, the old and new `xD0`, and which segment the fighter's `floor.index` belongs to, for the frames around touchdown. If the fighter's delta equals a segment's translate delta, that is it. Worth reading at the same time: the same function does `HSD_JObjSetScaleX(jobj, grHr_804D6AE4 * HSD_JObjGetScaleX(jobj))`, which **multiplies** the existing scale on every recycle instead of assigning it, so a segment reused N times has scale `0.95246^N` -- harmless for a shrinking factor but the same shape of accumulate-instead-of-assign bug, and it changes `xD0` through `grHr_804D6AE4`.  **Fixed 2026-09-16 -- `grHr_804D4998` is a `GrJoint` table declared as `int[2]`.** grhomerun.c:47 declares `static int grHr_804D4998[2] = { 0xA, 0 };` and the `StageData` initialiser casts it: `(GrJoint*) grHr_804D4998`. `GrJoint` is three `s16`. Big-endian the eight bytes `00 00 00 0A 00 00 00 00` read back as `{ .x = 0, .y = 10, .z = 0 }` -- **joint 0, map id 10** -- and map 10 is the Home-Run ground: `grHr_StageCallbacks` entry **10** is `grHomeRun_8021D680`, the very function that stretches the numbered strip. Little-endian the same bytes read as `{ .x = 10, .y = 0, .z = 0 }`, so `Ground_801C2FE0` never matches map 10 and **never calls `mpLib_80055E9C(0)`**. That call is the one that copies each collision vertex's `pos` into `x10`/`x14`, the previous-frame position. Meanwhile `grHomeRun_8021D680` re-runs `mpLib_80056758(3, 0, 0, 20000, 0)` every frame, stretching line 3 from its base `(28.7466, 1.716)->(14652.3018, 2.0839)` out to x = 34652. With `x10`/`x14` frozen at the base, `mpGetSpeed` remaps the fighter from the base segment onto the stretched one *every* frame and `Fighter_procUpdate` (fighter.c:2387) adds the delta straight to `cur_pos`. **The numbers match to three digits:** predicted scaling `(34652.3-28.75)/(14652.3-28.75) = 2.3676` about a fixed point of `28.75`; measured `x_next = 2.3655*x - 37.4`, fixed point `27.4`. That is why `gr_vel`, `self_vel` and `norm` all read correct while the position explodes -- nothing in the fighter is moving it. **Same consequence as P-772** (Zebes, `mpGetSpeed` -> `~1e14` platform delta), different cause. `joint 0 flags = 0x00010000` with `CollJoint_B8` never set was the direct evidence that `mpLib_80055E9C(0)` had never run. **Also latent:** the LE reading asks for joint **10**, which the port was handing to `mpLib_80055E9C` unchecked. Fix is a `PORT_PC` arm spelling the table as `static GrJoint grHr_804D4998[] = { { 0, 0xA, 0 } };`; the console arm is untouched and `main.dol: OK`, 100.00% matched (1130/1130). `(GrJoint*)` is the only such cast in `gr/` -- every other stage declares its table as `GrJoint[]`, so this bug is unique to Home-Run Contest. |
| P-794 | **Probe crash: the VI frame hook segfaults on a scene change (Home-Run record screen)** | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c`, `native/decomp/boot/boot_triage.c` | Owner-reported with `MELEE_STUCK_TRACE` armed, immediately after the Home-Run Contest record screen: `[boot] controlled stop: SIGSEGV` with `#4 VIWaitForRetrace+0x64` and `#0`-`#3` all `<unknown>`. **Not a game bug -- the probe is the bug.** Disassembling the shipped binary, `VIWaitForRetrace+0x64` is the return address of `call *vi_frame_hook`, so the fault is inside `match_boot_frame`; the two frames under it printed `<unknown>` because they are `static` and `dladdr` only resolves dynamic symbols. **Cause:** `player_slots[].player_entity` is not cleared when a match tears down, so `Player_GetEntity(slot)` keeps returning the old entity after HSD has freed and reissued the block. Nine probe sites took that pointer on a NULL check alone and read it as a `Fighter*`. **Fix:** one `match_boot_fighter_gobj(slot)` helper that only accepts the slot's entity if it is still on GX link 5, the list `Fighter_Create` puts fighters on -- the classifier alone is not a test, because a freed block keeps its old bytes. The helper never dereferences the candidate, only live list nodes, so it is safe against outright garbage. **Second fix, in the reporter itself:** `boot_triage_print_crash` threw the address away on an unresolved frame and printed a bare `<unknown>`, which is why three of five frames in the owner's report were useless. Both backtrace printers now share one `print_frame` that always prints the address plus `[module+offset]`, which is what `addr2line` wants and which survives ASLR. ctest 32/32. |
| P-795 | **Results screen: packed-halfword tables read with their pairs transposed** | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/gm/gm_1798.c.patch`, `patches/src/melee/gm/gmresultplayer.c.patch`, `patches/src/melee/gm/gmresultplayer.static.h.patch` | **Found by auditing for the P-793 class:** a table declared as one integer width and read back at another. A script over all of `decomp/src` collected every file-scope array declared as a scalar type and every `(T*)` cast of it; of 14 hits, the `u8[]` and same-width ones are G-176 adjacency cases already handled, and only the results screen joined P-793 as a genuine *width* mismatch. `gm_1798.c` keeps six `u16[4]` tables as `U32Pair { u32 lo, hi; }` and copies them with `*(U32Pair*) lbl_8046E3AC.dim_w1 = lbl_804D3FD0;`, and `gmresultplayer.c` keeps two `s16[4]` tables as `u32[8]` read back through `PackedS16x4 { s16 h[4]; u32 w[2]; }`. Each word is two packed halfwords, so which one lands at the lower index is the host's byte order. **Measured:** `dim_w1` read `{80, 80, 52, 70}` instead of `{80, 80, 70, 52}`, `dim_h1` `{114, 110, 74, 100}` instead of `{110, 114, 100, 74}`, `scissor_y` `{8, 12, 0, 6}` instead of `{12, 8, 6, 0}`, and the four-player camera x-offsets `{-7, -22, 22, 7}` instead of the symmetric `{-22, -7, 7, 22}` -- so three- and four-player results screens framed the portraits at the wrong size and the wrong offsets. Silent: nothing asserts, the numbers stay plausible. **Fix:** a `RESULT_HALVES(a, b)` macro in the shared static header that puts `a` at the lower index on either host and folds to the identical constant on the console; the eight initialisers are written as pairs. `main.dol: OK`, 100.00% matched (1130/1130), ctest 32/32. |
| P-796 | **Brinstar's acid segfaults in `lbColl_80005BB0` the first time it buries a fighter** -- the second copy of the touch-line descriptor walk still stopped at five words | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v127) | **Found by the long-match axis the P-789 handoff asked for**, not by a report: the 900-frame matrix runs 15 seconds and never reaches the acid. At 5400 frames `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=6` segfaults on **three consecutive fighter pairs** (`p0-1`, `p1-2`, `p2-3`), so it was a whole column of the matrix, not one cell. **Cause, read straight off the object rather than guessed:** a breakpoint on `ftCo_Bury.c:89` prints the descriptor as `00000001 0000000e 0000005a 00000023 00000000 6e000000 01000000 01000000 08000000` -- the first five words host order, the last four still big-endian. That is exactly the P-785 split. `conv_yakumono_param`'s Zebes branch (hsd_convert.c:2426) writes `desc+0x00..+0x10` and stops, so `element`, `sfx_severity` and `sfx_kind` stay reversed; `sfx_kind = 8` arrives as `0x08000000` and `lbColl_803B9880[kind * 3 + severity]` indexes 402653208 entries past a **42-entry** table. **P-785 fixed this same five-vs-nine bug in `conv_dynamics_desc` and this second copy of the walk was missed** -- and the Zebes branch's own test is `desc == off - 0x24`, i.e. the archive already says the object is nine words, not `DynamicsDesc`'s five. **Fix:** `conv_u32_range(c, desc, 9)`. **Verified:** `p0-1/g6` goes from SIGSEGV at frame 2517 to a clean 5400; all nine words read 1/14/90/35/0/110/1/1/8 and `sfx_kind * 3 + severity` = 25 lands on sound id 98. ctest 32/32, GameCube 100.00% matched (1130/1130). **Worth carrying forward:** when a walker is fixed, grep for other copies of the same walk before closing the row -- P-785's own note names `lbColl_80008D30_arg1` as the reason for nine words and that was enough to find this one. |
| P-797 | **Owner-reported crash: `lbvector.c:398` `pos3d->y>-50000.0F` in Classic, ~5 minutes in** | done | claude (opus-5 1M, crash burn-down), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v129**), `native/tests/soak.sh` | **It reproduces now, 3 runs out of 3, and the previous "ASLR-flaky" reading was an artefact of a broken repro line.** `soak.sh` set `MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS` on the child but never printed it, so the command it handed back ran a different match: 0 failures in 5 runs without items, 3 in 3 with them. Fixed in the same commit as this row. **Repro:** `MELEE_NO_CARD=1 MELEE_RNG_SEED=0xc689826b MELEE_MATCH_P0=23 MELEE_MATCH_P1=24 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=15 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4 melee_decomp_boot --boot-frames 1300 --boot-timeout 280 --boot-match 20` -- Roy vs Pichu on Brinstar Depths, asserts at **frame 1257**, same frame every time, and `gobj=0x808aa220` / `Fighter=0x808aa280` are stable across runs because MEM1 is a fixed mapping. **The full chain, each step caught on a hardware watchpoint rather than reasoned about:** (1) `FObjUpdateAnim` (`fobj.c:414`) hands `JObjUpdateFunc` type 5 the value **2.367e30**, and `HSD_JObjSetTranslateX` writes it to joint `0x80996040`, one of Roy's ECB bones' ancestors; (2) every one of the six `ecb_source.x10C_joint[]` world matrices inherits the translation `(7.12e28, 1.48e29, 2.55e30)`; (3) `mpColl_LoadECB_JObj` takes `dy = vec.y - coll->cur_pos.y` and writes `desired_ecb.bottom.y = 1.48e29`; (4) in `mpColl_80043754`, `s32 steps = x / 6.0F` converts that to int and **overflows to `INT_MIN`** -- so `1.0F / (steps - step)` is `-4.6566e-10`, which is exactly `-1/2^31` and is the `time` the watchpoint reported; (5) `mpCollInterpolateECB`'s `dest->x += time * (src->x - dest->x)` turns `ecb.bottom.y` from `5.16` into **-6.89e19**; (6) `mpColl_80044838_Floor` adds it to `cur_pos.y` through `mpLib_8004DD90_Floor`; (7) `ftDrawCommon_80080E18` -> `lbVector_WorldToScreen` asserts. **Ruled out by measurement, not by argument:** the stage's collision data is clean (all 102 `groundCollVtx` are sane, and the line the fighter is on, id 20, is `x0=12.58 x1=18.78 y0=46.43 y1=45.82`); the P-781 `default:` arm is already guarded and is not the path taken; `parseFloat` is **not** a P-798-style byte-order bug -- it builds the word arithmetically, so it gives the same float on PowerPC and x86; `FighterBone`'s flag bit-fields are not a G-180 case, they are only ever read and written through the same names (the single `flags8` use is a clear to zero). **The mechanism: an `HSD_FObj` is playing an animation buffer that has been reloaded underneath it.** The offending FObj carries `obj_type=5 length=25 frac_value=0 frac_slope=0 ad_head=0x809b6830` and has consumed **27 bytes of a 25-byte track**; decoding those bytes as the FObj believes (FLOAT) hits an invalid opcode at byte 6, while decoding them as S16/S16 parses perfectly and yields `-0.996, -0.978, 0.00134`. The `FigaTrack` that owns `0x809b6830` *now* belongs to a different joint (`0x80996f40`) and says `obj_type=3 length=7 frac_value=79 frac_slope=136`. So the track array the FObj copied from at bind time is gone, and it is reading a later animation's bytes with the earlier one's length and fraction encodings. `HSD_FObjLoadDesc` is never called with this `ad`; these FObjs come from `lbAnim_InitFrames` via `ftAnim_8006F4C8`. **The open question is why a joint keeps a stale AObj:** `ftAnim_8006F4C8` skips a part when `fp->parts[i].flags_b0 || flags_b5`, and a skipped joint keeps the AObj it already had. Next step is to instrument that skip and see whether the port skips a part retail rebinds, or whether the animation buffer is refilled earlier here than on console (the P-752 instant-DVD-read class). **Do not "fix" step (4).** Saturating the `float`->`int` conversion the way PowerPC does turns `INT_MIN` into `INT_MAX`, which trades this crash for a two-billion-iteration hang; it is a real port divergence but it is a symptom here, not the bug. **FIXED.** The root is seven steps above the assert: `conv_ft_data`'s per-costume TObj-index walk runs `for (k = 0; k < 8; k++)` over `ftData_x8_x8.xC` and used each slot as a pointer without checking `c->reloc[p]`. **Eight is the cap, not the count** -- a fighter with fewer costumes ends the table early, and the walk then read ordinary data as an offset and byte-swapped `n_tobjs` u16 wherever it landed. In `PlEm.dat` it landed on the **symbol string** at `0x18` and transposed its first two halfwords, so `ftData_80085CD8` asked the animation archive for `lPEymblem5K_Share_ACTION_WallDamage_figatree` when the archive holds `PlyEmblem5K_Share_ACTION_WallDamage_figatree`. `HSD_ArchiveGetPublicAddress` is a plain `strcmp`, so it returned NULL, `fp->x590` stayed NULL, and the rebind guarded by `if (fp->x590 != 0U)` never ran -- while the ARAM DMA had already overwritten the buffer the old `HSD_FObj`s were still reading. The curve player then decoded the new animation's bytes with the old animation's lengths and fraction encodings and produced **2.37e30** for a translate. **Fix:** stop the costume loop at the first slot that is not a relocation target. **Verified:** the repro failed 3/3 at frame 1257 before and now runs **5/5 clean to 2400 frames**; the **Venom hang** (Samus vs Yoshi, stage 22) was the same bug too -- the exploded ECB spun `mpLib_800515A0_LeftWall` forever, and it goes from SIGALRM on the old binary to **3/3 clean**; the `lbvector.c:397` **x** twin on Onett (Link vs Luigi, stage 9) was the same bug and also goes from panicking on the old binary to **3/3 clean**; ctest **33/33**; descriptor coverage unchanged. Converter **v129** -- rebuild after pulling. Full chain and the three lessons in **G-203**. |
| P-798 | **Bowser's fire breath draws a flat polygon across half the screen** -- every float immediate in every particle command script is byte-reversed | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/sysdolphin/baselib/particle.c.patch`, `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c`, `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c` | **Owner-reported with a screenshot**, plus "the same kind of issue on other characters where the right part of the screen flashes white on some moves" -- which turned out to be the same bug, not a second one. **A screenshot cannot say whether a flat polygon is geometry or shading**, and those have unrelated fixes, so the first step was a probe that answers it in one number: `MELEE_BIG_PRIM` reports primitives whose view-space extent or NDC span is far past the viewport. It filters to **direct-mode positions** -- `pobj.c` is the only `GXSetArray(GX_VA_POS)` caller, so indexed positions are models and direct ones are the effect/particle/2D path -- and skips primitives that stay inside the viewport, which is what keeps the legitimate full-screen backdrop quad and the stage geometry near the camera out of the report. **Reproduction needed a second missing axis:** no automated run has ever pressed B, so no special move, and with it most of the particle system, had ever been drawn outside the owner's own play. `MELEE_SPECIAL_TEST` holds B with the stick neutral (with the walk script's stick still held it is a *side* special and the neutral special never runs). **Measured:** the emitted `GX_QUADS` corners sit at **+/-1.6066465e24** in view space with a sane z. That is `0x67AA1C3E`; those bytes reversed are `0x3E1CAA67` = **0.153**, an ordinary particle size. **Cause:** `psReadFloat` (particle.c:638) assembles each immediate a byte at a time into a `union { f32; u8[4] }`, filling `bytes[0]` first. `bytes[0]` is the **most** significant byte on PowerPC and the **least** significant one on x86, so every float in every particle script -- size, position, velocity, gravity, friction -- arrives byte-reversed. `psDispSub` scales the billboard axes by `pp->size`, so one reversed `0xA0` (set size target) immediate is a sprite a hundred billion times too large, which rasterises as a flat wall of one texel: exactly the owner's screenshot. **Why the converter cannot fix this:** the particle command bank is an opcode stream, so nothing can byte-swap the floats embedded in it without decoding every opcode first. It has to be fixed at the read. **Fix:** fill the union from the other end under `PORT_PC`; no caller, no archive and no converter version change. **Same class as P-788** (the item/fighter command streams read little-endian) -- that is now three command streams with this bug, so **any byte-wise or cast read of archive command data is worth auditing as a class**. **Verified:** the `e24` quads disappear from the probe over a 900-frame Bowser-vs-Link run and what is left is a legitimate sprite 3 units from the camera; GameCube 100.00% matched, 100.00% linked (1130 / 1130); ctest 32/32. |
| P-799 | **Fountain of Dreams' background sparkles draw as a scatter of flat grey squares** -- GX point and line raster sizes are 1/6 pixel units and the port passed the raw byte | fixed | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c` | Owner-spotted in a capture taken while verifying P-798, so it is **not** a regression from it -- the squares are in the pre-P-798 captures too. **My first reading was wrong and the measurement is what said so.** The shape -- uniform, axis-aligned, same size at every depth, one flat colour -- reads as a point sprite sampling a single texel, and `GXEnableTexOffsets` really is a no-op in the HLE (GX uses it to expand a point into a quad and *generate* the texture coordinate across it, which `psdisp.c:1996` asks for with `GX_TO_ONE`). Implementing it changed nothing, and forcing the offset on for every point draw changed nothing either. **A topology census settled it: of ~7.1M point vertices in a 400-frame match, `TEX0` is present on 0** -- every point sprite in the game is untextured, so no texture-coordinate bug can be what is wrong with them, and their *size* is the whole of their appearance. (Quads, by contrast, are textured 1.19M to 5k.) **Cause:** `GXSetPointSize`/`GXSetLineWidth` take a `u8` in **1/6 pixel** units against the 640x480 EFB -- 6 is one pixel, 255 is the 42.5 px maximum the register can express. `psdisp.c` names the unit itself at the only place that sets it: `w = (pp->size > 42.5) ? 255.0f : 6.0f * pp->size`. The port handed the raw byte to `gl_PointSize` and `glLineWidth`, so every sprite was six times too wide in GX pixels and wider again on a framebuffer taller than 480 -- about 3x over at 800p, which is a ~10 px square where a 1-2 px twinkle belongs. **Fix:** one helper converting to pixels and then to the framebuffer scale, floored at 1 px so a visible primitive is never rounded away. **Verified visually** on the same frame: the squares become 1-2 px points and the sky reads as sparkles. **`decomp_efb` caught the change and was itself wrong**: its point case asked for `GXSetPointSize(5)` and asserted 5 px coverage, i.e. it encoded the same pixels-not-sixths assumption the backend had, so the case and the bug agreed with each other. It now asks for `30` and still asserts 5 px. ctest 32/32. No `src/` change, so the GameCube build is untouched. **Deliberately not shipped:** the `GXEnableTexOffsets` implementation, because with no textured point sprite anywhere in a match there is nothing that can demonstrate it working, and inert code on the per-draw path is not worth carrying. **Two things left for whoever wants them:** that no-op, and the more interesting question of **why** `pp->kind & DispTexture` is never set on the point path -- `psdisp.c:585` has a `GXTexCoord1x8(1)` point branch that apparently never runs, which points at `psEnableTexture` and the particle texture bank. Neither is what the owner saw. |
| P-800 | **Owner-reported crash: SIGSEGV in Pikachu's Thunder (`itpikachuthunder.c:112`)** | open | — | `native/decomp/boot/boot_triage.{c,h}`, `native/decomp/boot/boot_main.c`, `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c` | **Not fixed.** Stack: `it_802B1FE8` <- `ftPk_SpecialLw_8012765C` <- `ftPk_SpecialAirLwLoop0_Anim` <- `Fighter_8006A360` <- `HSD_GObj_RunProcs`. Verified with `addr2line` rather than trusted (`dladdr` reports the nearest *exported* symbol, G-190): all four offsets resolve exactly, and the fault is `itpikachuthunder.c:112`, `ip->xC4_article_data->x4_specialAttributes`, reached from `thunder_gobj = fp->mv.pk.speciallw.x0` -- which the caller null-checks but cannot check for staleness. **Correction, and the lesson is mine:** my first write-up of this row said the dump showed "Peach and Fox and no Pikachu", concluded the running gobj was mid-teardown and off GX link 5, and proposed a Training-mode character swap. **All of that was wrong.** `Fighter::kind` is a **`FighterKind`**, not a `CharacterKind`, and the two enums order the roster differently: `Ft_Kind_Pikachu` is **12**. Fighter #0 *was* the owner's Pikachu, player 0, airborne at `y = -45.67` with `self_vel.y = -1.9`, i.e. falling off-stage while using the move. The dump was right and the reading of it was not; the owner said so in one word. The dump now prints the kind by name so this cannot recur. **Mechanism, as far as it is established:** `ftPk_SpecialLw_Enter` clears `x0` and sets `x4 = 1` (the union puts `specialhi.x0/x4` and `speciallw.x0/x4` on the same words, checked). The Thunder's destroy callback `it_2725_Logic39_Destroyed` sets `specialhi.x4 = 3` -- still truthy -- and **never clears `x0`**, so between the item dying and the next entry, `x0` is a dangling `Item_GObj*` that `ftPk_SpecialLw_8012765C` dereferences every frame. **Ruled out:** a `bool` width mismatch on `x4` (the decomp's `bool` is `int` in both builds, so `x4 = 3` reads truthy either way -- it is not an endianness bug), and a failed spawn returning a bad pointer (`it_802B1DF8` returns NULL on failure and the caller guards it). **Still open:** why the console tolerates the dangling read and we do not -- the likely answer is that `user_data` or `xC4_article_data` is NULL here rather than stale-but-mapped, which is precisely what the faulting address now printed on the stop line will say. **Not reproduced** over four special directions, 5400 frames, three stages and two seeds. The owner reproduces it trivially, so the next step is one run on the rebuilt binary: `controlled stop: SIGSEGV at <addr>` distinguishes a NULL base with a struct offset added (`0xc4` means `ip` was NULL; `0x4` means `xC4_article_data` was) from a wild pointer, and the new item list says whether that gobj is still a live item. |
| P-792 | **Audit: 47 archives have a `yakumono_param` that no marker claims**, so their stage parameters stay big-endian -- the P-791 class, enumerated | open | — | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, data in `native/AI/learnings/stage_param_audit.txt` | **Found by asking the question P-791 raised: which other stages fall through to the raw fallback?** Method, which is the reusable part: from a `MELEE_ROOT_TRACE=1 MELEE_UNWALKED=1` run, take every archive with a `yakumono_param` public and test its root names against `stage_param_markers`; the ones matching nothing get the Zebes-shaped fallback and are raw. Then dump each one's first ten words -- `MELEE_DUMP` prints **pre-walker**, i.e. big-endian, so a block that reads as sensible values there is real data that is *not* being converted. The dumps are committed alongside. **26 of the 47 are already covered** and need nothing: the `GrT*` Target Test archives hold only `DynamicsDesc*`, which `conv_yakumono_touch_lines` walks (P-787). **`GrZe` is covered too** -- the fallback *is* the Zebes walker. **Substantial and genuinely unconverted, worst first:** `GrGb.dat` (**Great Bay, a VS-legal stage**: `5000/8000` as two `s16`, then 1.5, 0.1, 1.5, 30.0, 0.1, 0.05, 0.05, 1.0, 15.0); `GrFz.dat` (1800, 3000, 120, 420, 1200, 60, 40, -60, 130, -72.5f); `GrFs.dat` (600, 900, 1000, 900, 600, 600, 300, 5.0f, 10.0f, -5.0f); `GrPs1/2/4.dat` (identical to each other and to the handled GrPs/GrPs3 -- they only need the existing marker widened); and the Adventure stages `GrNBr`, `GrNKr`, `GrNPo`, `GrNSr`. **Short ones, one or two words before shared data:** `GrHe` (3, 3), `GrNZr` (1200, 1), `GrPu`, `GrSh`, `GrEF1/2/3`, `GrNBa`, `GrNLa`. **Each needs its struct read from the `gr*.c` consumer before a walker is written** -- P-708's method, and the reason this is filed rather than batch-fixed: several of the handled stages turned out to have `s16` fields where a flat `conv_u32_range` would have silently swapped pairs. **`GrFs`/`GrFz`/`GrNBa` are listed by P-708 as "raw by test design"**, so check `test_decomp_assets` before changing them. |
| P-791 | **No trophies fall in the Classic trophy bonus round** -- `GrNFg.dat`'s `yakumono_param` was never converted | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v126) | **Owner-reported, and with nothing in the console** -- correctly, because nothing errors when items simply never spawn. He had to jump off the stage to end the round. Repro: `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=83` (the StKind that maps to `Gr_Kind_FigureGet`; **StKind is not GrKind** -- `stage_id_map` in stage.c is the table, and 83 -> 38). **Cause:** `grFigureGet_Params` (grfigureget.c:30) is `{ s32 x0; s32 x4; s32 x8; f32 xC; f32 x10; f32 x14 }`, and `GrNFg.dat` matched no marker in `stage_param_markers`, so it fell through to the Zebes-shaped raw fallback and stayed big-endian. Read live it was `{-1946157056, 1342177280, 50331648, 6.05e-41, 6.04e-41, 2.59e-41}`; byte-swapped that is **`{140, 80, 3, -84.0f, 84.0f, 50.0f}`** -- 3 trophies, 140 frames apart with 80 of jitter, dropping between x -84 and 84 from 50 above the camera top. Every field decodes to something sensible, which is the check that matters. **Why the symptom is "none" rather than "wrong":** `x8` is the trophy count and read `0x03000000`, so the spawn loop never terminated; `x0` is the delay before the next drop and read `0x8C000000`, about **-1.9 billion**, and the stage assigns that after the first trophy and thereafter only decrements it -- so **exactly one trophy ever spawned**, at a nonsense x (`xC`/`x10` bound it and both were denormals), and none followed. The headless trace confirms it: one item, kind 160, at frame 82, and nothing after. **Fix:** marker `GrdFigureget` -- 44 of GrNFg.dat's publics carry it and **no other archive on the disc does** -- plus `conv_figureget_param`, a flat six-word walk; the raw dump shows the next structure starts at exactly +0x18. **Verified:** the parameters now read `{140, 80, 3, -84, 84, 50}` at runtime; coverage unchanged at 82.83%; ctest 32/32. **One thing left for the owner to confirm:** in the headless harness the trophy *ids* come back -1 and `it_802F2094` asserts on a NULL jobj, because `Toy_80305058`/`Ground_801C5840` pick from collected-trophy save data and the harness runs `MELEE_NO_CARD=1`. That is very likely a harness artifact rather than a second bug, but it is unproven -- the owner has a card and should say whether trophies now fall. |
| P-790 | `gx_gl: MISMATCHED EFB copy` spams the console every frame on any stage with shadows -- a false positive in my own diagnostic | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c` | **Owner-reported: thousands of identical lines, and no visible rendering fault -- correctly, because there was none.** The line reads `copied 256x256 fmt=32, sampled 256x256 fmt=0`, and 32 is `0x20`. **The copy-texture formats name which channels an EFB copy takes, not how the result is stored:** `GX_CTF_R4` is `0x0 | _GX_TF_CTF` (GXEnum.h:146) and its storage is bit-for-bit a `GX_TF_I4` -- same 4bpp, same 8x8 tiles. `HSD_ShadowInit` (shadow.c:108) does `GXSetTexCopyDst(w, h, 0x20, 0)` into an image the shadow system allocates as `GX_TF_I4`, so **every fighter's shadow tripped the P-738 mismatch test on every frame it was sampled**. The test compared the two format numbers for equality, which is the wrong question. **Fix:** `gx_copy_format_compatible()` -- an explicit table of copy/sample pairs that share a storage layout. **Deliberately not a blanket `& ~0x20`:** `GX_CTF_R8` is `0x8 | 0x20` and `0x8` is `GX_TF_C4`, a paletted format with nothing in common with an 8bpp copy, so the pairs are spelled out and anything unlisted still warns. **Verified:** a 3000-frame match with shadows emits **zero** of these where it previously emitted thousands; ctest 32/32. **Note on the claim:** `gx_gl.c` is listed to codex (graphics retry) on the board, but no such session is live and the tree was clean, so this was taken and recorded rather than left spamming. It touches only the diagnostic predicate, not any rendering path. |
| P-789 | **`*** stack smashing detected ***` a few minutes into a match** -- every `MTXOrtho`/`MTXPerspective`/`MTXFrustum` caller that passes a real `Mtx` overruns it by 16 bytes | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/gm/gm_1832.c.patch`, `patches/src/melee/lb/lbvector.c.patch` | **Owner-reported from live play at frame 18030 -- about five minutes in, where every automated run stops at 900 frames (fifteen seconds).** The game appeared to freeze on a dark blue screen; that is the Classic intro's full-screen quad left on the framebuffer when the canary aborted the process mid-transition. Stack: `__stack_chk_fail` <- `fn_80185408` (gm_1832.c:667) <- `fn_8018564C` <- `gm_Scene_IntroEasy_OnFrame`. **Cause, and it is arithmetic rather than a guess:** `MTXOrtho` and friends are declared to take `Mtx` -- `f32[3][4]`, **48 bytes** -- but each writes `m[3][0..3]` as well (`extern/dolphin/src/dolphin/mtx/mtx44.c:99`), i.e. **64 bytes**. Every caller passing a real `Mtx` therefore writes 16 bytes past the end. On the console that lands in padding the decompilation declares immediately above the matrix -- `u8 _[0x30]` in `fn_80185408`, `u8 _[16]` in `lbVector_WorldToScreen`, the second sized to the overrun exactly -- which is why it is harmless there and why that padding exists at all. GCC lays the frame out differently and the canary catches it. **Two callers in the tree pass a real `Mtx`:** `gm_1832.c:631` (the crash) and **`lbvector.c:371`**, which is `lbVector_WorldToScreen` -- called every frame for every fighter's camera box, and the same function as the P-781 assert. `cobj.c` already uses `Mtx44` correctly everywhere. **Fix:** widen both declarations to `Mtx44` under `PORT_PC`. `Mtx` and `Mtx44` both decay to `f32(*)[4]`, so no call site or index expression changes. **`extern/` is not touched** -- the declaration there is the SDK's own and is read-only by rule. **Verified:** an 18,000-frame VS match (past the frame the owner died at) runs clean, ctest 32/32, GameCube build 100.00% matched 1130/1130. **Worth noting for the harness:** 900 frames is fifteen seconds and this needed five minutes, so a long-match axis is not optional -- it is the axis that found this. `hsd_3A76.c:507` passes `(MtxPtr)((u8*)&projection_m[0][0] - 0x14)`, deliberately offset, and wants checking against the same arithmetic. |
| P-787 | Two more Target Test failures the sweep uncovered | open | — | — | Found by the same `MELEE_SOAK_STAGES="40 41 .. 65"` sweep that found P-786, and left open deliberately so they are not lost. **`tobj.c:373 assertion "tobj->repeat_s && tobj->repeat_t"`** on stages **41** and **45** -- a TObj whose wrap modes are zero, i.e. an unconverted or unreached `HSD_TObjDesc`, which is the same family as the P-786 joint tree. **`ftcoll.c:229 assertion "0"`** on stages **46** and **51** -- the touch-line descriptor class fixed for Mute City in P-785, but these stages' descriptors are reached some other way, so their `yakumono_param` equivalents need the same reference. Repro for each: `MELEE_MATCH_P0=0 MELEE_MATCH_P1=0 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=<41|45|46|51> MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0`.  **Two of the four fixed 2026-09-16, converter v125 (claude, opus-5).** `ftcoll.c:229` on stages **46 and 51** was the P-785 class again: the Target Test stages keep their touch-line descriptors in `yakumono_param` and nothing else in the archive points at them -- `grTMewtwo_UnkStruct` (grtmewtwo.c:17) is **eight** `DynamicsDesc*` and nothing else; Ganon's is three and Jigglypuff's one. Raw, the `damage` word read `0x0A000000` for the int 10. Fixed with `conv_yakumono_touch_lines`, which follows every relocation-backed word of an unrecognised `yakumono_param` whose target passes `looks_like_touch_line_desc`. **Shape-tested rather than keyed per stage**, because there are 27 of these archives and only a marker symbol would tell them apart; the test is strict because `conv_dynamics_desc` writes nine words, so a false positive corrupts rather than skips -- none of the nine may be a relocation field and all nine must be small non-negative read big-endian, which a joint, a float block or a pointer table fails at once. It is idempotent too: once converted the words read large big-endian, so a second visit declines. Stages 46, 51 and 56 now run a clean 900. **Still open: `tobj.c:373` on stages 41 and 45.** Measured at the assert: the `HSD_TObj` is otherwise sane (`scale` (1,1,1), a valid `imagedesc`, `wrap_s = GX_CLAMP`, `wrap_t = GX_MIRROR`) but `repeat_s`/`repeat_t` are both **0** and `flags` reads **0x80031BD0**, which is an address, not a flag word. `repeat_s`/`repeat_t` are `u8` at +0x3C/+0x3D of `HSD_TObjDesc` and `flags` is the `u32` at +0x40, so a byte-order bug cannot explain a zero byte -- the likely readings are that this TObj is built at runtime by the stage rather than loaded from a desc, or that the desc it came from is shifted. Next step: break at `tobj.c:373`, walk back to the `HSD_TObjLoadDesc` that produced it, and dump the desc's +0x3C..+0x44. | |
| P-785 | Audio clips and goes wild on an item pickup; `lbColl_80005BB0` segfaults | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v120/v121) | **Owner-reported ("it gets really loud and clips when I pick up a bomb") and the same bug as P-783, which is how it was found.** Fixing P-783's damage word let Mute City get further and segfault in `lbColl_80005BB0` <- `ftColl_80078384` <- `ftCo_800C0B20`, at `lbAudioAx_80024184(lbColl_803B9880[sfx_kind * 3 + sfx_severity], ...)` -- `lbColl_803B9880` is a **28-byte** table and `sfx_kind` read **117440512**. **Cause:** a stage's `on_touch_line` is declared to return `DynamicsDesc*` (`{ void* data; int count; Vec3 pos }`, 0x14) but every consumer reads it as `lbColl_80008D30_arg1` (lb/forward.h:103) -- **nine `u32`**: `state, damage, kb_angle, unkC, unk10, unk14, element, sfx_severity, sfx_kind`. The two views agree where they overlap (`count` and `damage` are the same word at +0x04) and diverge after +0x10, which is exactly where `conv_dynamics_desc` stopped. So `element`, `sfx_severity` and `sfx_kind` stayed big-endian in every archive that has one of these. Where the index lands in mapped memory the game plays a **garbage sound id** instead of crashing, which is what the owner hears. **Fix:** convert nine words when the object has room for them, and read `data` back *after* the conversion instead of before; `conv_u32` leaves relocation fields alone so covering +0x00 is safe where `data` really is a pointer. Also referenced the two descriptors from `conv_mutecity_param` (`yakumono_param->x8`/`xC`, at `GrMc.dat +0x4d55c`/`+0x4d580`), since nothing else in that archive points at them. **Verified:** Mute City `p13-14/g10` goes from asserting at frame 359 to a full clean 900; matrix **17 -> 14 of 754**; coverage 82.60 -> **82.83%**, floor raised; ctest 32/32. **Owner still to confirm** the bomb-pickup audio, since the headless harness cannot hear it. |
| P-779 | `ftcoll.c:1296` -- "attack power over 500!! **inf**", an item hitbox doing infinite damage | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | — | **Owner-reported from live play 2026-09-16; does not appear in any headless sweep (0 of 754 with items on).** The value is literally `inf`, so this is a float that was never converted rather than a large number. Chain: `scaled_dmg = it_8026B1D4(item->entity, hit) * [p_ftCommonData->x128] * [p_ftCommonData->x714] * fp->dmg.x182c_behavior` (ftcoll.c:1146-1158); `x182c_behavior` is a literal `1.0f` (fighter.c:1042), so the `inf` comes from the **item hitbox damage** or from one of the two `ftCommonData` multipliers. Stack: `ftColl_80077C60` <- `ftColl_8007925C` <- `Fighter_8006CB94`. **Same blind spot as P-778**: items were never spawned in any headless run until `MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS` landed today, so the whole item attribute/hitbox path is barely exercised. **Not caused by the recent converter work as far as can be told** -- nothing in this session touched item attributes -- but that is worth confirming rather than assuming, by checking whether it reproduces against an older converter version. **Next step:** break at ftcoll.c:1296, print `item->kind`, `raw_dmg`, `p_ftCommonData->x128` and `->x714`, and whichever is `inf` names the unconverted struct.  **Owner hit it a second time 2026-09-16, same assertion and same stack**, with `no effect from animlist 64259` interleaved in the log again. **One candidate ruled out:** `it_8026B1D4` (it_26B1.c:65) adds `itemSpeed * it_804D6D28->x80_float[5] + x80_float[6]`, and those come from `ItCo.dat`, the family with the most unconverted words left -- but they read **{0.6, 0.78, 1, 1.35, 2, 3, -0.4, 2500, 0.1, 0.05}** at runtime, all sane, so `ItemCommonData` is converted and the speed term is not the source. That leaves `hit->damage` on the item's own hitbox (per-kind item attributes) or `fp->dmg.x182c_behavior`. Because it still does not reproduce headless, the cheapest next step is a conditional breakpoint the owner can run while playing: `break it_26B1.c:65 if itemHitboxUnk->damage > 500 || itemHitboxUnk->damage != itemHitboxUnk->damage`, then print `itemHitboxUnk->damage`, `((Item*)gobj->user_data)->kind` and `*itemHitboxUnk`. If the damage field is already bad there, the item kind names the unconverted attribute block; if it is sane, the multipliers are.  **SOLVED 2026-09-16 (claude, opus-5). Root cause found, repro is headless, fix is one walker in `hsd_convert.c` -- handed to the P-758 session, which still holds that file. The owner has now hit this three times; it is the highest-value converter item on the board.** **Repro, no play needed:** `MELEE_MATCH_P0=13 MELEE_MATCH_P1=14 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=15 MELEE_MATCH_ITEMS=4`, seed `0x838169d0` -- `[item] BAD VELOCITY: frame=474 kind=34 vel=(-9.93326e+22,2.52905e+23,0)`, **bit-identical to the owner's live capture**, which is itself the tell: a fixed bit pattern, not random heap. **Chain, traced end to end.** The assert is a red herring twice over -- the item hitbox's `damage` is **0.0** and both `ftCommonData` multipliers are **0.5**. `it_8026B1D4` (it_26B1.c:68) adds `sqrt(vel.x^2+vel.y^2+vel.z^2) * it_804D6D28->x80_float[5]` when `flags.x14` is set, and `9.93e22` squared is `9.87e45`, past the `3.4e38` a float holds -- **the square alone is `inf`**, so the item's *velocity* is the whole bug. A watchpoint on the item's `x40_vel` lands in `it_80273748` (it_2725.c:701, `item->x40_vel = *vel`), reached from `Item_8026AD20` <- `ftCo_80095EFC` <- the item-throw subaction; `*vel` already holds the garbage and `xCC_item_attr->x4_throw_speed_mul` is a clean `1.0`, so the attribute block is fine. `vel` is built by **`ftCo_80095D5C` (ftCo_ItemThrow.c:463)** out of `Fighter_804D6550`. **`Fighter_804D6550` is `ftLoadCommonData[1]` in `PlCo.dat`** (fighter.c:189) and **`conv_ft_common_data` never walks index 1** -- it covers 0, 4, 5, 8, 16 and 20 only. Dumped live, the table is raw big-endian: `0x66662640 0xd8e9973e 0x0000803f | 0x66664640 0xdb0fc93f 0x0000803f | 0x33335340 0xdb0fc9bf ...`, which byte-swapped is **2.6 / 0.296706 / 1.0**, then **3.1 / 1.5708 / 1.0**, then **3.3 / -1.5708** -- a throw speed, a throw angle in radians, and a multiplier, all obviously right once swapped. **Fix:** walk `pData[1]` as `ftCo_ItemThrowAttrs { f32 x0; f32 x4; f32 x8 }`, 12 bytes per entry, bounded by `next_pointed_at_after` like the rest of the file. **Do not be fooled by how the consumer is written:** `ftCo_80095D5C` reads `*(float*)(array_element - 0x468)` and `- 0x464` after `array_element = Fighter_804D6550 + motion_id * 12`, which looks exactly like a G-176 cross-symbol overlay -- it is not. `ftCo_MS_LightThrowF` is **94** and `94 * 12 == 0x468`, so those two reads are just `table[motion_id - ftCo_MS_LightThrowF].x0` and `.x4` with the bias folded into the offset. The table is indexed from motion 94 up to at least `ftCo_MS_LightThrowF4` (108). **Worth doing at the same time:** 17 of the 23 `ftLoadCommonData` slots have no walker, and this one produced a crash the owner hit three times, so the others deserve the same audit. **A detector landed with this diagnosis** (`[item] BAD VELOCITY`, `match_boot.c`): every live item's velocity is checked each frame against 1e6 and NaN, reported through `boot_triage_note`, with a matching `soak.sh` key -- so this class can no longer only be found by the owner playing.  **FIXED 2026-09-16, converter v120/v121 (claude, opus-5).** Walked `ftLoadCommonData[1]` as `ftCo_ItemThrowAttrs { f32 speed; f32 angle; f32 mul }`, bounded by `next_pointed_at_after`/`next_public_after` rather than a guessed count. The headless repro (`p13-14/g15`) now runs its full 900 frames with no `[item] BAD VELOCITY`. |
| P-780 | A fighter freezes and stops responding to input while the match keeps running | **fixed** | claude (opus-5 1M), 2026-09-17 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v132**) | **Fixed.** `Fighter_804D654C` is `pData[2]` of `PlCo.dat`'s `ftLoadCommonData` -- the `float[6][5]` item-swing animation-speed table that `ftCo_Attack_800CCF58` reads (`ftswing.c:78`) and hands to `Fighter_ChangeMotionState` as the animation speed. `conv_ft_common_data` walked `pData[0]/[1]/[4]/[5]/[8]/[16]/[20]` but **not `[2]`**, so every entry stayed big-endian and read **`4.6006e-41`** -- the byte-reversed `1.0f`. `HSD_AObjSetRate` therefore got a denormal, the swing animation's `curr_frame` never left 0, `AOBJ_NO_ANIM` was never set, and `ftAnim_IsFramesRemaining` stayed true forever -- which is the **only** exit condition in `ftCo_800CD1BC`. **Owner's repro:** Home-Run Contest, pick up the bat, dash-swing. **Measured** with the new `MELEE_ANIM_STALL` probe: `motion_id=127` (`ftCo_MS_BatSwingDash`), `curr=0.00 end=46.00 rate=0.00` on all 51 parts holding the state open. **Verified:** the table now reads `1.0 / 0.75 / 1.2 / 3.0 ...` at runtime, and the bat's dash column (`[1][4]`) is **1.000** where it was `4.6e-41`; ctest 33/33. **Note the `rate=%.2f` trap** -- the denormal printed as a hard `0.00`, which disguised a byte-order bug as a zero. G-208. **Ruled out along the way:** P-810's animlist decode (zero trace lines through the pickup). |
| P-825 | **Audit: six more `ftLoadCommonData` members were never byte-swapped** -- move staling, Bunny Hood, Metal Box and the crowd all silently did nothing | done | claude (opus-5 1M), 2026-09-17 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (converter **v133**) | Follow-up to P-780 and done as an audit rather than a bug report: `conv_ft_common_data` had grown one member per crash (`pData[1]` P-779, `[8]` P-689, `[5]` P-754, `[2]` P-780), so a probe walked **all 23 indices** and found six still big-endian -- **`[3]`** move staling (9 f32), **`[12]`** fighter scale modifiers (39 f32), **`[13]`** Bunny Hood (15 f32), **`[14]`** Metal Box (9 f32), **`[15]`** gravity/weight multipliers (2 f32), **`[21]`** `gCrowdConfig` (0x44). All are multipliers, so left big-endian a `1.0` reads `4.6006e-41` and whatever it scales collapses to zero: **staling never reduced damage, Bunny Hood granted nothing, the Metal Box changed no weight, and the crowd never reacted.** None of it crashed, which is why none of it was ever reported. **Sizes taken from the decompilation and corroborated by the data:** the structs are laid end to end in `PlCo.dat` and chain exactly -- `0xa7d8 + 0x9C == 0xa874 + 0x3C == 0xa8b0 + 0x24 == 0xa8d4 + 0x08 == 0xa8dc` -- so a wrong size would not fit. **Verified at runtime:** staling `0.09/0.08/0.07/0.06`, Bunny Hood `2.0`, Metal weight `3.0`, crowd thresholds `100/130/160`, gasp cap 9. ctest 33/33 (three consecutive full runs). G-209. |
| P-777 | **Mute City** segfaults in `Ground_801C2FE0` on a NULL `Ground_804D6950` | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `patches/src/melee/gr/grmutecity.c.patch` | Uncovered by the P-769 fix: Mute City now gets past the collision assert and dies later. `ground.c:1694` does `if (Ground_804D6950[map_id] == 0)` and `Ground_804D6950` is a `static u8*` that is NULL. Repro: `MELEE_NO_CARD=1 MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x838169d0 MELEE_MATCH_STAGE=10 ./build/native/melee_decomp_boot --boot-frames 900 --boot-timeout 90 --boot-match 20`. Find who is supposed to allocate it and why that did not run for this stage. | **FIXED -- G-176, eighth instance.** Found with a hardware watchpoint on the symbol, which is the right tool for "who clobbered this" and took one run: `Ground_804D6950` went from a real heap pointer to `0x3dcccccd`, which is **0.1f as a float bit pattern**, written by `grMuteCity_801F106C` (grmutecity.c:1161). `grMc_CarState` is a local typedef `{ s32 idx[30]; grMc_CarEntry cars[30]; }` overlaid on `grMc_8049F440` (the idx array), so `state->cars` reaches `grMc_8049F4B8` only by assuming the linker emitted it at `0x8049F440 + 30*4 = 0x8049F4B8`. Under `-fdata-sections` it does not, so all 31 `state->cars[i]` writes landed in unrelated symbols. **The first fix broke the GameCube match and the checksum caught it:** rewriting the accesses as `cars[i]` is semantically identical but MWCC emits different code. The landed fix **biases the base pointer** under `PORT_PC` so `state->cars` resolves to the real array and the 31 accesses stay byte-identical; `state->idx` is never used in this function. Verified: Mute City passes **26 of 26 fighters** (the two remaining failures there are `ftcoll.c:229` on Ice Climbers, a different bug), GameCube **100.00% matched**, ctest 32/32.
| P-774 | Four more cross-symbol overlays through `grVe_803E5348` in `grvenom.c` | done | agent-a (G-176 brief), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/melee/gr/grvenom.c.patch` | Same class as P-767 (G-176), same base symbol, not yet hit by a repro. `grVenom_80204F20` (~1039), `grVenom_802052E0` (~1111), and two more at ~1156 and ~1431 do `s32* base = (s32*) &grVe_803E5348;` and then index far past its 0x38 bytes -- e.g. `base[base[gp->u.venom.xC8 + 14] + 170]`, where word 14 is `grVe_803E5380[0]` (adjacent, 0x38) and word 170 is 0x2A8 away, several symbols out. Under `-fdata-sections` every one of those reads is wrong. They are on the arwing spawn paths, which the 900-frame soak does not reach; a longer run or a seed that spawns arwings should. **Fixed (P-774, G-176).** Console layout pulled from the linked DOL's symbol table and confirmed byte for byte with `objdump -s -j .data`: `grVe_803E5348` 0x38 -> `grVe_803E5380` +0x38 (0xC) -> `grVe_StageCallbacks` +0x44 -> **`grVe_StageData` +0x184 (0x64, named by the console map but never by the decomp file)** -> `grVe_803E5530` +0x1E8 (0xD4) -> `grVe_803E5644` +0x2FC (0x28) -> `grVe_803E566C` +0x324 -> `grVe_803E5680` +0x338 -> `grVe_803E56A0` +0x358 (0x18). Every far read now names its symbol under `PORT_PC`: word 14 = `grVe_803E5380[slot]`; `base[v + 170]` = `grVe_803E5530[48 + v]` (VA 0x803E55F0; entries 48..52 = 3,3,3,3,6 -- passed to `grVenom_80203EAC`); `base[type + 0x7A]` = `grVe_803E5530[type]` (word 0x7A = 0x1E8 bytes, exactly the table's start); `base[idx0 + 0xD6]` = `grVe_803E56A0[idx0]`; the `grVe_AnimData` overlay resolves to `anim_args` = `grVe_803E5644` (+0x2FC; read is `grVe_803E5644[2*xF4 + fire_kind]`, xF4 1..4, fire_kind -1..1) and `anim_ids` = `grVe_803E56A0` (+0x358); the spawn triple `base + type*12 bytes + 0x218` = `grVe_803E5530[12 + type*3]` (type = `arwing_type` in 1..11, words 12..45). `VenomSpawnData` loses its 0x218 pad under `PORT_PC`; the console path is byte-identical. |
| P-762 | `assertion "pobj->u.jobj"` at `pobj.c:411` — hits roughly **1 in 6** random matches | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` | Found by a 40-seed hand-run of the P-759 soak idea, in 50 seconds, before the harness existed: 7 of 40 seeds failed and all seven are this one assertion. Repro: `MELEE_NO_CARD=1 MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x612f394c ./build/native/melee_decomp_boot --boot-frames 900 --boot-timeout 90 --boot-match 20`; other seeds `0x1e544e69`, `0x75fb59ae`, `0x13740648`, `0x4bd74cd4`, `0x13007f72`, `0x2a7e5ed4`. The process still exits **0** -- the assert reports and continues -- so a harness must grep the log. **Cause, established end to end.** The stage is **Great Bay** (`GrGb.dat`, loaded at 0x811b4c00, and one of the worst-covered files at 50.9%). The `HSD_PObjDesc` at data offset **0x79634** still has its big-endian `u16` pair at +0x0C: it reads `0x190001a0`, where the console means `n_display = 0x0019` (25) and `flags = 0xa001`. Byte-swapped, `flags` reads `0x01a0` and **loses bit 0x2000, `POBJ_ENVELOPE`** -- and since `POBJ_SKIN` is `0 << 12`, a PObj with no type bits *is* a skin. So `HSD_PObjResolveRefs` takes the skin branch, passes the envelope-list pointer (0x8122e240, which dumps as four pointers then nulls -- an envelope array, not a joint) to `HSD_IDGetData` as a joint **ID**, gets NULL, and asserts. `conv_pobj` converts that pair correctly, so **nothing is wrong with the walker -- this PObj is simply never reached**. **Reference chain, measured with `MELEE_FIND_PTR`** (now a permanent probe: it prints every pointer field aimed at a given data offset): pobj `0x79634` <- dobj `0x7964c` (+0x0C) <- joint `0x7be68` (+0x10) <- fields `0x8e4f0` **and** `0x8e634` <- fields `0x8e500` and `0x8e6cc`, and nothing points at those two. Two referrers for one joint tree, with no relocation target above them, says they are **elements of an array whose base is the real root** -- find the array base just below `0x8e4f0` and give it a walker. `MELEE_ROOT_TRACE` now prints `root 0x<offset> <name>` for every public symbol, which is how to find the owning root. **Ruled out: `ALDYakuAll`.** It is the nearest non-image root below the chain and it *is* unhandled, but `ground.c:493` assigns its entries to `itemStateDesc[].xC_script` -- they are item-state **scripts**, byte streams that must stay raw (G-178). Do not add a walker for it. **Fixed:** the referrers at `0x8e4f0`/`0x8e634` are `ItemModelDesc.x0_joint` fields belonging to the stage's **Article**, which `itemdata` (root `0x7665c`) names in its `GroundItemData.unk4` -- and `conv_itemdata` swapped `unk0` (the item kind) and never followed `unk4`. `ground.c:488` files each Article in `it_804A0F60` and `item.c:578` then loads `article->x10_modelDesc->x0_joint`, so nothing else in the archive reaches that tree. Converter **v100** walks it through the existing `conv_article`; the assert is gone on all seven original seeds and on an independent 40-seed sweep that had failed 7/40. Descriptor coverage **73.60% -> 76.88%** (+6,866) from this one walker, floor ratcheted. Regression: `check_stage_item_articles` in `native/tests/test_assets_stages.c` -- structural, not a crash replay, since a `POBJ_SKIN` stores an **ID** in its union and never a pointer, so a union field the relocation table names while the type bits say skin *is* the corruption; reverting the walker makes it print `flags=0x01a0 says SKIN but its union is a relocation target` and drops coverage back to exactly 73.60%. Most stages declare an empty `itemdata`, so the check takes the expected article count per stage (GrGb 1). |
| P-756 | **Phase 0:** descriptor-walk coverage over the whole disc, with a CI ratchet | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.{c,h}`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | The denominator for ADR-0023 class A. The relocation table names every pointer in an archive, so its targets enumerate every object the game can reach; `c->seen`/`c->num` say which a walker visited. **Descriptors** (targets whose own first word is a pointer) are the honest metric -- image data, display lists and vertex buffers are pointed at and correctly never walked, so raw target coverage understates us by ~15 points. Baseline over 861 archives: `targets 774413 walked 457693 (59.10%)`, `descriptors 209261 walked 154019 (73.60%)`, `struct-roots 1964 unhandled 833`. Measured on every `decomp_assets` run; `MELEE_COVERAGE_FLOOR` (73.60) fails the test on regression, and **may only be raised**. `MELEE_COVERAGE_JSON=<path>` writes per-file JSON for the dashboard. |
| P-757 | **Phase 1:** cross-check every walker against DWARF | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/tools/dwarf_{layout.py,types.c}`, `native/tools/check_walker_offsets.py`, `native/tests/dwarf_check.sh`, `native/CMakeLists.txt`, `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` | The 130 walkers hand-transcribe field offsets that the compiler already knows exactly -- that mismatch is what P-746 was. Generate `type -> size, field offsets` from debug info and assert each walker agrees. **Caveat:** the native build's DWARF describes *GCC* bit-field allocation, which differs from MWCC (G-180/G-181/G-188); either apply the MSB-first rule mechanically (the transform `conv_waitanim_flags` already does by hand) or build the GameCube target with `-g` for authoritative layouts -- `build/GALE01/main.elf` currently has zero debug sections. Precedent: P-745 already pulled struct sizes from DWARF by hand via `gdb -ex 'print sizeof(...)'`. Low risk: changes no behaviour, only asserts. **Ordering: this must land before P-758.** The burn-down is volume work suited to a cheap model, but only once a wrong field offset fails a test instead of shipping; without this gate, added walkers raise coverage while silently corrupting data. **Landed as ctest `decomp_layout`.** The caveat was resolved by **using the native build's DWARF and putting bit-fields out of scope** rather than by building the GameCube target with `-g` -- see **ADR-0024**. Short version: the converter's output is read by the *running port*, through GCC-compiled structs, so the native DWARF is the right answer here and not an approximation of it; and MWCC/GCC differ in bit-field *bit* numbering, not in the byte offsets of ordinary fields. Empirically, 232 offsets across 49 walkers -- hand-transcribed from console behaviour over months -- agree with GCC's DWARF exactly, which a layout divergence would have blown up on the first run. Building the matched DOL with `-g` risks the 100.00% match for a gate meant to protect it, so it stays unbuilt. **How it works:** `tools/dwarf_types.c` includes the headers and builds with the port's own layout flags plus `-g3 -fno-eliminate-unused-debug-types` (GCC emits debug info only for types a TU *uses*, so scraping ordinary objects gives a set that changes silently); `tools/dwarf_layout.py` reads `objdump --dwarf=info` into `type -> size, fields`, flattening anonymous unions and indexing tag *and* typedef names; `tools/check_walker_offsets.py` parses each walker and asserts its `in_data` bound equals `sizeof`, every `conv_u32/conv_u16/rd32/rd16(c, off + N)` lands on a real field of matching width, and no **swapping** access lands on a pointer field (relocation targets are already host order -- the P-746 shape). A walker opts in with a `/* DWARF: <Type> */` comment and can say `partial` when it deliberately stops short of `sizeof` (`conv_ps_cmd_list`'s trailing `cmdList[]` byte stream, `conv_cobjdesc`'s union variants) -- so the shortfall is a decision on the record. **Verified by injection:** a wrong offset, a swapped pointer field, a wrong access width, a wrong size macro and a removed annotation each fail the test. **Ratchet at 49/106 walkers** (232 offsets); raise it as annotations land, never lower it. `--list-unannotated` prints the worklist. Not cross-checked: 226 accesses through a computed base (`e = off + i * 0x10`, the array walks), and types no header defines (`ftCo_AttackEntry` lives in a `.c`). |
| P-758 | **Phase 1:** burn down the 55,242 unwalked descriptors | open | — | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` | Worst families first, from the P-756 baseline: `Pl*` 241 files, 56.8%, **35,157 of the gap**; `Gr*` 76 files, 77.0%, 12,384; `It*` 2 files, 86.2%, 3,651; `Vi*` 11 files, **0.9%**, 1,096 (an entire family essentially untouched -- likely one missing root rule, so cheap). Worst single files: `GrTe.dat` 5.1%, `PlGn.dat` 5.5%, `PlKp.dat` 8.9%, `PlZd.dat` 9.7%, `PlGk.dat` 13.8%, `PlYs.dat` 15.0%. Note several fighters sit under 20% while `ItCo` is at 86%, which suggests per-character `ftData` sub-structures are reached for some kinds and not others -- start there rather than file by file. 35 of the 758 archives that contain descriptors are fully walked, and 103 archives contain no descriptors at all. Raise `MELEE_COVERAGE_FLOOR` with each landing. **HEAD ITEM, diagnosed 2026-09-15 -- do this one first, it is 82% of the `Pl*` gap.** The 241 `Pl*` files split sharply: the 34 `PlXx.dat` character-data files are at **20.2%** and hold 22,271 of the 35,157 gap, while the 207 costume files are at 75.9%. Inside `PlXx.dat`, **20,623 of the 22,271 unwalked descriptors are the same 0x14-byte struct**, carrying **82% of the cold (still big-endian) words**. It is `HSD_FObjDesc` -- confirmed field by field against `fobj.h:53` with `MELEE_DUMP`: `{next ptr, length u32, startframe f32, four raw bytes type/frac_value/frac_slope/dummy0, ad ptr}`, chained through `next` and headed by an `HSD_AObjDesc` `{flags, end_frame, fobjdesc, obj_id}`. **`conv_aobjdesc` already exists** and is called from ten places, so no new walker is needed -- what is missing is the *reference chain* that reaches these. Traced in `PlMr.dat` (root `ftDataMario` = 0x8de4): `ftData->x1C` = 0x2534 is the part-animation table; its entries are 0xC bytes at 0x2518/0x2524/0x2530; **`conv_ft_data`'s x1C walk converts only `entry+0x00` and `entry+0x02` (the two u16) and never follows `entry+0x08`**, which points at the per-part animation arrays at 0x8d14/0x8d34/0x8d48 -- arrays of animation pointers interleaved with `u8` part-id runs (`21 22 23 ... 2d`) -- and those head the AObj/FObj chains at 0x9db8..0xf93c. **Bound that array by relocation evidence (`c->reloc[p]`), not by a guessed count** -- the `vis_table` walk a few lines above is the pattern to copy. Walking one word past the end of a fighter table is exactly what caused P-739, and it lands in the `CMD_BE` command scripts. Two of the new matrix crashes (**P-765**, **P-771**) are probably the same root. **Unblocked 2026-09-15: all four gates are in place.** P-757 (ctest `decomp_layout`) and P-759 (ctest `decomp_soak`) landed alongside the P-756 coverage ratchet and the existing suite, so this is now safe volume work for a cheap model -- a wrong answer fails a test rather than shipping. **What that obliges each new walker to do:** give it a `/* DWARF: <Type> */` annotation and raise the `decomp_layout` floor (currently 49) in `native/CMakeLists.txt`; raise `MELEE_COVERAGE_FLOOR` in `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` by the coverage it actually gained; and leave `decomp_soak` green.
