If the type has no DWARF, add its header to `native/tools/dwarf_types.c` -- an annotation naming a type the tool cannot find is a failure, not a pass. **Worked example to copy: P-762**, the first well-specified item, is done -- `conv_itemdata` -> `conv_article` raised coverage 73.60% -> 76.88% on one walker. Its row records the method: `MELEE_FIND_PTR` traces a descriptor back to its referrers, `MELEE_ROOT_TRACE` names the nearest public root, and the fix goes where the *reference chain* is broken rather than where the crash is. **HEAD ITEM LANDED 2026-09-16 (converter v104), task stays open for the rest.** `conv_ft_part_anim` (`/* DWARF: ftData_x1C */`) now walks each `ftData->x1C` entry and follows `x8`, the `HSD_AnimJoint*` array that `ftAnim_ApplyPartAnim` (ftanim.c:1281) indexes with `Fighter_x8B0_t.x11`. Confirmed against `decomp/src/melee/ft/types.h:717`: `{u16 x0; u16 x2; u8* x4; HSD_AnimJoint** x8}`, size 0xC, and DWARF agrees. `x4` is `x2` **bytes** of part indices and stays big-endian; `x2` bounds `x4`, **not** `x8`, so nothing in the archive records `x8`'s length -- which is why it was never followed. **Bounded by evidence, two ways at once:** stop at the first slot that is not a relocation field, and stop at `next_pointed_at_after()`. In `PlMr.dat` the arrays at 0x8d14/0x8d34/0x8d48 hold four pointers each; both bounds independently give four, the first stopping on the `21 22 23 ... 2d` part-index runs and the second on 0x8d24. No guessed count anywhere (P-739). **Coverage 76.87% -> 79.50%** (+5,512 descriptors); the 34 `PlXx.dat` went **20.2% -> 39.9%**, gap 22,271 -> 16,759. `MELEE_COVERAGE_FLOOR` 76.88 -> 79.50, `decomp_layout` floor 49 -> 51 (it was already at 50 before this walker; the ratchet was one behind). ctest 32/32; targeted soak on stages 7, 11 and 31 x all 26 fighters (78 runs) is **byte-identical before and after** -- 7/26, 1/26, 0/26 failing with the same signatures, so no new failure. **Two corrections to the diagnosis above.** (1) The head item was scoped at ~20,600 descriptors; it recovered **5,512**. The remaining 16,759 in `PlXx.dat` is a *different* broken chain reaching the same `HSD_FObjDesc` struct, not more of this one -- re-measure with `MELEE_UNWALKED=1` before assuming which. (2) It predicted **P-771** (Corneria, `HSD_JObjAddAnim`) was probably the same root: it is **not**. Stage 7 fails the same 6 runs with the same assertion before and after this fix. **P-765** was already fixed separately at v102. **Follow-up, converter v105:** the outer `x1C` table needed the same second bound. In `PlMr.dat` the table at 0x2534 holds three entries and the word straight after it, 0x2540, is **`ftData->x20`** -- an `ftData_x20 { HSD_Joint** x0; f32 x8; }` whose `x0` is a relocation field exactly like the slots are -- so a `c->reloc[slot]` bound reads an `HSD_Joint**` array as a fourth part-animation descriptor (the P-739 shape; the pre-existing walk had the same over-run, inert only because `mark()` happened to have claimed 0x8e60 already). `next_pointed_at_after()` ends the table at 0x2540, at three. Per-file coverage is **byte-identical across all 861 archives** before and after the bound fix, which is the proof the over-run reached nothing and that the tighter bound cuts no real entry in any of the 34 files. **Follow-up 2, converter v106 -- a real over-walk, shipped in v105 and now fixed.** The `x8` bound needed a *third* clamp: **`next_public_after`**. A relocation run walks straight into a neighbouring object that nothing *points* at because the game reaches it **by name**, and `ftData` itself is exactly that object. In `PlCa.dat` the third `x8` array is at 0x99f8 and **`ftDataCaptain` is at 0x9a04, three slots later**, so v105 read `ftData->x0`, `->x4`, `->xC`, ... as `HSD_AnimJoint*` -- `ftCo_DatAttrs` and the `x1C` table among them. Four fighters did it: **Falcon, Donkey, and both wireframes** (`PlCa`, `PlDk`, `PlBo`, `PlGl`). **It was corrupting 20 words**, and the diff says what they were: in `PlCa.dat`/`PlDk.dat` v105 byte-swapped the **public-symbol name strings** -- `506c7943` "PlyC" became "CylP", `655f4143` "e_AC" became "CA_e" -- which is the same class the opencode agent hit from `conv_ft_vis_lookup` walking `off=0x18`. Verified by diffing the converted archives byte for byte: exactly 4 of 861 differ, 20 words, all in those four files. Coverage moves **166368 -> 166354, i.e. 14 descriptors down** and still 79.50%: removing a wrong walk *lowers* the metric, which is the honest direction and why the floor stayed at 79.50 rather than being adjusted either way. ctest 32/32; soak stages 7/11/31 x 26 fighters unchanged (7, 1, 0, same signatures). **Also checked and found sound:** the two bounds already there. `next_pointed_at_after` on the outer table agrees with an ftData-own-field bound in all 58 `ftData*` archives; on the inner array it correctly drops 16 tail elements that look like array members but fail every `HSD_AnimJoint` test (e.g. `PlMr.dat` 0x8d54, which an article field at 0x8d84 points at). Under all three bounds, **every one of the 320 walked `x8` targets passes a field-by-field `HSD_AnimJoint` check**; before the `next_public_after` clamp, 22 did not. **`Pl*` IS DONE. The rest of its number is not real, and this is measured, not argued (2026-09-16, v106).** The 0x14 shape left in `PlXx.dat` is **`HSD_AnimJoint`**, not `HSD_FObjDesc`, and `conv_anim_joint` already handles it. It is unreachable **by design**: `ftData_x1C.x8[i]` does not point at its tree's root, it points **into the middle**, at the node for part `x0` -- `root = x8[i] - x0 * sizeof(HSD_AnimJoint)`, which holds for **317 of the 320** arrays on the disc. The nodes before it are earlier parts of the same serialized animation; nothing in the archive points at them, and `ftAnim_GetNextAnimJointInTree` (`ftanim.c:26`) cannot reach them -- it goes child, then next, and pops upward only through its own stack of already-visited nodes, which starts **empty** at `x8[i]`. So the engine never walks above the node it was handed. Each unreachable node reports `cold=3` (`aobjdesc`, `robj_anim`, `flags`) of which **the first two are null**. **Instrument added so nobody chases this again:** `MELEE_UNWALKED` now prints **`chg=`**, the cold words a byte swap would actually change. Disc-wide **54% of `cold` is padding**; `PlXx.dat` is **75%**, `Gr*` is **90%**. Re-measured worklist: `PlXx.dat` 68,513 cold -> **17,325 changeable**, and 16,025 of those are the unreachable `flags`, leaving **~1,300 genuinely open**. `Gr*` 14,616 cold -> **1,528 changeable**. **`Vi*` looked like the answer and is not, and the reason matters.** It first measured at 13,892 changeable words. Almost all of it was one miscount: the report's extent runs to the next thing anything *points* at, and **a public symbol is not pointed at**, so in `Vi1201v2.dat` the object at 0x4fae8 swallowed `visual1201v2Scene` and `ftDemoVi1201V2MotionFileGkoopa` and reported 4096 words -- nearly all of them the motion file's **byte stream, which must never be swapped**. Same mistake as the walker bound in v106, made in the instrument instead. Clamping the extent at the next public symbol drops `Vi*` to **1,262** and the **disc-wide total from 82,596 to 31,629**. **Honest worklist by changeable words:** `Pl*` 20,597 (but ~16,025 are the unreachable `HSD_AnimJoint.flags`, so ~4,500 real); **`It*` 7,430 -- the best target left**, with `ItCo.dat`/`ItCo.usd` at 3,715 each and a single descriptor (**0x4ec8, words=3013 ptr=66, chg=2368**) holding most of it; `Gr*` 1,528 (89% padding); `Vi*` 1,262. Everything else is under 300. **Corrected again at v108:** `ItCo`'s 0x4ec8 -- the worst single descriptor on the disc at 2,368 changeable words -- was a third measurement artifact. It is `itPublicData`'s 118-entry `Article*` table and **every entry was already walked**; `conv_article_array` just never `mark()`ed the base, so `measure_coverage` reported the whole thing unwalked and the extent ran 2,893 words past the array's trailing NULLs. Marking it changes **zero converted bytes across 797 archives** and drops `It*` 7,430 -> **2,694**, disc-wide **31,629 -> 26,893**. **Honest total: 26,893 changeable words, of which ~10,900 is genuinely open** (`Pl*` ~4,500 after removing the 16,025 unreachable, `It*` 2,694, `Gr*` 1,528, `Vi*` 1,262). Worst files: `PlKp` 1,410, `ItCo` 1,347, `PlDr` 1,245, `PlGn` 1,156. Procedure and the table are in `native/AI/workflows/burn_down_descriptors.md` ("What is already settled"). **Landed 2026-09-16, converter v114: `conv_orphan_anim_trees` -- coverage 79.50% -> 82.40%**, +6,067 descriptors, 11,168 words across 106 archives. It is the animation counterpart of `conv_orphan_matanim_trees` and uses the same standard of evidence: a **three-level signature**, `HSD_AnimJoint -> HSD_AObjDesc -> HSD_FObjDesc`, with every pointer word null-or-relocation, plausible big-endian `end_frame`/`startframe`, a sane `length`, a non-null `ad` stream and small `flags`. `conv_anim_joint` marks what it walks, so the scan only ever adds. **Read the +2.9% with its caveat: 54% of it is `PlXx.dat`, the unreachable ancestor prefix, where converting changes nothing the engine reads.** The other 46% is real -- `It*` 1,828 words, `Gr*` 1,399, **`PlKbCp*` (Kirby copy) 1,180**, `Vi*` 709. ctest 32/32; soak over 8 stages x 26 fighters with items on is **5 failures before and after**. **Caution for anyone repeating that sweep:** stage 7 `p2-3` is **load-sensitive** -- it shows at `MELEE_SOAK_JOBS=4` on a busy machine and not at `JOBS=2`, on *both* binaries, and isolated stage-7 runs are deterministic at 3 either side. It is not a regression signal. |
| P-759 | **Phase 0:** seeded soak matrix in CI | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/tests/soak.sh`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | Fighters x stages x N seeds, headless, thousands of frames, with the seed and backtrace captured automatically on failure. Justification is empirical: one arbitrary seed (`MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337`) found **three** crashes in an afternoon (P-752/753/754) that had been latent for the project's whole life, because the RNG was frozen until P-751. **Costed, and it is nearly free:** a 900-frame headless match is **1.25 s on one core** with no GPU, no window and no display (`melee_decomp_boot` runs game logic only). 40 random seeds take 50 seconds; a hand-run of exactly that found **P-762** at a 1-in-6 rate. No VPS is required, though one works -- note the disc image can never go on a public CI runner (AGENTS.md rule 0), so a self-hosted runner or the owner's own machine is the right home. Harness must dedupe by assertion text and **grep the log rather than trust the exit code**: P-762 reports and exits 0. **Landed as `native/tests/soak.sh` + ctest `decomp_soak`.** Classification greps the log for, in order: an `assertion "..." failed` line (the dedupe key, with `file:line`); a `controlled stop: <signal>` (keyed by signal plus the first backtrace frame that is not triage/assert plumbing, and `SIGALRM` is reported as a hang rather than a crash); a `STOP: <reason>` other than the two budget reasons; or no `[boot] summary` line at all. The exit status is only that last backstop. Seeds are a 32-bit xorshift over `(base, index)`, so a run replays exactly from the base it prints: the ctest uses a **fixed** base (a regression test, not a lottery) and `MELEE_SOAK_SEED_BASE=random` is the discovery sweep. `MELEE_SOAK_SEEDS` / `MELEE_SOAK_JOBS` / `MELEE_SOAK_FRAMES` / `MELEE_SOAK_MATCH` / `MELEE_SOAK_TIMEOUT` tune it; `MELEE_SOAK_SEED_LIST` pins explicit seeds, and CI pins the seven P-762 ones so that fix cannot silently regress. Measured: 40 seeds in **10 s wall** on 8 cores (70 s CPU), and the 7/40 P-762 rate reproduced on an independent seed set before the fix, 0/40 after. Failing runs keep their log and print a repro command plus the top 14 frames; passing runs delete theirs. Note it is the one ctest that deliberately overrides the blanket `MELEE_RNG_SEED=tick`, since it sets the seed per child.  **Matrix mode landed 2026-09-15.** `MELEE_MATCH_P0` / `MELEE_MATCH_P1` / `MELEE_MATCH_STAGE` on `melee_decomp_boot` pick the fighters (CKind) and stage (StKind); `MELEE_SOAK_FIGHTERS=all MELEE_SOAK_STAGES=all` sweeps 26x30 = 780 runs in **10 minutes** on eight cores. **The implementation detail that matters:** rewriting `gmVsMelee_StartData` from the VI-frame hook does *not* work -- `onEnterDebugVs` and `gm_Scene_Vs_OnEnter` run in the same game frame, so the hook only ever sees the value before the transition or after the fighters have loaded. It looks like it works (the global does hold the selection from the next frame on) while the match that loaded is the stock one. The fix is to wrap `gm_Mode_DebugVs_States[0].on_enter`, which is ordinary writable data; `match_boot.c` calls the original and then overrides. `[match] loaded p0=.. p1=.. grkind=..` reports what *actually* loaded, because a selection that silently does not take would make a sweep look like broad coverage while re-running one scenario. **Result: 240 of 780 runs failed, 10 distinct bugs (P-764..P-773)** -- where 200 seeds of the default Link-vs-Mario scenario had passed cleanly. Seeds vary the stage but never the fighters, which is why P-725 and P-755 sat open through a green soak. It also forced a real fix: `cache_store` used a fixed `<path>.tmp`, so parallel boots sharing one asset cache could interleave writes into it. |
| P-760 | **Phase 2:** differential trace against the GameCube build | open | — | `native/tools/` (new), `patches/src/**` | The biggest multiplier available, and only we can do it: our GameCube build is `100.00% matched`, i.e. a reference implementation. Run the same input script and the same `MELEE_RNG_SEED` through the DOL under Dolphin and through the port; have both print a small per-frame state hash (fighter positions, action states, seed) via `OSReport`, which Dolphin captures; diff the logs. The first differing frame names the bug. This turns class B from hours of archaeology into a bisect -- P-752 would have been minutes. **P-751 was its prerequisite**: both sides need a pinnable seed. Extend later to frame hashes, which is the only way to catch things that look wrong without crashing. |
| P-761 | **Phase 3:** promote the class-C scans to CI tests | open | — | `native/tests/`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | Three sweeps have each been run once by hand and found real bugs; none of them is a test. (1) Unions pairing a bit-field group with a whole scalar (found P-744). (2) `(Type*) &symbol` casts where the cast type is larger than the symbol (found P-745) -- **this class produces no compiler warning**, which is what makes it dangerous (G-176). (3) `-Werror=unknown-attributes` on any non-GCC build, so the 81 `CMD_BE` / 6 `PORT_BF_BE` sites cannot silently no-op under clang (ADR-0022). |
| P-753 | Material-animation trees in `ItCo.usd` are never walked, so `HSD_TObjAddAnim` loops 768 times over a 3-entry TLUT table | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v98), `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.h`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | Second crash uncovered by P-751, unrelated to P-752 and **pre-existing** -- the frozen RNG stream never reached it. Repro: the `decomp_opening` idle run with `MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337` segfaults in `HSD_TlutLoadDesc(tlutdesc=0x03000300)` <- `HSD_TObjAddAnim` (`tobj.c:83`) <- ... <- `Item_80268BE0` (`item.c:1056`). **Diagnosis:** the live `HSD_TexAnim` at `ItCo.usd`+0x2A85E0 has every *pointer* relocated correctly but the `u16` pair at +0x14 still reads `0x03000300` -- `n_imagetbl = n_tluttbl = 0x0300` where the archive means 3 -- so `conv_texanim` never ran on it and `for (i = 0; i < ta->n_tluttbl; i++) HSD_TlutLoadDesc(ta->tluttbl[i])` reads 765 entries past the end. **Why the walk missed it:** the tree hangs off an `ItemStateDesc` at +0x4924, whose `ItemStateArray` at +0x4914 belongs to an `Article` at +0x49D0 that **nothing in the archive points at** -- it is in none of `itPublicData`'s three article arrays (all 208 entries dumped and checked), and a relocation-table search for its offset finds no referrer, so the game reaches it by arithmetic the converter cannot model. **Fix (converter v98):** rather than guess the missing root, find the shape. `conv_orphan_matanim_trees` tests every *unwalked* relocation target against a three-level signature -- `HSD_MatAnimJoint` -> `HSD_MatAnim` -> `HSD_TexAnim`, each pointer word null-or-relocation, ending in counts that are small read big-endian and have a zero low byte -- and walks the ones that match. Same precedent as P-748/G-183: a blanket "convert every relocation target" is unsafe, a validated scan is not. It finds exactly **3** trees in `ItCo.usd`, which `check_orphan_matanims` now pins (the failure mode is silence: without the scan the file converts "successfully"). Verified by reverting the scan and reproducing the same segfault. ctest 30/30; the GameCube build is untouched (no `src/` change). **The repro now runs further and hits the next pre-existing bug, filed as P-754.** |
| P-754 | `Fighter_804D6540[Ft_Kind_None]`'s count is never converted, so a hidden-part walk runs 16.7 million times off the end of memory | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (v99), `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | The next crash on the P-753 repro (`MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337`, the `decomp_opening` idle run): SIGSEGV in `ftParts_8007506C(ftkind=Ft_Kind_None, part=256)` <- `ftAnim_8006FCE4` <- `ftAnim_8006FE08`. **`Ft_Kind_None` (33) is a legitimate index here, not corruption.** `ftAnim_8006FE08` takes the `fp->kind != fp->x597_bits` branch when a fighter animates *another* fighter's tree -- Kirby with a copy ability -- and `Fighter_804D6540` (PlCo.dat `pData[5]`) has a real slot at 33 for it: entry[32] is NULL, entry[33] is a valid `{ list*, count }`. The converter walked `i < FT_KIND_MAX` (33), stopping exactly one short, so that entry's count stayed big-endian at `0x01000000` and `for (i = 0; i < temp_r3->x4; i++)` ran 16,777,216 times over a one-element list. Fixed by walking `i <= FT_KIND_MAX`; the count now reads 1 and `check_hidden_parts_none_slot` pins it. **Two wrong turns worth recording (G-188):** (1) `fp->x597_bits` reading 33 looked like the MWCC bit-field bug, so `PORT_BF_BE` went on the fp+594 union -- that union aliases one word as bytes *and* as a value from opposite ends, and moving it broke `x594_bits`, the joint mask, so **every fighter's skeleton came apart** (owner caught it). Reverted; `conv_waitanim_flags` already repacks that word correctly and the field was right. (2) A read-side accessor taking the byte at fp+597 was equally wrong: the word arrives host-order from converted wait-anim data, so the accessor fixed one path and broke the other. The bad index was real all along -- the bug was the table it indexed. Converter v99; ctest 30/30; no `src/` change, so the GameCube build is untouched. **The repro now gets further and hits `ftparts.c:518` ("fighter parts model num over!") in `ftKb_SpecialN_800F00B8`, filed as P-755.** |
| P-755 | `FtPartsDesc.model_num` is garbage on the Kirby copy-ability path (`"fighter parts model num over!"`) | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` (`conv_kirby_hat`, v117) | Reached only after P-754 is fixed, on the same repro (`MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337`, the `decomp_opening` idle run). A clean assert rather than a segfault: `ftParts_8007487C` (`ftparts.c:518`) does `vis->model_num = desc->model_num;` and reports when it exceeds 11. Stack: `ftKb_SpecialN_800F00B8` -> ... -> `ftParts_8007487C`. The `FtPartsDesc` lives at `ftData_x8.x0` and `conv_ft_data` converts `model_num` for the normal fighter archives, so the question is how the **Kirby copy** archives (`PlKbCp*.dat`, public `ftDataKirbyCopy*`, which do reach `conv_ft_data` -- no unhandled-root line for them) end up with an unconverted one: check whether the copy path builds its `FtPartsDesc` from a *second* descriptor in the copy file that nothing walks, the way P-753's articles were reached. Third bug in a row on Kirby's copy path, which is simply the part of the game this seed exercises and no other stream has.  **CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED, converter v117 -- and this row asked exactly the right question.** It asks how the Kirby copy archives "which do reach `conv_ft_data`" end up with an unconverted `model_num`. **They reach it because they should not.** `ftKb_SpecialN_800EED50` (ftkirby.c:2773) loads each `PlKbCp*.dat` into `ft_80459B88.hats[kind]`, which is a **`KirbyHatStruct`** (`ft/types.h:2020`) -- `{ HSD_Joint* hat_joint; FtPartsDesc desc; ftDynamics* hat_dynamics[5]; }` -- not an `ftData`. The root dispatch matched them on the `"ftData"` prefix, so `conv_ft_data` read +0x08 as `ftData->x8`, +0x0C/+0x14 as the WaitAnim arrays, +0x1C as the part-animation table, every offset meaning something else. **The `FtPartsDesc` is embedded at +0x04**, not behind a pointer the way `ftData->x8` holds it, which is exactly why the normal fighter path converts its `model_num` and this one never did. In `PlKbCpCl.dat` +0x04 read `0x01000000` -- big-endian 1 -- and `ftParts_8007487C` reports above 11. **`model_num` now reads 1 in 20 of the 24 archives.** **Honesty note: I could not reproduce the original assert to confirm the close.** `MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337` on the idle boot does not fire it at v114 *or* v117, so this is "cause found and the bad value fixed", not "repro verified green". Re-open if it returns. **Four archives are deliberately skipped** -- `PlKbCpDk`, `PlKbCpFc`, `PlKbCpMt`, `PlKbCpPr` put their relocations at +0x04/+0x0C/+0x14 instead of +0x00/+0x08, so they are not `KirbyHatStruct` and read as one their `model_num` is 1,275,068,416. The walker is **self-validating** and skips them rather than guessing; they are now converted by nothing, which is better than by the wrong walker. **Naming those four is the open follow-up, and it is now narrowed to one question.** Pushed as far as static evidence goes 2026-09-16: all four read `{BE 1, ptr}, {BE 2, ptr}, {n, ptr}...` at the public, and **`hat_joint` at +0x00 would have to be a pointer** because `ftKb_LoadHat` does `HSD_JObjLoadJoint(hat->hat_joint)`. **Strong hypothesis: the public sits 4 bytes into the struct** -- read as `KirbyHatStruct` based at `public - 4` all four fit: `hat_joint` = the word at -4, which is **0 in all four**; `model_num` = 1 (<=11); `vis_table` = a relocation; and the `hat_dynamics` slots then land on a mixture of pointers and non-pointers, which is exactly the pattern the 20 good archives show. **Competing explanation: the `hats[]` index is off by one.** `ftKb_SpecialN_800EED50` writes `((HSD_Archive**) &ft_80459B88)[kind]` while consumers read `ft_80459B88.hats[kind]`, and `ft_80459B88_t` is declared `{ Kirby_Unk* x0; KirbyHatStruct* hats[Ft_Kind_Max]; }` -- so `hats[K]` is byte offset `4 + 4K` and receives the file for kind **K+1**. `LOAD_HAT` passing `(kind) + 1` to three helpers hints the same. **Decisive test, one runtime print, not a static one:** log `ft_80459B88.hats[kind]` for `Ft_Kind_Donkey` at the point `ftKb_LoadHat` runs and compare it against `PlKbCpDk.dat`'s public offset (0xc8) plus the archive base. If it is base+0xc4 the first hypothesis is right; if it is a different archive entirely, the second is. **I deliberately did not guess between them** -- applying the wrong one silently corrupts four fighter archives, which is the failure this session has already shipped once (v105). **`hat_dynamics[]` is also deliberately not walked, and I did the work to find out why it cannot be done slot-by-slot yet.** Surveyed all 20 valid archives: the slots hold pointers, nulls **and non-pointer values** in different combinations per fighter (`PlKbCpMr` is `P.xxx`, `PlKbCpPp` is `PPxxx`, `PlKbCpYs` is the only `PPPPP`), which confirms the overload from the data side as well as the source. The per-kind table the decompilation gives is also **not self-consistent with the archives**: `ftKb_SpecialN_800F14B4` loads `hats[Ft_Kind_Pichu]` and dereferences `hat->hat_dynamics[4] + 4`, but `ftDataKirbyCopyPichu`'s slots 3 and 4 are both **NULL** -- which is the same off-by-one question as above and has to be settled first. Note also that the two orphan scans already reach any `HSD_AnimJoint`/`HSD_MatAnimJoint` trees behind these slots, so the residual value is only the `Article`/`ftDynamics`/`FtPartsVisLookup` pointees. That is the G&W lesson again: the slots are overloaded per fighter kind -- `[0]` is an `Article*`, `[1]` is a **`u32` mask and not a pointer at all**, `[2]` an `HSD_Joint*`, `[3]` an `FtPartsVisLookup*` (which is **P-776**), `[4]` both an `ftDynamics` and a raw byte offset. Walking them as `ftDynamics` corrupts four uses out of five. `conv_ft_dynamics` is factored out of `conv_ft_data` and ready for whoever writes the per-kind table. 24 archives change, 6,365 words. ctest 32/32; soak 8 stages x 26 fighters items-on **5 failures before and after**. |
| P-751 | Every boot replays the same RNG stream, so 1P always draws the same opponent on the same stage | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/Runtime/platform.h.patch`, `patches/src/melee/gm/gmmain.c.patch` (new), `native/platform/os.c`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | Owner, after P-750 fixed the shuffle: "now it is always just link and hyrule." P-750 was real and is still fixed -- the shuffle now runs -- but it shuffles with the same random numbers every time, so it lands on the same order. **Melee has exactly one entropy source:** `gmmain.c:156`, `*HSD_RandSeedPtr = OSGetTick()`, sampled once after GX comes up; `HSD_Rand` itself is `seed * 214013 + 2531011` from a fixed seed of 1 and is console-exact, so it is **not** the thing to change (same warning as P-750). On console that tick depends on how long the drive took to spin up. The port's timebase is a virtual counter that starts at zero and advances in fixed steps -- deliberate, so tick *deltas* and the boot log stay reproducible -- so the sample is a constant. Measured: `MELEE_RNG_TRACE=1` showed the seed frozen at one value through the whole frontend and identical across runs. **Fixed** by seeding from the host clock at that one call site (`PortRandomSeed`, `PORT_PC`-gated, `tick ^ CLOCK_REALTIME ^ pid`): the timebase is untouched, `OSGetTick` is still read so its clock side effect survives, and `HSD_Rand` is untouched. The seed is printed at boot so a run that turns up a bug can be replayed with `MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x...`; `MELEE_RNG_SEED=tick` restores the old console-style value and is what every ctest pins (one `ENVIRONMENT_MODIFICATION` for the whole suite, so a new test is deterministic by default). GC build `100.00% matched`; ctest 30/30. G-185. **Other streams find real bugs** -- see P-752, which this change is what uncovered. |
| P-752 | SIGSEGV in `HSD_Synth_80389334`: a dangling SFX node after the same `.ssm` is loaded twice | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/melee/lb/lbaudio_ax.c.patch` (new) | Found by P-751: with the RNG unfrozen the frontend VS run segfaults at `synth.c:660`, `node_idx = voices[0]->index`, with `voices = {NULL, NULL}`. Repro: `MELEE_RNG_SEED=0x13371337 ./build/native/melee --frontend --no-items --input native/tests/frontend_vs.txt --frames 2400`. **The chain of evidence, in order.** Pikachu's Dash subaction asks for SFX id 0 (the subaction bytes decode to exactly that, and `ft_80087D0C` passes 0 through), so `HSD_Synth_80389334` walks `HSD_Synth_804C29E0[0]` for an entry whose `unk4` is 0. One matches -- but it is **freed memory**: the node was registered as id 362 (bank 2, `nr_select.ssm`), and by the time of the crash its words read `id=0 n=0`, so the acquire loop runs zero times and `voices[0]` stays NULL. Its overwritten `x0` also truncates bucket 0's chain, which is why the *real* id-0 entry (`main.ssm`, still loaded) is no longer reachable. **Why a node is dangling:** the bank list showed `entrynum=178` (`nr_select.ssm`) loaded **twice** into bank 2 -- as were `captain.ssm`, `mario.ssm` and `nr_vs.ssm`. Two copies register the same ids; `HSD_Synth_80388DC8` unlinks the *first* node it finds by id, so unloading one copy unlinks the other's nodes and leaves its own linked into a block that is then freed. **Why it loads twice:** `lbAudioAx_80027168_inline_2` does `lbl_80433A64[slot] = HSD_SynthSFXLoad(...)`, and `fn_80026C04`, the completion callback, marks the finished slot by searching `lbl_80433A64` for the entrynum. On console the DVD read takes milliseconds, so the assignment always wins. In the port the read completes in microseconds and the completion is delivered at `HSD_SynthSFXLoad`'s own `OSRestoreInterrupts` -- **inside the call, before its return value is stored** (stack confirmed: `platform_pump_completions` <- `OSRestoreInterrupts` <- `HSD_SynthSFXLoad`). The callback finds nothing to mark, `fn_80026650` hands out the same slot again, and the file is queued a second time. **Fix:** publish the entrynum with `DVDConvertPathToEntrynum` *before* the load at both call sites, `PORT_PC`-gated. Every `.ssm` now loads exactly once and the repro exits 0. **A general fix was tried first and rejected:** holding every completion until the pump after the one that posted it (by virtual tick, then by generation counter) is the more faithful rule, but the engine's synchronous loaders -- `AXDriver_8038DA70`'s `while (flag == 0) callback();` and `HSD_SynthSFXWaitForLoadCompletion` -- spin with **no pump point at all** in the loop, so they depend on inline delivery and the game hung on a blue screen at `mode=40`. See G-186. GC build `100.00% matched`; ctest 30/30. |
| P-750 | 1P Classic always draws the same opponent on the same stage | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/melee/gm/gmclassic.c.patch` | Owner: "every single time I go in single player it's always Yoshi on Yoshi's Island." `gmClassicIntroDataBuffer` (0x80490880, 0x20) and `gm_804908A0` (0x804908A0, 0x70) are adjacent on the console -- the symbol map confirms nothing between -- and `gmClassicRuntimeData` (0x90) is that region typed. `gm_Mode_Classic_OnLoad` shuffles the matchup order through `runtime[order_offset]`, where 0x80 is `gm_804908A0[0x60]`, the same bytes as the `order` argument; it is Fisher-Yates written through the overlay. Separated by `-fdata-sections`, the swap read and wrote 0x60 bytes **past** the intro buffer, so the order stayed `order[i].idx = i` and the writes went wherever the linker put the next symbol. Measured before: `order[0x60..]` = `0 0 0 0 3 4 0 0`; after: `5 3 6 8 0 4 7 1 2`. Fixed with the block treatment, `STATIC_ASSERT`ed so adjacency is a compile-time property. **Note the RNG is not at fault** -- `HSD_Rand` seeds at 1 and advances per call with no entropy, which is console-faithful; do not "fix" it. G-184. Third instance of the class that produces no `-Warray-bounds` warning (G-176, P-745), and it hid behind `gmclassic.c` already having a patch for a *different* overlay in the same file. |
| P-749 | `HSD_MemAlloc` fails on a fixed 32 KB shadow buffer at a 1P stage transition | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | — | Owner backtrace: `HSD_ShadowSetSize` (`0x100 x 0x100` I4 = 32768 bytes, a constant) <- `lbShadow_8000ED54` <- `ftDemo_CreateFighter` <- `gm_Scene_IntroEasy_OnEnter`, after finishing a stage and pressing Start. A **fixed** size failing means the heap is gone, not that the request is silly -- unlike P-742, which was a runaway. Measured and ruled out: the HSD heap is flat inside a match (~10.12 MB free over 3000 frames of the title demo) and flat on the Classic CSS (5.90 MB over 6000), so nothing leaks while a scene runs; the loss must be at a scene transition, which no current harness drives. **`HSD_MemAlloc` now reports the failing size and the free total on the way to the assert** (`patches/src/sysdolphin/baselib/memory.c.patch`), and `MELEE_ALLOC_TRACE=1` reports every allocation >= 256 KB, which is how a runaway is caught before it exhausts anything. Ask the owner to re-run; "32768 bytes FAILED, heap free = N" separates a leak (N small) from fragmentation (N large) in one line. **Owner 2026-09-15: no longer reproduces** -- "it doesn't seem to crash the same way it did before, now it's a segfault, it's in a different part." Keep open but dormant: the report stands, the reporting patch is in, and the next occurrence will name the size and the free total. The new segfault is a different bug and gets its own issue when the owner sends the backtrace.  **FIXED 2026-09-16 (claude, opus-5) — the request was never 32 KB in the port.** The owner hit it again in a **four-player** match: `HSD_MemAlloc: 2097152 bytes FAILED, heap free = 826464`, from `HSD_ShadowSetSize(256,256)` <- `lbShadow_8000ED54` <- `Fighter_Create` <- `Player_80031AD0(slot=3)`. 2,097,152 is 64x the 32,768 the row predicted, and 64 is exactly `8*8` -- the I4 tile area. `gx_tex_tile_shift` in `native/platform/gx_vi.c` is a transcription of the SDK's `__GXGetTexTileShift`, and **`GX_TF_I4` had been dropped from it**, so it fell to `default:`, got shifts of zero, and `nx * ny` counted texels instead of tiles. Every fighter's shadow is a 256x256 I4 buffer, so each one took **2 MB instead of 32 KB**; three fit in the arena and the fourth did not. **Two more errors in the same switch, in the opposite and more dangerous direction:** `GX_TF_C8` and `GX_TF_C14X2` were in the 3/3 group instead of 3/2 and 2/2, so they under-counted by half and by three quarters -- an undersized buffer is a silent heap overflow at the first copy, not a failed allocation. All three fixed against `GXTexture.c:55` (whose first group reads `GX_TF_I4, 0x8, GX_TF_CMPR, ...`, and `0x8` is `GX_TF_C4`). Verified at runtime: I4 256x256 = 32768, I8 = 65536, C8 = 65536, C14X2 = 131072, CMPR = 32768, RGB565 640x406 = 522240 (the figure P-738 recorded independently), RGBA8 64x64 = 16384. Free HSD heap in a two-player match goes from ~9.6 MB to **13.6 MB**; a four-player match reclaims about 7.9 MB. ctest 32/32. **This is very likely also P-725 and part of the `memory.c:55` family** -- any allocation failure that needed only a few MB of headroom was competing with up to 8 MB of shadow buffers that should have been 128 KB. |
| P-747 | Shield bubble never drawn; fighter appears to vanish when shielding | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 (from opencode's handover) | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | **The fighter vanishing is correct** -- retail hides the body while guarding (`ftAction_80071FA0` -> `fp->x221E_b5`, skipped at `ftdrawcommon.c:320/348`) and the bubble is the thing you should see. One bug: `ftData->x20`, the guard blend pose, was never walked by `conv_ft_data`, so its joint tree stayed big-endian -- every joint read `scale = 4.6006e-41`, which is `1.0f` byte-reversed. `ftCo_Guard.c` blends that raw `HSD_Joint` in via `lb_8000C868`, the result reaches the live shield joint via `lb_8000C490` (whose destination is also its second source, so the garbage fed back and drifted), and `efLib_Update` takes the bubble's scale from that joint's matrix. Converter v95 walks it; `translate.z` goes from `-1.4e31` to `2.73` and the bubble renders (capture confirmed). **Credit to opencode** -- their handover had the two numbers that mattered (`scale = -nan` on the bubble, `translate.z = -3.09e30` on the attach joint) and the `MELEE_SHIELD_TEST` harness. From there two gdb watchpoints named the whole chain. `ctest decomp_shield` asserts the pose; note it must assert the matrix **row magnitudes**, not finiteness -- `1e31` in a translate collapses the rows to denormals near `1e-40`, which passes a finite check and still scales the bubble to nothing, and the first version of the test passed on the broken data because of it. G-182. |
| P-748 | Grabbing a shielding opponent asserts `jobj->child` in `HSD_JObjResolveRefs` | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | Found by opencode during P-747 and still present after it, so it is its own bug. Repro: `MELEE_SHIELD_TEST=1 ./build/native/melee --match --frames 500` -- around frame 320 the CPU reaches the shielding fighter and the grab path dies. Stack: `fn_800D8EC8` -> `it_802A2BA4` -> `it_802A2568` -> `HSD_JObjLoadJoint` -> `HSD_JObjResolveRefsAll` -> `HSD_JObjResolveRefs`, assertion `jobj->child` at `jobj.c:694`. Preceded by `"no effect from animlist 1796"`. A joint being loaded with a null child where the descriptor promises one points at another unwalked or mis-walked descriptor, the same family as P-747; start by finding which article `it_802A2568` builds and where its joint comes from. **FIXED (converter v97, G-183).** Link's hookshot chain joints live in `itLinkHookshotAttributes.x54/x58/x5C`, reached from `ftData->x48_items`, and nothing converted them. `flags` kept its archive value `0x40100080`; read little-endian that is `0x80001040`, and `JOBJ_INSTANCE` is `1 << 12`. `HSD_JObjResolveRefs` then treats `child` as an **ID** instead of a pointer, looks it up, gets nothing and asserts -- a byte-swapped flags word sent the loader down a different branch entirely, which is why the crash is nowhere near the data. Fixed two ways because the articles arrive by two paths: a per-kind table (`article_joint_fields`) for hookshot/yoyo/climbers-string/foods where the kind is known, and for the `x48_items` run where it is `-1`, a scan of relocation-target words whose pointee passes `looks_like_unconverted_joint` -- the discriminator is the scale triple read big-endian, `(1,1,1)` for every joint in these blocks. A blanket "convert every pointer" is not safe: `conv_joint` does not validate its argument. `decomp_shield` now runs 700 frames so the CPU's grab is inside it, with `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` on the assertion; removing the scan reproduces the crash. Credit to opencode for finding it. |
| P-746 | Convert `yakumono_param` for the three P-708 priority stages: Brinstar Depths, Mute City, Big Blue | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | Same class as P-707 (Peach's Castle drone) and P-738 (the Stadium monitor), and P-708 named these three as the priority because they drive the looping ambient through `Ground_801C5440`. Converter v94. Each layout was taken from the consumer's own struct and then **verified field by field against the raw disc bytes** before being written: `grKraid_YakumonoParam` 13 words (150/240/180, 0.2/0.3, 120/300, kraid_pos_x -60/-30/0/30/60/0); `grMc_YakumonoParam` 0x50, four leading pointers the relocation pass owns and `conv_u32` skips, the decomp's `pad10` turning out to be unnamed floats (-12, 2, 15); `grBb_YakumonoParam` 0x144 ending at `x140_scale`, which is exactly where the next pointed-at object starts. All three sizes agree with `next_pointed_at_after`, which is the independent check that the struct and the archive describe the same object. Markers are publics unique across every `Gr*.dat` that has a `yakumono_param` (`GrdKraidAntenna1`, `GrdFzeroAdver1`, `GrdBigBlueArch2`). `check_kraid_param` pins the actual values so a layout that merely looks converted still fails; dropping the descriptor produces 7 failures. ctest 29/29. **Still raw after this (P-708 remainder):** Icemt, RCruise, Garden, Shrine, Pura, Inishie2, Battle, OldPupupu, OldYoshi, OldKongo. GrNBa/GrFs/GrFz stay raw by test design. |
| P-745 | `gm_181A.c` writes twenty bytes past `lbl_80472ED8` on every Multi-Man mode | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/melee/gm/gm_181A.c.patch` (new) | Found by a scan for `(Type*) &symbol` casts where the cast type is larger than the symbol's declared type -- the G-176 class, which produces **no** `-Warray-bounds` warning because the cast is to a complete type, so the P-721 inventory built from warnings never listed it. 30 such casts in `src/`; sizes pulled from the release build's DWARF (`gdb -ex 'print sizeof(...)'`) left one real hit: `lbl_80472ED8_t` is `ASSERT_SIZE`d at 0x6BC while `RegClearRecordOverlay` is 0x6D0, and `0x80472ED8 + 0x6BC == 0x80473594` is the very next symbol, `lbl_80473594`. The file's own `@todo` above `gm_80182554` says so. It is not just a read: `gm_80182554` **writes** five fields through the overlay and `gmmultiman.c` calls it for all six Multi-Man modes, so on `-fdata-sections` that is twenty bytes of wild write into whatever the linker put there -- a good shape for a crash that looks random and far away. Fixed with the `toy.c`/`hsd_4D11.c` block treatment: one struct with both at the console's offsets, `STATIC_ASSERT`ed and confirmed in the linked binary (`record` at block+1724 = +0x6BC). Both names are file-local so plain macros suffice. | P-744 | Sweep for more MWCC-vs-GCC bit-field layout bugs of the G-180 class | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `patches/src/Runtime/platform.h.patch` (new), `patches/src/melee/gm/types.h.patch`, `patches/src/melee/gr/types.h.patch`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | Scanned every union in `src/` that pairs a scalar with a bit-field group (24 of them), then filtered to those with a **non-zero** whole-scalar read or write -- the only shape where the allocation order can matter, since `= 0` is order-independent and by-name access is self-consistent. Most hits are member-name collisions (`value`, `flags`, `x3`, `xC4` appear in dozens of unrelated structs); after checking types by hand, **one real bug**: `grCorneria_GroundVars::xC4`. `grcorneria.c:501-503` write `xC4.value = 1` while the rest of the file reads `xC4.flags.b0/b1/b2`; MWCC puts b0 at 0x80 so the console sets an unnamed bit and leaves all three clear, GCC put b0 at 0x01 and set it -- Corneria's Arwing logic diverged. The other two candidates were already fixed (`UnkFlagStruct`, `FtStatusFlags`). **Mechanism changed for the better:** `PORT_BF_BE` in `Runtime/platform.h` wraps `scalar_storage_order("big-endian")`, which gives MWCC's allocation with the fields still in natural order. G-180's hand-reversal only works for a group that fills its storage unit -- reversing Corneria's three bits without five bits of padding puts b0 at 0x04, verified -- so `UnkFlagStruct` is migrated to the macro and the test now covers a partial group too. G-181. |
| P-742 | **Particle generator descriptors (`HSD_PSCmdList`) are never byte-swapped**, so a generator asks for ~4e8 particles and exhausts the HSD heap | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `patches/src/sysdolphin/baselib/psdisp.c.patch` (new), `patches/src/sysdolphin/baselib/hsd_3915.c.patch` (new), `patches/src/melee/gm/gm_1832.c.patch` (new) | **Crash**, reproducible headlessly: `MELEE_ITEM_TEST=1 ./build/native/melee --match --frames 900` panics on `assertion "adr"` at `memory.c:23`, stack `psGenerateParticle0` <- `hsd_80398F0C` <- `hsd_8039DAD4` <- `efLib_particles_proc_main`. **Measured, not inferred:** at the failure `HSD_MemAlloc(152)` fails with `OSCheckHeap=128`, having been 10 MB thirty frames earlier -- `HSD_ObjAllocAddFree` grows the particle pool one 152-byte object at a time, so ~66,000 particles were allocated in well under a second. The generator's own fields say why: `count=4.28448e+08 type=0x700 kind=0x8400001 life=3072 random=-4.28448e+08 size=6.89649e-41`. `life=3072` is `0x0C00`, which is **12 byte-swapped**; `size` is a denormal; `type=0x700` is `0x0007` swapped. `generator.c:574` is `while (gen->count >= 1.0F) { ... gen->count -= 1.0F; }`, so a garbage `count` is an unbounded emission loop. **Source:** `gen->*` are copied field for field from `ptclref_804D0E5C[bank][idx]` (`generator.c:1075`), which `psInitDataBanks` (`particle.c:143`) points straight into the raw particle bank. **The format needs care, three ways.** (1) `HSD_PSCmdList` (`psstructs.h:58`) is a 0x3C header -- two u16 pairs, a u32 `kind`, twelve f32 -- followed by `cmdList[]`, a **byte stream that must stay raw**, exactly the trap that made the subaction scripts look like a renderer bug (G-178). (2) `psInitDataBankLocate` **relocates the bank in place** (`((s32*) cmdBank)[i + 2] += (s32) cmdBank`), so the offset table has to be host order before that runs. (3) `version = *(u16*) cmdBank` reads the **high** half of word 0 on the console; converting that word as a u32 makes the host read the low half instead and silently take the version-0 path. Also convert `HSD_PSTexGroup` (0x18 header) and `HSD_PSFormGroup`. **Pre-existing, exposed by P-739** -- confirmed by running the same repro against the pre-P-739 converter: no article spawns and no crash, because the GFX subaction commands never ran. Likely the same root cause as the owner's `no effect from animlist 1796` reports and the half-second audio blast when Link's bow is fully drawn. **FIXED (converter v92 + three `PORT_PC` patches, G-179).** Two bugs, one on top of the other. (1) `conv_ps_cmd_bank` converted exactly one field of each `HSD_PSCmdList`, `kind` at +0x08, and left the other 0x34 bytes raw; the full 0x3C header is now walked and the trailing `cmdList[]` byte stream is still left alone. (2) With the descriptors fixed, particles rendered for the first time and hit a clean `SIGSEGV` in `psDispSubMakePolygon` on `GXWGFifo.u8 = tex_base;` -- the `GXVert.h` shim routes the SDK's *inline* vertex helpers but not the 49 hand-inlined raw FIFO stores in `psdisp.c` (35), `gm_1832.c` (12) and `hsd_3915.c` (2), whose own comment said the address "is only touched if such a function actually runs, which the S2/S4 paths do not" -- true only while particles never rendered. A `WGFIFO_F32`/`WGFIFO_U8` macro pair per file routes them under `PORT_PC` and expands to the original expression otherwise, so `main.dol` is still 100.00% matched. Covered by `ctest decomp_projectile`'s `FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`; reverting the header walk reproduces the panic. |
| P-743 | Articles spawn but render nothing and deal no damage (Link's bow and arrow, Samus's charge shot) | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 |  `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `patches/src/melee/gm/types.h.patch` (new), `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | Owner, after P-739: the animation plays, the bow and arrow **sounds** play, and the article GObjs exist -- `MELEE_ITEM_TEST` reports `article kind=76` (`It_Kind_Link_Bow`) and `kind=64` (`It_Kind_Link_Arrow`) -- but nothing is drawn and nobody takes damage. So the spawn path is fine and the fault is downstream of `Item_8026862C`. **One measured lead:** the arrow spawns with `life=0.0` while the bow has `life=1400.0`. `Item_80269528` decrements `xD44_lifeTimer` and destroys the item at `<= 0` when `xDC8_word.flags.x15` is set, so a zero lifetime is either a missing `ItemAttr` field or the article never leaving its held state. `it_802A83E0` (`itlinkarrow.c:179`) reads `item->xC4_article_data->x4_specialAttributes` and calls `it_80275158(gobj, attr->x0)` to pick the state -- check those against the raw `PlLk.dat` bytes the way `test_decomp_assets` checks `ItemAttr` (P-654). Note P-742 may share a cause: both are article/effect data hanging off the fighter archive. **Pre-existing and invisible until P-739**, since no article had ever spawned. **FIXED (converter v93, G-179).** `conv_article` walked `x4_specialAttributes` only when `item_kind == ITEM_KIND_FOODS`, so every projectile read its attributes big-endian. Traced over its lifetime, Link's arrow launched with `vel=(2.67e23, 2.33e22)` and `life=0`; it now launches at `vel=(1.36, 0.12)` with `life=60` counting down, arcs under gravity and connects for 5%. The block is bounded by `next_pointed_at_after()` (the G-178 rule) and converted as dense 4-byte fields; the only two `*Attributes` structs in `it/` that are not uniform 4-byte, `itWhispyAppleAttributes` and `itOctarockAttributes`, are spelled out rather than skipped. `ctest decomp_projectile` now asserts **damage** rather than a spawn, which is what makes it cover this: the arrow spawned long before it could fly. **Owner confirmation pending.** **Second cause, also fixed (G-180):** with the attributes converted the arrow flew and dealt damage but was still **invisible**, and so were Link's bow and Mario's fireball. `UnkFlagStruct` (`gm/types.h`) is a `u8 byte` unioned with eight 1-bit fields; MWCC allocates the first bitfield at the MSB, so retail's `byte = 1` sets **b7**. `item.c:719` does exactly `item_data->xDAA_byte = 1` and `it_8026EECC` tests `xDAA_flag.b7` before drawing, so under GCC's LSB-first layout b7 stayed clear and every item model was skipped while its hitbox, physics and lifetime ran normally. Reversed under `PORT_PC`, the `ItemAttr` treatment (P-654/G-123) applied to the union itself. **This had already been found once and fixed in the wrong place:** `patches/src/melee/ft/types.h.patch` adds `FtStatusFlags`, a private copy of `UnkFlagStruct` with exactly this reversal, for one Fighter field, and its comment even says "hiding the model". The general case was left for every other user. `test_decomp_assets` now asserts the bit order directly (no disc image needed) and fails on the reverted declaration. **Finding it needed a negative result:** the model tree, materials, display lists, `JOBJ_ROOT_*` flags, the render callback and GX link 6's presence in the camera mask all checked out. Scaling the model 30x from outside and diffing the frame gave **zero differing pixels**, which says "never drawn" rather than "drawn wrong" -- that moved the search from the renderer to the caller, and one print at the top of `it_8026EECC` showed `b7=0`. |
| P-741 | Pokemon Stadium's SIS text window (`grStadium_801D2BEC`, 250x160) captures a fully black EFB every frame | open | — | — | Seen in the owner's `MELEE_EFB_TRACE` run: `dest=0x814974a0 src=(0,0 250x160) mean=(0,0,0) nonblack=0/128685`, every frame, and **no draw ever samples it** (its `sampling EFB copy` line never appears even though the buffer is invalidated and re-decoded each frame). Both may be correct -- `fn_801D2ED0` erases to black and renders SIS text that is empty outside display states 0/1/9..14/17, and the monitor only binds that descriptor in those states -- so this needs re-checking now that P-738 lets the state machine actually reach them. Panic `file isn't exist S<garbage>.usd = -1` / `entry_num != -1` at `lbfile.c:114`, from `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` <- `tyDisplay_8031C454` <- `Ground_801C0754` <- `Stage_802251E8` <- `gm_Scene_IntroEasy_OnEnter`. `ty/tydisplay.c` holds three 0xAC-byte `TyDspArchNames` tables (803B8988 joints, 803B8A34 matanims, 803B8AE0 archive filenames); both `tyDisplay_8031C454` and `tyDisplay_8031C5E4` cast `&_tyDisplay_803B8988` to `TyDspNameTables*` and read `->matanim_names`/`->arch_names` at +0xAC/+0x158. **Measured in the linked binary:** +0x158 from that symbol is `0x380980`, a function pointer -- so `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` was handed machine code as a filename. Sixth instance of the P-721 class (after G-154, P-646, G-166/P-718, P-719/P-720, P-721/`toy.c`) and the first that is fatal rather than cosmetic. **Done (this commit):** `TYDSP_JOBJ_TABLE`/`TYDSP_MATANIM_TABLE`/`TYDSP_ARCH_TABLE` read each table through its own symbol under `PORT_PC`; exact, because the console offsets land on precisely those objects and no access crosses a table boundary. GC build `100.00% matched, 100.00% linked`; `ctest` 28/28. G-176. **Note for the next agent:** this site emits **no** `-Warray-bounds` warning -- the cast is to a complete type, so GCC cannot see the object is smaller than the struct. The P-721 inventory built from warnings will therefore never list it; grep for the "emitted back-to-back" doc comments too. **CONFIRMED by the owner (2026-09-15): VS against a CPU starts again.** Note the scripted `frontend_vs.txt` flow does *not* reach `tyDisplay_8031C454` -- it runs clean with and without the fix -- so the only pre-owner evidence was the binary-layout measurement. | P-738 | Pokemon Stadium's monitor is still dense coloured noise after P-736 | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | **P-736 fixed a different capture.** Its note says "the Stadium monitor's corruption ... 250-pixel RGB565 captures", but 250x160 is `grStadium_801D2BEC`'s **text window** (`lb_800121FC(&text->desc, 0xFA, 0xA0, 4, 0x7D2)`). In Training mode `gm_8018841C()` forces display state 7 (`grStadium_801D2528`), which points the monitor TObj at `gp->u.display.xD8` -- `lb_800121FC(&wrapper->desc, 0x280, 0x196, 4, 0x7D3)`, a **640x406 RGB565** live feed captured by `grStadium_801D2FD0` with `lb_800122C8(&desc, 0, 36, 0)`. G-174's rounded-up tile count cannot matter there: 640/4 and (406+3)/4 are exact in both the encoder and `native/gx/texture.c`'s decoder. **Ruled out by reading the code, so do not redo these:** (1) encoder/decoder tile round-trip for 640x406 -- offsets and `GXGetTexBufferSize` (522240 bytes) all agree; (2) EFB->GL rectangle mapping -- `efb_copy_tex` uses the same `gl_width/640`, `gl_height/480` scale the draw path uses (`gx_gl.c:1424`), and the port stretches rather than letterboxes; (3) capture ordering -- `GObj_SetupGXLinkMax`'s third argument is the **priority**, not the GX link, and it puts all three display GObjs on `gx_link_max`, so the copy runs after the whole scene is drawn, not before it; (4) `GX_HLE_MAX_DRAWS` -- the owner's HUD renders, and it is last. **Measured from the owner's screenshot:** the corruption is high-frequency along x with only gradual vertical drift, i.e. not a shear and not a stride error. **Next step is the owner's run, not more reading:** `MELEE_EFB_TRACE=1` (this commit) prints one line per `GXCopyTex` with the source rect, destination size/format and the mean colour of the readback, and `texture_for` now reports whenever a draw samples a copy destination at dimensions that differ from the copy. Remaining live hypotheses, in order: the monitor TObj still points at the archive's original `HSD_ImageDesc` (`grStadium_801D1EF8` only reassigns it on a state change with `xF8_0` set); `image_ptr` came back from `lbDvd_GetPreloadedArchive(0x7D3)` instead of `HSD_MemAlloc`, so the texture is an unrelated file's bytes; the readback itself is empty. The trace distinguishes all three in one run. **Owner's first trace (2026-09-15) narrowed it hard.** Only two captures ever run in a Stadium match: the 250x160 text window every frame (`dest=0x814974a0`, `mean=(0,0,0) nonblack=0/128685` -- **always fully black**) and the 124x80 close-up **exactly once**, at the frame the stage loads (`dest=0x8152a7a0`, `src=(200,160 124x80)`, real content). **The 640x406 live feed is never captured at all.** That is consistent with the code: `grStadium_801D2D78` creates the feed wrapper with `flag = true`, so `grStadium_801D2FD0` only copies once `grStadium_801D2344`'s **case 7** clears it; the close-up wrapper is created with `flag = false`, which is exactly why it copied once and froze; and the text window has no flag at all, which is why it copies every frame. So the live-feed buffer is still whatever `HSD_MemAlloc` returned -- **uninitialised heap decoded as RGB565, which is precisely the owner's noise**. Second oddity in the same log: the 250x160 buffer is **never sampled by any draw** (its `sampling EFB copy` line never appears even though it is invalidated and re-decoded every frame), while the frozen 124x80 *is* sampled every few frames. So `grStadium_801D1EF8` put the monitor TObj on the close-up (`xE4 == 8`) and left it there, while `grStadium_801D2344` case 8 -- which would re-copy every frame -- is not running. **The open question is therefore the display state machine, not the GX layer:** is `grStadium_801D1390` (the display Ground's think proc, table entry 1 of `grPs_StageCallbacks`) being called, and what is `xE4` doing? `MELEE_STADIUM_TRACE=1` (this commit) prints `xE4`/`xEA`/the countdown/the focused player and which of the three descs the TObj points at, every 30 frames, straight to stderr. Paired with `gx_gl: sampling UNWRITTEN runtime buffer` (also new, and always on) that should close it. **Second trace (2026-09-15) produced nothing, and that was the probe's fault, not the game's:** `viewer_main.c` installs the frame hook in frontend mode only for `MELEE_TITLE_TEST`/`MELEE_CPU_TEST`, so `match_boot_init` -- and therefore the whole `MELEE_STADIUM_TRACE` check inside it -- was never reached. Fixed, and the probe now prints `[stadium] trace armed` plus a reason whenever it has nothing to say, so "no output" can never again mean four different things (G-168). The same run also showed the `UNWRITTEN` heuristic was far too broad: `melee_dvd_origin` only knows whole DVD-loaded file buffers, so every texture relocated out of one tripped it -- 30 false positives against one real hit. It now also requires `gx_hle_asset_remaining(image) == (size_t) -1`, which knows the registered asset ranges; the same flow reports zero. **Owner adds: the monitor is already noise at the very start of the match**, which fits the live-feed buffer never having been written rather than a state that decays -- so a 15-20 s capture is enough. |
| P-739 | Special-move projectiles never appear and deal no damage (Link's arrow, Fox's blaster) | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-15 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/decomp/boot/match_boot.c`, `native/CMakeLists.txt` | Owner report: holding B plays Link's bow animation but no bow and no arrow is drawn, and nothing takes damage. **It is not the item switch and not a crash** -- items are enabled by default (S6) and the match keeps running. Common items work (P-643: all 40 common-item models load), and the per-fighter special `Article` arrays were converted (P-656/G-125 for `ftData->x48_items`, P-654/G-123 for `ItemAttr` flag bit order), so the data is host-order. That narrows it to the spawn path rather than the tables: the fighter's special-move code calls into `it/` to create the article GObj. Note `patches/src/melee/it/kinds/itlinkarrow.c.patch` already exists -- start by reading what it does and why. Repro: VS or Training, Link, hold B; also Fox/Falco neutral-B, Samus charge shot, Mario's cape/fireball. Acceptance: the article renders and applies damage, with a `ctest` that asserts the opponent's percent rises from a scripted neutral-B the way `decomp_hit` does for normals. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED (converter v91, G-178).** Not the item switch, not the article tables, not the item state machine -- the **subaction command scripts** were byte-swapped. `conv_ft_data` walked `0x424` bytes from `ftData->x0`; `0x424` is the size `fighter.c:146` gives `fighter_dat_attrs_alloc_data` (the runtime *backup* block), while the struct in the archive is `ftCo_DatAttrs` = **0x184**. The extra 0x2A0 bytes ran through `ftData->x4` -- which is why the per-character attributes were converted at all -- and then into the special-move command scripts that follow it. For `PlLk.dat`, `ftDataLink->x0` is 0x33DC so the walk reached 0x3800 and swapped the scripts at 0x363C/0x36F0. They are `CMD_BE`, so the interpreter read `030000d0` instead of `d0000003`, decoded opcode 0, and stopped on the first word of every special. Measured in-game: SpecialNStart word 3 is `4c000001` = opcode 19 `set_cmd_var idx=0 value=1`, which is precisely what `isDrawn()` in `ftlinkspecialn.c` waits for before calling `it_802A83E0`. **Fix:** clamp x0 to `sizeof(ftCo_DatAttrs)` and convert x4 explicitly, bounded by `next_pointed_at_after()` -- the next offset anything in the archive points at, which is exact and reproduces the decomp's own struct sizes (0x184/0xDC for PlLk, 0x84 for PlMr) across all 32 fighter archives. x4 needed its own walk for a second reason: for Kirby (ext size 0x424) the old walk *truncated* it. `ctest decomp_projectile` (new) drives a B-pressing script and asserts `It_Kind_Link_Arrow` (kind 64) spawns; with the 0x424 bound it reports zero. **Owner-confirmed working as far as it goes (2026-09-15): the scripts run and the bow/arrow sounds play.** It then exposed two pre-existing bugs that nothing had ever been able to reach -- P-742 (the particle bank is unconverted, and the GFX subaction commands now run into it) and P-743 (articles spawn but do not render or damage). Both were verified pre-existing by running the same repro against the pre-P-739 converter. |
| P-740 | `GXLoadTexObj` silently keeps the previous texture when the per-frame table is full | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.{c,h}` | `gx_hle.c:2189` is `if (frame_tcount < GX_HLE_MAX_TEXTURES) { ...; gx.cur.texmap[id] = frame_tcount; ... }` -- when the 512-entry table fills, the function returns without even clearing `texmap[id]`, so every later draw in that frame samples whichever texture was bound in that slot last. That is precisely the failure G-173 was written about: silently aliasing an unsupported index turns a missing resource into moving corruption. `frame_dcount` (`GX_HLE_MAX_DRAWS`, 1024) and `frame_vcount` saturate just as quietly, dropping the rest of the frame. None of the three says anything on stderr. The owner's Yoshi's Story frames report `draws=203 verts=10860`, so no overflow is *proven* today -- this is a latent hazard, filed rather than chased. Fix: clear the slot (or bind nothing) and report once per limit, then raise the caps if a real scene hits them. **No longer latent, and no longer a guess: the owner reported HUD textures "cycling to different unrelated stuff" for a few seconds and then recovering** -- which is this failure exactly, since the slot keeps its previous binding until the frame's load count drops again. **Why it appeared now:** the P-758 conversion work made more texture *animations* actually run, so more textures are loaded per frame than when 512 was chosen. **Done, all three parts of the filed fix:** `GXLoadTexObj` now sets `gx.cur.texmap[id] = -1` on overflow (the established unbound sentinel -- `gx_gl.c:2490` and `:2651` already range-check it), so an overflow reads as a *missing* texture rather than moving corruption, which is the G-173 point; `gx_hle_report_overflow` prints one line per cap the first time it is exhausted, because silence is what made this hard to attribute; and `GX_HLE_MAX_TEXTURES` is raised 512 -> 2048 (~128 KB of BSS at ~64 bytes an entry). `GX_HLE_MAX_DRAWS` is deliberately **not** raised -- nothing has been observed hitting it, and it now reports if it is. |
| P-733 | **`GXInitTlutObj` ignored its object**, so every sprite drew with whichever palette was initialised last | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c` | `GXInitTlutObj` wrote a single `gx.pending_tlut` and `GXLoadTlut` copied that, discarding its `tlut_obj` argument — fine only while init and load are adjacent. `tobj.c:1205` and `psdisp.c:2122` do call them back-to-back, which is why this survived. **`sobjlib.c` does not:** it builds a sprite's TLUT object at creation (`:176`) and loads it at draw (`:322`), and every sprite on a screen is created before any draws, so they all took the last-created palette. TLUT objects now get the same per-object slot table texture objects already had. **CONFIRMED by the owner: this fixed the yellow legends.** I had wrongly written it off — I grepped `tyfigupon.c`/`tydisplay.c` for `SObj`, found none, and concluded the viewer could not be affected. The sprites come from the shared helper `lb/lb_01F8.c`, not from the screen's own file. **Lesson: grep the call graph, not the one file you are looking at.** Scope is therefore broad, not trophy-specific: every `HSD_SObjLib` user is affected — `lb/lb_01F8.c`, `lb/lbmthp.c`, `gm/gmopening.c`, `gm/gm_1832.c`, `gm/gmregtyfall.c`, `if/soundtest.c` and `ty/toy.c` — so any screen whose sprites shared a wrong palette should be re-checked. |
| P-734 | A CI texture with no TLUT bound returned 0 **silently** from `bind_texture`, so the draw fell back to untextured with nothing on stderr | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c` | This is why the owner's texture errors *disappeared* after P-728 while the artifact stayed: before the fix `t->format` held `0x08000000`, missed the `format == 8 \|\| 9` test and fell to the non-CI decoder, which reported. With the format correct the CI branch is taken and the `palette == NULL` guard returns 0 without a word — a flat quad of the TEV register colour, indistinguishable from intended art. Now reported (rate-limited to 8, since it recurs per frame) with dimensions, format, entry count and `from=`. **This is the third time a silent failure path has cost a whole round trip** (G-168 for panics, P-728 for the archive name). |
| P-735 | Trophy close-up viewer draws its button legends as flat yellow blocks | done (P-733) | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | — | **Two hypotheses tried and both wrong, recorded so the next agent does not repeat them.** (1) Untextured fallback from a failed decode — no: the owner's log after P-728 shows zero decode failures. (2) P-731's missing `flush_direct` in `GXSetTevColor` — a real bug and correctly fixed, but the artifact survived it. (3) P-733's TLUT aliasing — does not apply, the viewer uses no SObj. **Resolved by P-733** (per-object TLUT slots), which I had explicitly ruled out for this screen on a bad grep — the sprites come from `lb/lb_01F8.c`, not from `tyfigupon.c`. Kept open in the record because the two dead ends above are worth not repeating, and because the *method* was the lesson: the fix landed only once I stopped reasoning from symptoms and looked at which GX call pairs were split across time. If it stays silent, follow melee-pc `93fc1c7d1`'s method: filter the draw log down to the artifact class (they keyed on `dst == GX_BL_ONE`; here, draws with `texmap == 255`) and read its actual TEV state, rather than sampling draws and hoping one is the artifact — they lost several rounds to exactly that. |
| P-731 | **`GXSetTevColor`/`GXSetTevColorS10` did not flush pending geometry**, so a run of differently coloured draws collapsed onto whichever colour was set last | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c` | Every other TEV setter in the file flushes — `GXSetTevColorIn`, `AlphaIn`, `ColorOp`, `AlphaOp`, `Op`, `Order`, `SwapMode`, `SetNumTevStages`, `KColorSel`, and `GXSetTevKColor` itself. These two were the only exceptions, which is what made them findable. Symptom the owner reported: the trophy close-up viewer's button legends render as flat yellow blocks. `hsd_3A76.c` sets `TEVREG0` to `bg_color`, draws the panel quad, then draws every glyph, so the panel and glyphs shared a colour. **Not confirmed as the whole story** — the yellow is no longer a texture-decode failure (those are gone after P-728) and the TEV state was never dumped, so this is a correct fix reasoned from the code rather than one proven against the artifact. Owner retest pending; if it persists, dump the artifact class's TEV state the way melee-pc's `93fc1c7d1` did (filter the draw log to the artifact, do not sample). |
| P-732 | Trophy description text renders overlapping after A -> back -> **next trophy** | fixed (owner-confirmed 2026-09-17) | — | — | **Owner reports this no longer reproduces.** Closed on his observation, and **the fixing change is not identified** -- a lot landed between the report and now (P-727/P-728/P-733/P-735 on this screen alone, plus the P-758 converter burn-down). Nobody should cite a specific commit for it. If it returns, the handoff `handoffs/2026-09-14-P-732-trophy-text-doubling.md` still holds the original analysis. **What this does settle: P-730 was suspected of sharing a cause with this, and that link is now disproven** -- this one went away on its own while P-730 is unchanged, so they are separate faults. | Owner screenshot: the first six lines are clean, the rest doubled; the name/series box (`Super Mario Bros.` / `10/85`) is doubled too. The two copies are the **same string at different letter spacing**, which points at the SIS parser's `restart:` path (`hsd_3A76.c:604`) — `case 1`/`case 2` reset `kerning`, `fitting`, `alignment` and the colour to defaults and re-enter the layout loop, so a second pass would lay out identically until the first spacing opcode. **Checked and ruled out:** the trophy text archive. `SIS_ToyData` is an unhandled converter root, but harmlessly so — all 317 of `SdToy.dat`'s relocations are the message-pointer table (max offset 1264, table ends 1268, **zero in-stream**), so the message streams hold no embedded pointers and need nothing past relocation. The two `SIS_S32`-read-as-pointer sites (`hsd_3A76.c:324`, `:734`) therefore never see a relocated value in this archive. Also verified `HSD_SisLib_FontAtlas` is real extracted data (146944 bytes, 54598 non-zero), so glyphs are not the issue, and `sizeof(HSD_Text)` is **160 on the host, matching `li r3,160` in retail `803A5ACC`** (`SisBlock` is 12) — both now pinned with `STATIC_ASSERT`, and the assertions were verified to fire (G-163). **Cause found from the owner's decisive clue** — clean until you enter the close-up and come back, then deterministic per trophy. Leaving it runs `Toy_80310660(1)` -> `HSD_SisLib_803A5E70()`, which resets the SIS heap wholesale but leaves `HSD_SisLib_804D7978`, the head of the text list, pointing into it. `HSD_SisLib_803A5ACC` walks that list from the head to find the tail before every allocation (`sislib.c:252`). The console survives only because the first post-reset allocation lands back on the old head's address, so the stale node is overwritten by the very text the walk appends to — the list repairs itself by coincidence, and only while the allocation sequence is identical. Texts and their 0x10 string buffers interleave, so any divergence leaves the head aliasing a string buffer and the walk following freed memory. Clearing the head under `PORT_PC` is equivalent on the console path (`803A5ACC` assigns it from the first text when NULL) and removes the coincidence. `main.dol: OK` (100.00% matched), ctest 28/28. **The list-head fix is real but did NOT fix this artifact** — owner confirmed still broken. **Read the handoff before touching this.** Four hypotheses are disproved with evidence, two published conclusions had to be retracted (G-172), and the repro is narrower than I first thought: it needs a *trophy change* after the close-up, not just the close-up. Measured and trustworthy: layout emits exactly one copy's worth of glyphs, one quad per glyph, one render per frame, one render pass per retrace, identical layout state across trophies. The screenshot is **consecutive lines overlapping**, not two horizontal copies — start at `case 7`'s `y_offset`/`current_height` with the `[line]` trace, which was written and built but never run. Note `tyfigupon.c` is the *Lottery* scene (`GS_TOY_LOTTERY` in `gmscdata.c`), not the close-up; the Gallery and its close-up are both `toy.c`/`GS_TOY_GALLERY`. I wasted a pass reading the wrong file. |
| P-730 | Trophy List shows ~5 empty row boxes for a split second, then they vanish | open | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | — | Updated from the owner: the rows *are* built (5 boxes matches the counter) but carry no text and are then torn down. Note the same screen's other text — `Normal`, `Game`, `A-Z`, the counter, `TROPHY LIST` — renders correctly, so the font and the text engine work there; it is specifically the row entries. ~~Likely shares a cause with P-732~~ -- **disproven 2026-09-17**: P-732 is fixed and this is unchanged, so they are separate faults. Next step: `_Toy_8030FE48` builds `data->entries[]` and the `prev`/`next` ring, and sets `visible_count`/`first_entry`/`last_entry`; check those against the retail values for a 5-trophy save. **Merged with a duplicate P-730 row (2026-09-17)**, filed the same day from an earlier screenshot that predated P-727/P-728. Its surviving point: **the counter and the list read different sources** -- the counter comes from the save's `trophy_count`, the list walks `toy->trophy_count` or `*gmMainLib_GetTrophyCount()` depending on `gm_IsCurrently1PMode()`/`GM_TOY_LOTTERY` (`_Toy_8030FE48`, toy.c:5716 and :5736) -- which is exactly how the counter can read 5 while the list draws nothing. **Probe landed 2026-09-17: `MELEE_TROPHY_TRACE=1`** prints, from `_Toy_8030FE48`, the totals and both counts, the 13 `Toy_sbss_804D6EDC[]` sort entries, and for each row the `trophy_id` plus the `archive_name`/`symbol_name` that `Toy_8030813C` returned. That distinguishes the three live hypotheses in one run: a `visible_count`/ring fault (rows built, no content), a `Toy_8030813C` id-scan fault (all rows identical or entry 0, the P-645 class recurring), or a sorted-table fault (`Toy_sbss_804D6EDC` empty/garbage, the P-727 class). |
| P-728 | **Trophy screen backgrounds fail every texture decode** — `16385x61440 fmt=100663296` — because `TyMnBg.dat`'s `ToyFigureBg*_sobjdesc` roots were unclaimed by the converter | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | The numbers decode the bug: `16385` is `0x4001` = `0x0140` = **320**, `61440` is `0xF000` = `0x00F0` = **240**, `0x06000000` = **6** (RGBA8). A 320x240 RGBA8 background read big-endian. `HSD_SObjDesc` is just `{ HSD_ImageDesc* image; HSD_Tlut* tlut; }` (sobjlib.h), so the converter follows both pointers. `toy_sobj_loop` in `Toy_80310324` loads the three symbols and hands them to `HSD_SObjLib_803A477C`. **How it was found:** the owner's `avail=921810` matched `TyMnBg.dat`'s size (921842) minus the 32-byte HSD prefix — which is why P-729 now prints the originating disc file. `MELEE_ROOT_TRACE=1` then named the three unhandled roots directly. `HSD_CONVERTER_VERSION` 88 -> 89. Test verified to fail without the fix (reports the exact `16385x61440 fmt=100663296`). G-170 again — **any `*_sobjdesc` in any archive was affected, not just the trophy screen.** |
| P-729 | Texture decode failures named no archive, so identifying the culprit meant guessing | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/platform/dvd.c`, `native/platform/platform.h`, `native/decomp/gx/gx_gl.c` | The GX asset registry and `HSD_ArchiveParse` both see only an address and a length; the DVD layer is the one place a filename and a destination address are both in hand. `DVDReadAbsAsyncPrio` already resolves the disc offset with `find_file()`, so it now records (addr, len, name) in a 64-slot ring and `melee_dvd_origin()` looks it up newest-first. Both texture-decode errors print `from=<file>`. **Hint, not fact:** buffers are reused as archives load and free, so an entry can name a file whose data has since been replaced — good enough to point at an archive, not to be trusted as proof. |
| P-727 | **The trophy tables in `TyDatai` were never byte-swapped** — `**** Not Found Toy Model!(3073)` and the empty Collection | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c`, `native/tests/test_decomp_assets.c` | Seven symbols load from `TyDatai` in one `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` call (`toy.c:6824`) and **none** were claimed by the converter's name dispatch, so they stayed big-endian. Silent, because every field is a small integer and a byte-swapped small integer is another plausible small integer. `tyModelSortTbl` is `ToyNameData[293]`; `_Toy_803064B8` reads `x0` as the trophy id and `Toy_8030813C` looks it up in `tyModelFileTbl` (converted back in P-645). **Trophy 268 is `0x010C`; byte-swapped that is `0x0C01` = 3073** — the exact id in the owner's panic. **Do not guess entry counts.** My first attempt used `TY_TROPHY_COUNT` for all three fixed-size tables; the real extents come from the gaps between public symbols — `tyInitModelTbl` 10584 bytes, **`tyInitModelDTbl` only 216 (6 entries, not 293)**, `tyModelSortTbl` 3528. Sweeping 293 entries from `tyInitModelDTbl` ran through its three neighbours and re-swapped them at u32 granularity, which *transposes each `u16` pair* — and that is invisible to any plausibility check, because both halves stay small integers. Now clamped with `next_public_after()`. The two `-1`-terminated table kinds walk to their terminator and need no count. `HSD_CONVERTER_VERSION` 87 -> 88 (G-067). **Test:** `check_ty_datai_tables` compares all 1758 `u16` against the raw big-endian bytes — the only check that catches both non-conversion *and* wrong granularity; verified to fail (57344 vs 224) with the converter disabled. `ctest` 28/28. G-170. |
| P-726 | `Toy_80310324` passes `sym + 4` as an out-pointer for a `UNK_T sym[1]`, so `lbArchive_LoadSymbols` writes 16 bytes past a one-element stack array | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `patches/src/melee/ty/toy.c.patch` | The stack-resident sibling of G-166. On the console that address was another slot of the same frame and nothing read it back — `Toy_80307470` re-fetches the value with `HSD_ArchiveGetPublicAddress` — so it was free scratch and the decompilation reproduces it faithfully. Here it lands on whichever local GCC put there (`toy`, `tg`, `syms`, a spill slot) and silently corrupts it, which surfaces later as nonsense rather than as a crash at the write. `sym` is used at that one site only, so the fix is to pass the slot that exists. G-169. **Sweep worth doing:** `arr + N` / `&arr[N]` with `N >=` the declared extent on *locals*, the same way the `-Warray-bounds` inventory covers `.data` (P-721). |
| P-723 | **Panics were silent and exited 0**, so every failed assertion in normal play looked like the user closing the window — the owner reported "a lot of bugs/crashes that just close like this" across the whole game | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/platform/os.c`, `native/decomp/boot/boot_triage.{c,h}` | `OSPanic` wrote to `boot_triage_out()`, which the viewer points at `/dev/null` unless `MELEE_VIEWER_TRIAGE` is set, then left through `boot_triage_stop`'s `exit(0)` so the `abort()` below it never ran. `OSReport` went the same place, discarding the game's own diagnosis (`"**** Not Found Toy Model!(%d)"`, `"Cannot find symbol %s."`). **Now:** panics echo to stderr unconditionally, dump the last 64 `OSReport` lines from a ring buffer, print a symbolised backtrace (`-rdynamic` was already on, so `dladdr` names game functions), and `abort()` when no harness is waiting on the `siglongjmp`. `MELEE_LOG_REPORTS=1` echoes every `OSReport` live. The first owner run after this named its own bug in one line — `**** Not Found Toy Model!(3073)` under `Toy_8030813C <- Toy_80310324` — where the same failure had been a blank exit. G-168. **This is the highest-leverage change of the session: it converts the whole "it just closes" class into readable bug reports.** |
| P-724 | Boot banner printed `GC Calendar Year 1930 Month 3 Day 1` — `days_to_calendar` was Hinnant's `civil_from_days` with the era shift missing and a `struct tm`-style `+1900` added | done | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `native/platform/os.c` | Two compensating errors that looked self-consistent: no `days += 719468` (the algorithm counts eras from 0000-03-01, not 1970-01-01) and `td->year = year + 1900` when `civil_from_days` already yields the real year. For ticks=0 that gives `yoe=30`, `doy=0`, `month=3`, `day=1`, `year=30+1900` — exactly the 1930-03-01 printed. Fixed both; `wday` now counts from the unshifted value (1970-01-01 was a Thursday). Checked against `gmtime_r` over 70,000 days (1887..2079): **0 mismatches** including weekday, and the GC epoch resolves to 2000-01-01, a Saturday. **The clock still starts at zero**, so absolute reads are the epoch rather than today; `MELEE_WALL_CLOCK=1` seeds `OSGetTime` from the host RTC (a constant base, so every delta is unchanged). Left off by default because it makes the attract demo differ per run and `decomp_opening`'s idle run depends on replaying the same demo — and turning it on immediately surfaced P-725. |
