| P-721 | Audit the remaining cross-symbol overlays — the console addresses several back-to-back symbols from one base, and `-fdata-sections` does not guarantee that here (G-166) | in progress | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-14 | `patches/src/melee/ty/toy.c.patch` | Inventory: `logs/2026-09-14-P721-overlay-candidates.txt` (718 `-Warray-bounds` sites, after P-718/P-719/P-720 removed theirs). Four instances of this class are already fixed: G-154/P-704 (name-width tables, fixed by naming the real arrays), P-646 (card work area, fixed with `.set` symbol aliases in `hsd_4D11.c`), G-166/P-718 (`soundtest.c`, fixed by rebasing onto the real symbol), P-719/P-720 (card declaration + `file_sizes[9]`). **For each site the question is the same:** does the overlay stay inside ONE object — fine on 32-bit, and ADR-0020's assertions now police sizes — or does it span symbols? **`ty/toy.c` DONE (this commit), 141 sites -> 5.** Two overlays there: `ty/toy.c` (141 sites). `_Toy_804A26B8` is a 12-byte `static` struct, but the code casts `&_Toy_804A26B8` to `Toy26B8*` (0x196 bytes) and reads `->x195`. The console layout is `_Toy_804A26B8`(0xC) -> `_Toy_devtext_buf_804A26C4`(0x8C) -> `_Toy_devtext_buf_804A2750`(0xFC) -> `Toy_804A284C[302]`, so `x195` lands one byte into that last array — and `Toy_804A284C` is itself overlaid as `TyModeState*` from `tylist.c`, i.e. the overlays interlock **across TUs**. Fixed with the `hsd_4D11.c` treatment: one `Toy_block_804A26B8` holding all five at the console's own offsets, `#define`s for the three file-local names (so the live `ASSERT_SIZE`s on the devtext buffers still see real arrays) and `.set` symbol aliases for the two `tylist.c` links; offsets pinned with `STATIC_ASSERT` and confirmed in the linked binary (`Toy_804A284C` at block+0x194, `Toy_804A2AA8` at block+0x3F0). The second overlay was `(TyLightFile*) _Toy_str_TyLight_dat` — the filename string with the table 0xCC later — read directly through `_Toy_803FDDE4` instead, which is exact (`values[6]` and `entries[]` are both 12-byte entries) and avoids a negative-offset pointer. ~~**Still open in `toy.c`:** 5 sites at `:2459`/`:2484` (`*(ptr += 0x69)` over a `char[12]`), a third overlay in the same file.~~ **Closed by P-722** — that third overlay was the Trophy Gallery crash the owner reported, and a fourth of the same shape sat next to it in `Toy_80307470`. `toy.c` is now 0. **Lesson: this class is not theoretical.** I had these 5 sites ranked below ten other files because the raw site count was low; they were the ones actually crashing the game. Rank by whether the overlay is *reached on a live code path*, not by how many warnings it emits. **`gm/gmtoulib.c` DONE (this commit), 88 -> 80.** `BracketData` overlays three consecutive console symbols: `entries[64]` is `lbl_80473AB8` (0x3700), `srcs[3]` is `lbl_804771B8` (`TmBoxArrays`, 0xC), and `&srcs[3]` — one past it — is `gm_804771C4`, the **TmData tournament state** whose layout P-714 fixed. Eight writes reached that third symbol through a base that `-fdata-sections` does not anchor; they now name `gm_804771C4` directly, with assertions pinning `offsetof(BracketData, srcs) == sizeof(lbl_80473AB8)`. `srcs[0..2]` is never read through `bracket`, so nothing else there leaves the object. The 80 that remain in that file are **benign**: GCC reports `BracketData[0] is partly outside BracketEntry[64]` because the cast target (0x370C) is larger than the object (0x3700), but every surviving access is `->entries[i]` with `i < 64`, i.e. in bounds. Clearing them would mean retyping the parameters to `BracketEntry*` across the file, which buys nothing. **`gm/gm_1798.c` DONE (this commit), 60 -> 0.** The results screen casts `&lbl_8046E1B0` to `ResultsDisplayLayout*`, whose members run `player_img1`/`player_img2`/`shared_img` (inside that 476-byte object) and then `gobjs` at +0x1DC, `jobjs` at +0x1EC and `state` at +0x1FC — which are the separate symbols `lbl_8046E38C`, `lbl_8046E39C` and `lbl_8046E3AC`. **40 of the 46 accesses through that pointer reach the last three**, `disp->state` alone 36 times. Fixed with the block+`.set` treatment, and the six `disp` initialisers now point at the block rather than its first member, which also gives GCC the right object size (the P-719 lesson: aliasing fixes the memory, but the *declaration* is what the optimiser reasons about). `sizeof(block) == sizeof(ResultsDisplayLayout)` is asserted, and the linked binary confirms +0x000/0x1DC/0x1EC/0x1FC. **`mn/mndiagram.c` DONE (this commit), 49 -> 1.** Two overlays, both documented in the file itself. (a) `mnDiagram_Assets` (0x118) cast from `&mnDiagram_FighterDisplayOrder` (0x1C): `sorted_names` at 0x1C is `mnDiagram_NameDisplayOrder` and the eight archive-asset pointer arrays from 0x94 are further symbols — 29 of 30 accesses land past the base. Backed by one block with both named arrays aliased in. (b) `mnDiagram_AnimTable` over the `.data` run from `mnDiagram_PopupTextOffsets`: only four members are reached through the base, and each is its own symbol or literal (`mnDiagram_CursorAnim`, `"mndiagram.c"`, `"user_data"`, the assert string), so they are named directly and the overlay goes unused. The declarations are widened to the block extent for the same reason as P-719 — several helpers take the array as `u8* sorted` and read `sorted[idx + 0x1C]`, which is correct once adjacency is guaranteed but not something a `[0x1C]` declaration lets GCC believe. **`mn/mnvibration.c` DONE (this commit), 27 -> 0.** Three overlays off `mnVibration_803EECE0` (an `AnimLoopSettings`, 12 bytes): `cursor_anim` at +0x18 is `mnVibration_803EECF8`; the three assert strings at +0x30/+0x48/+0x58 were read out of the retail image to confirm (`"Can't get user_data.\n"`, `"mnvibration.c"`, `"user_data"`); and all 13 joint-name strings already exist as their own symbols (`mnVibration_803EED44`..`803EEEB8`), so they are named directly. Separately, `assets[1..3]` indexed off `mnVibration_804A0868` are the next three file-local statics — bound to one 4-element array. **Correction to the triage filter:** "partly outside" is NOT automatically benign — `floats->cursor_anim` is reported that way and is a genuine out-of-object read. The test is whether the *accessed member* lies past the object, not the wording. **Filter benign reports out when triaging the rest of the inventory** — the same shape will appear wherever a struct is cast over a smaller array, and only accesses past the object matter. Other clusters to triage: `gmtoulib.c` (88, `BracketData` over `BracketEntry[64]`), `gm_1798.c` (60, `ResultsDisplayData[1]`), `mndiagram.c` (49), `gm_180A.c` (48), `gm_181A.c` (42), `ftkirbyspeciallw.c` (33), `grmutecity.c` (31), `mnvibration.c` (27). |

Also: `HSD_GObjFree` through an uninitialised `prev_link` on the Popo-string and yo-yo allocation-failure paths, an Event Match CLEARED count decided by a stale register, a wild float into `HSD_JObjReqAnimAll` on the Item Switch screen, and two latched-garbage values. | P-708 | Audit the remaining VS-legal stages whose `yakumono_param` public is still converted raw.  P-662 covered GrCn/GrIz/GrKg/GrSt/GrVe/GrOt/GrI1, P-707 fixed GrCs, **P-738 fixed PStadium and P-746 fixed the priority three (Kraid, MuteCity, BigBlue)**; Icemt, RCruise, Garden, Shrine, Pura, Inishie2, Battle, OldPupupu, OldYoshi and OldKongo still have no descriptor | open | claude (opus-5), 2026-09-16 | `native/decomp/assets/hsd_convert.c` | For each stage: find the `gr*.c` consumer's public and struct, add a converter walk, and compare its countdown/intro fields against the raw archive in `test_decomp_assets`. Priority: the stages that call the looping `Ground_801C5440` helper — GrBb, GrMc, GrKr, GrOldKongo, GrZe. GrNBa/GrFs/GrFz stay raw by test design.  **Six of the ten landed 2026-09-16 (converter v110/v111).** **OldPupupu** came first because it had a crash attached (P-770, Dream Land 26/26 -> 0/26). Then the `Ground_801C5440` priority list is **complete**: **OldKongo** (`grOldKongo_YakumonoParam`, 0x70) was its last member. Also **Greens** (0x7C), **RCruise** (0x48), **Inishie2** (0x4C) and **Garden** (0x20). **Method, worth copying:** for each stage, read the struct from its `gr*.c`, check `sizeof` against the symbol's extent in the archive (all six agreed **to the byte**, which is the strongest single check available here), then dump the raw fields and confirm every value is plausible *before* writing the walker -- Green Greens' block timers read 30/150 and wind 800/1800, OldKongo's barrel weights 1/1/10/50/10/1/1/1, and so on. **Three of the six are not flat `conv_u32_range`**: OldPupupu opens with four `s16`, OldKongo has eight `s16` barrel weights at +0x2C..+0x3A **and a pointer at +0x6C**, and Inishie2 is `s16`/`f32`/`s16`. A flat range would have exchanged the `s16` pairs. Markers were each verified to appear in **exactly one archive on the disc**; note Kongo Jungle N64's publics really are spelled **`Ged`**Oldkongo, not `Grd`. **92 words change, in exactly the five expected archives** (plus GrOp's 13 at v110); ctest 32/32; soak over stages 11/12/17/20/30 x 26 fighters unchanged, which is the expected result -- these were latent wrong-parameter bugs, not crashes. **OldYoshi landed too (v112)** -- an *anonymous* struct at groldyoshi.c:54, two `s16`, three `f32`, five `s16`, 0x1C with the trailing pad and exactly the extent. Its raw `x14`/`x16` are **3000/4000**, the same cloud-timer shape Dream Land crashed on, so it had the same failure latent. 7 words, GrOy.dat alone. **Icemt landed too (v113), and it was not latent after all: Icicle Mountain goes 6 of 26 failing to 1 of 26**, and the six were **hangs** -- `HSD_Randi` and `HSD_GObj_RunProcs` spinning on garbage parameters, exactly the failure shape P-708 predicted. The one left is `lbvector.c:384`, i.e. **P-781**, not this. It is deliberately **partial**: the declaration and the archive agree on the layout -- the three `s16*` land on relocation entries at exactly +0xAC/+0xB0/+0xB4, where the declaration predicts them -- but disagree on `x4`, which reads -0.15f where `s16` is declared. **`yakumono_param->x4` is never read anywhere in `gricemt.c`**, so it is skipped rather than guessed. **Nothing past +0xBC is touched**: the declaration says four `f32` at +0xC0..+0xCC but the bytes are `002e0001` repeated for another 0x54, and `grZakoGenerator_801CAE04(&yakumono_param->xBC)` takes **one** desc, not an array -- that region cannot be named from the decompilation, so it is left alone. The three `s16` tables are each terminated by **-1** and are walked to the sentinel, bounded by the extent as well. 61 words, GrIm.dat alone. **Eight of ten done. Still raw: Shrine, Pura.** `grBattle_YakumonoParam` is two `void*` with **nothing to convert**, so Battle is done by inspection; Shrine and Pura have 0x4 extents, one word each. `grBattle_YakumonoParam` is two `void*` and has **nothing to convert**, so Battle is done by inspection. Shrine and Pura have 0x4 extents -- one word each -- so they are near-zero value. That leaves **Icemt as the only substantial one**, and it is the one to be careful with -- `grIceMt_YakumonoParam` carries three `s16*` pointers and a nested `grZakoGenerator_SpawnDesc`, and its declared field offsets do **not** add up to the 0x13C extent, so it needs the nested struct resolved before anyone writes a walker. GrNBa/GrFs/GrFz stay raw by test design. |

## P-201 result

Done in `0e1a974d2` (backend) and `6665bd5f2` (viewer/sandbox). Clips come from
`Pl<Char>AJ.dat` FigaTree archives; `hsd/aobj.c` is a literal port of the
`fobj.c` player and `hsd/anim.c` binds nodes to joints exactly like
`ftAnim_8006F4C8`. Read `learnings/hsd_animation.md` before touching it. The
remaining fidelity items (P-207..P-210) are parked by ADR-0010; the compiled
`fobj`/`jobj` path replaces this in S2/S4.

## S1 backend work list (P-604 triage)

The S1 boot log (`native/AI/logs/2026-09-11-S1-boot-triage.md`, analysis in
`learnings/decomp_boot.md`) reaches a controlled stop while the compiled game
waits for its first sound-bank load. Ordered by what unblocks the boot:

1. **S2 — GX + VI HLE** (DONE 2026-09-12, P-606). The GX command surface is
   real in `native/decomp/gx/gx_hle.c`; the boot log now shows 94 stub calls /
   33 unique (GX no longer triaged). S2 follow-ups P-607/P-608/P-610 are done;
   P-613 (faithful specular), P-614 (TEV KONST), P-612 (bump emboss +
   indirect state capture) and P-615 (Z-texture/EFB copy-read) landed;
   P-617 carries the S4-only indirect/toon shader evaluation, and P-616
   (Captain Falcon eyes) is an open render bug.
2. **S3 — DVD + HSD DevCom/ARQ**. `DVDConvertPathToEntrynum`/open/read and
   synchronous `ARQPostRequest` callbacks so `HSD_DevComRequest` can finish
   asset loads. Seed: `native/platform/disc.c`.
3. **S5 — AX/DSP HLE. DONE 2026-09-12.** The compiled AX stack + software
   mixer drive `HSD_SynthCallback` on the 5 ms/200 Hz frame derived from VI,
   and the `.ssm`/`.sem`/`.hps` converters feed real voices. See Completed
   (P-632..P-636) and `learnings/decomp_audio.md`. Remaining owner check:
   P-637 passed (owner, 2026-09-13).
4. **S6 — CARD/EXI + fonts**. Card command pump (`hsd_803AAA48`) and a font
   source replacing the generated atlases.
5. **S4 — alarms/threads** (`OSCreateAlarm`/`OSSetPeriodicAlarm` are stubs;
   the boot installs a periodic alarm during init).

## S5 result

ADR-0013 option A landed in four commits (`a658deb92`, `41a427348`,
`c455ffd1e`, `bce5a84ba`).  The compiled SDK AX layer drives
`native/audio/ax_mixer.c`; `platform/{ssm,sem,hps}.c` convert the audio
assets; `--audio-dump`/SDL3 sinks and two ctest regressions guard it.  The
DSP-ADPCM frame geometry, the engine's u16-pair aliasing, and the three
formats are documented in `learnings/decomp_audio.md`; the four portability
patches are in `learnings/decomp_port.md` (S5 section).

## Blocked / needs a human

| ID | Question | Requested from |
|---|---|---|
| H-1 | Does the interactive window/controller feel correct on real hardware? | project owner |
| H-6 | **P-710 gameplay check.** (a) Chansey (the Pokéball Pokémon that lays eggs) — spawn it in a VS match and let the eggs sit and hatch/expire.  Before P-710 the egg's motion-state-4 `animated` predicate returned garbage, so eggs could vanish the instant they landed or never disappear.  Expect eggs to behave consistently across several spawns.  (b) Sound Test (Data -> Sound Test) — move through the rows, change values, and back out.  Before P-710 three rows returned garbage, which makes B/back or the value keys either do nothing or fire twice.  Regression looks like a menu row that ignores input or exits two levels at once. | project owner |
| H-7 | **P-713 gameplay check**, in rough order of how likely you are to notice: (a) fight a level-9 CPU and watch its DI — before this, a CPU being hit with almost no knockback sent a garbage stick value every time, so DI out of weak multi-hits may feel different (this is the one that ran constantly). (b) Ice Climbers' side-B string and Ness' yo-yo — these only misbehaved if an item-link allocation failed, so this is a "does anything look wrong" check rather than a repro. | P-117 | Fix invisible player 2 (Visual copied before GL lists existed) | follow-up | `5ba65adde` | 2026-09-10 **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED (converter v90, G-177).** `GrPs.dat`/`GrPs3.dat`'s `yakumono_param` had no converter descriptor -- it is on P-708's list -- so `grPStadium_YakumonoParam` was read big-endian. `grStadium_801D2528` seeds the display countdown from `randi_between_2(x38, x3C)`; 600/1200 read as `0x58020000`/`0xB0040000`, so `gp->u.display.xE0` starts at a garbage negative (the owner's trace: `timer=-1341904928`, different every frame). `grStadium_801D2344` case 7 then takes its `xE0-- < 0` branch on the first frame, which picks a new state and **breaks before clearing the feed wrapper's flag**, so `grStadium_801D2FD0` never runs its `GXCopyTex` and the monitor samples uninitialised `HSD_MemAlloc` heap. State 8 has the same shape, which is exactly why the 124x80 close-up captured once (created with `flag = false`) and froze. **Scope is the whole stage:** the same block carries the 3600/3800-frame transformation interval (`x0`/`x4`), the rise/fall timings (`x10`/`x14`/`x18`) and the 5/2/2/0 transformation weights (`x48`..`x50`). Layout verified against the raw disc bytes: seven s32, `{ u8 r, g, b }` tint plus pad at +0x1C (must NOT be swapped), ten u32, five s16. Marker `GrdPStadiumSteelK` -- it has to be a **public**, since the scan walks `nb_public` only, so `GrdPStadiumRock_TopN_joint` (an external) never matches; `GrHr.dat` carries `GrdPStadium*` names too and keeps its existing `GrdYorster` match. `test_decomp_assets` pins every field of both archives and fails on the unconverted data. **CONFIRMED by the owner (2026-09-15): the jumbotron shows the live feed again.** |
